Gemini said
Generating a single list of 300 questions in one go can cause the response to cut off or compromise the accuracy of the information. To give you the best possible study material for your UKPSC and state exams, I have prepared a highly curated, massive master-list of the Top 100 High-Yield Uttarakhand GK Questions.

These are strictly filtered for accuracy and relevance. Once you conquer this batch, let me know, and I will generate the next set for you!

🏔️ Geography & Topography
When was Uttarakhand carved out of Uttar Pradesh to become a separate state? November 9, 2000.

What are the official capitals of Uttarakhand? Dehradun (Winter) and Gairsain (Summer).

What is the total geographical area of Uttarakhand? 53,483 sq. km.

How many administrative districts does Uttarakhand have? 13 districts (divided into Garhwal and Kumaon divisions).

Which is the largest district in Uttarakhand by area? Chamoli.

Which is the smallest district in Uttarakhand by area? Champawat.

Which Indian states share borders with Uttarakhand? Himachal Pradesh (North-West) and Uttar Pradesh (South).

Which international borders does the state share? Nepal (East) and Tibet/China (North).

Which is the highest mountain peak in Uttarakhand? Nanda Devi (7,816 meters).

At which exact place do the Bhagirathi and Alaknanda rivers meet to form the Ganga? Devprayag.

Where does the Alaknanda River originate? Satopanth Glacier.

Where does the Bhagirathi River originate? Gaumukh (Gangotri Glacier).

Which river forms the natural eastern boundary between Uttarakhand and Nepal? Sharda (Kali) River.

Which river is primarily responsible for forming the Doon Valley? Song River (along with Asan and Suswa).

Which lake in Uttarakhand is famous as the "Mystery Lake" or "Skeleton Lake"? Roopkund Lake.

Which district is known as the "Lake District of India"? Nainital.

Which pass connects the Pithoragarh district with Tibet and is used for the Kailash Mansarovar Yatra? Lipulekh Pass.

The Khatling Glacier is the origin place of which river? Bhilangana River.

Which village on the Indo-Tibetan border is known as the "First Village of India" (formerly the last village)? Mana (Chamoli district).

On the confluence of which two rivers is the Tehri Dam built? Bhagirathi and Bhilangana.

🦌 State Symbols & Identity
What is the State Animal of Uttarakhand? Alpine Musk Deer.

What is the State Bird of Uttarakhand? Himalayan Monal.

What is the State Flower of Uttarakhand? Brahma Kamal.

What is the State Tree of Uttarakhand? Buransh (Rhododendron).

What is the official State Sport of Uttarakhand? Football.

What is the official State Instrument? Dhol.

What is the State Song of Uttarakhand? "Uttarakhand Dev Bhoomi..." (Written by Hemant Bisht).

What was the original name of the state before it was changed in 2007? Uttaranchal.

Which two rivers are depicted on the state emblem of Uttarakhand? Ganga and Yamuna.

When was Sanskrit declared the second official state language of Uttarakhand? 2010.

📜 History & Freedom Movements
In the ancient Skanda Purana, what name is used to refer to the Garhwal region? Kedar Khand.

In the Skanda Purana, what name is used to refer to the Kumaon region? Manas Khand.

Who founded the Parmar (Paramara) dynasty of Garhwal around 888 CE? Kanakpal.

Which ancient dynasty ruled from the Katyuri Valley (modern Baijnath) and built the Jageshwar temples? The Katyuri Dynasty.

Who was the first British Commissioner of Kumaon after the Anglo-Nepalese War? Edward Gardner.

Which British explorer or administrator is known for implementing early revenue settlements in Kumaon/Garhwal? George William Traill.

Who is the author of the famous historical book "Kumaon Ka Itihas"? Badri Dutt Pandey.

Who is the author of "Garhwal Paintings"? Mukundi Lal.

What was the ancient/historical name of Uttarkashi? Barahat.

Who was the freedom fighter from Tehri Garhwal who died in 1944 after an 84-day hunger strike against autocratic rule? Dev Suman.

Who founded the Gandhi Ashram in Almora? Shanti Lal Chaturvedi.

Who was the first Chief Commissioner of Kumaon in independent India? K.L. Mehta.

Who is the only recipient of the "Bharat Ratna" from Uttarakhand to date? Govind Ballabh Pant (awarded in 1957).

Who was the first recipient of the Padma Vibhushan from Uttarakhand? Ghananand Pandey.

Who was the first Uttarakhand-born winner of a Nobel Prize? Ronald Ross (born in Almora, awarded for Malaria research).

🏛️ Polity & Administration
Who was the first Chief Minister of Uttarakhand? Nityanand Swami.

Who was the first elected Chief Minister of the state (after the first assembly elections)? Narayan Dutt Tiwari.

Who is the only Chief Minister of Uttarakhand to complete a full 5-year term? Narayan Dutt Tiwari.

How many seats does Uttarakhand have in the Lok Sabha? 5.

How many seats are allocated to Uttarakhand in the Rajya Sabha? 3.

What is the total number of members in the Uttarakhand Legislative Assembly (Vidhan Sabha)? 70.

Where is the High Court of Uttarakhand located? Nainital.

Who was the first Governor of Uttarakhand? Surjit Singh Barnala.

Which district has the highest number of tehsils in the state? Pithoragarh.

Where is the Lal Bahadur Shastri National Academy of Administration (LBSNAA) located? Mussoorie.

🎨 Art, Culture & Heritage
What is the traditional martial dance of the Kumaon region called? Chholiya.

Which local festival is known as the "Butter Festival" and is celebrated in Dayara Bugyal? Anduri Utsav.

"Aipan" is a traditional art form of Uttarakhand. What colors does it predominantly use? White paste (rice flour) drawn over a red (geru) background.

What is "Bulaak" in the context of Uttarakhandi culture? A traditional ornament worn on the nose.

Where is the Hill Jatra festival, known for its pastoral and agricultural roots, celebrated? Soar Valley, Pithoragarh.

The famous Egaas Bagwal is celebrated how many days after Diwali? 11 days.

Which district is famous for "Bal Mithai," a sweet made from roasted khoya coated with sugar balls? Almora.

To which deity is the Badrinath temple dedicated? Lord Vishnu.

To which deity is the Kedarnath temple dedicated? Lord Shiva.

Apart from Kedarnath and Badrinath, which two shrines make up the "Char Dham" of Uttarakhand? Gangotri and Yamunotri.

Which Sikh pilgrimage site is located in the Chamoli district at a high altitude? Hemkund Sahib.

Which famous fair is held at the confluence of the Kali and Gori rivers in Jauljibi? Jauljibi Mela.

What is the traditional dress generally worn by Garhwali women called? Ghaghra-Choli (often with a Pichhaura).

Which town is famously known as the "Yoga Capital of the World"? Rishikesh.

Which region of Uttarakhand is known for the Nanda Devi Raj Jat Yatra, held every 12 years? Chamoli/Garhwal region.

🌿 Flora, Fauna & National Parks
Which is the oldest National Park in India, located in Uttarakhand? Jim Corbett National Park (established in 1936).

What was the original name of Jim Corbett National Park? Hailey National Park.

Which National Park in Uttarakhand is located across the Shivalik range in Haridwar, Dehradun, and Pauri? Rajaji National Park.

Which National Park in Uttarakhand is recognized as a UNESCO World Heritage Site? Nanda Devi and Valley of Flowers National Parks.

Which river passes directly through the Valley of Flowers? Pushpawati River.

Which is the largest National Park in Uttarakhand by area? Gangotri National Park.

Which is the smallest National Park in the state? Valley of Flowers National Park.

Where is the Asan Conservation Reserve, known as India's first conservation reserve and a haven for birds, located? Dehradun.

Which wildlife sanctuary is famous for having the maximum number of musk deer? Askot Wildlife Sanctuary (Pithoragarh).

Who was the pioneer of the Chipko Movement in Uttarakhand? Sunderlal Bahuguna and Gaura Devi.

📊 Demographics, Economy & Miscellanea
According to the 2011 census, which district has the highest population? Haridwar.

Which district has the lowest population in the state? Rudraprayag.

Which district boasts the highest literacy rate in Uttarakhand? Dehradun.

Which district has the highest sex ratio in the state? Almora.

Which district has the lowest sex ratio? Haridwar.

Where is the G.B. Pant University of Agriculture and Technology (India's first agricultural university) located? Pantnagar (Udham Singh Nagar).

Where is the Indian Military Academy (IMA) situated? Dehradun.

India's first Chief of Defence Staff (CDS), Gen. Bipin Rawat, belonged to which district of Uttarakhand? Pauri Garhwal.

Who was the first woman from Uttarakhand (and India) to scale Mount Everest? Bachendri Pal.

Who is the author of the famous book "Man-Eaters of Kumaon"? Jim Corbett.

What is the real name of Yogi Adityanath, who was born in Pauri Garhwal? Ajay Singh Bisht.

Which two towns are proposed to be connected by the longest road tunnel in the world (approx. 30 km)? Dehradun and Tehri.

Which place in Uttarakhand is famous for a specific, high-quality variety of tea leaves? Berinag.

Which district is known as the "Mini Kashmir of Uttarakhand"? Pithoragarh.

What is the local name for the berries of the Myrica esculenta tree, heavily consumed in Uttarakhand? Kaphal.

Which district in Uttarakhand has borders with both Nepal and China? Pithoragarh.

Which dam is known as the highest dam in India and Asia? Tehri Dam (260.5 meters).

Which is the oldest canal in the state of Uttarakhand? Upper Ganga Canal.

Which district has the highest net sown agricultural land area? Udham Singh Nagar.

Who was the first woman MP from Uttarakhand after the state's formation? Mala Rajya Laxmi Shah.

Here is the second batch of your master-list! This set dives deep into the river systems, ancient tribes, legendary temples, and key administrative facts of Uttarakhand.

Let’s keep the momentum going with questions 101 through 200:

🌊 Rivers, Glaciers & The Panch Prayag
Which two rivers merge at Vishnuprayag? Alaknanda and Dhauliganga.

Which two rivers meet at Nandaprayag? Alaknanda and Nandakini.

Which two rivers form a confluence at Karnaprayag? Alaknanda and Pindar.

Which two rivers converge at Rudraprayag? Alaknanda and Mandakini.

At which Prayag does the Alaknanda officially become the Ganga? Devprayag (where it meets the Bhagirathi).

Which major river originates from the Bandarpoonch glacier? Yamuna.

The Pindar river originates from which glacier? Pindari Glacier.

Which river is known as "Saryu" in its upper reaches and flows through Ayodhya later? Kali River (also known as Sharda).

Which river system is the largest non-glacial perennial river system in Uttarakhand? Nayar River (Eastern and Western Nayar).

The Dudhatoli Range is the source of which major perennial river? Ramganga (West).

Where does the Mandakini river originate? Chorabari Glacier (near Kedarnath).

Which high-altitude lake is located near the Hemkund Sahib Gurudwara? Lokpal Lake.

Which glacier is the largest in the Kumaon region? Milam Glacier.

Which glacial lake was responsible for the devastating 2013 Kedarnath floods? Chorabari Tal (also known as Gandhi Sarovar).

Which river forms the stunning Sahastradhara waterfalls in Dehradun? Baldi River (a tributary of the Song river).

🛕 Temples, Panch Kedar & Panch Badri
Which is the highest Shiva temple in the world? Tungnath.

Which part of Lord Shiva is worshipped at Kedarnath (Pratham Kedar)? The hump (of the bull form).

Which part of Lord Shiva is worshipped at Tungnath? The arms.

At which Panch Kedar temple is the face of Lord Shiva worshipped? Rudranath.

Which Panch Kedar temple remains open throughout the year? Kalpeshwar (where the matted hair/Jata is worshipped).

At which temple is the navel of Lord Shiva worshipped? Madhyamaheshwar.

Which temple is known as the "Bhavishya Badri" (Future Badrinath)? The temple in Subhai village near Tapovan, Joshimath.

Which of the Panch Badri temples is the only one to remain open year-round? Vridha Badri (Animath village).

In which form is Lord Vishnu worshipped at Yogdhyan Badri? Meditating pose.

Who is believed to have established the Adi Badri temple complex? Adi Shankaracharya.

Which famous temple is situated directly on the banks of the Mandakini river? Kedarnath.

Which Goddess's temple is situated on a rock pillar in the middle of the Alaknanda river near Srinagar? Dhari Devi.

Which revered Sikh shrine was discovered by Sant Sohan Singh and Hawaldar Modan Singh? Hemkund Sahib.

Which ancient sun temple in Uttarakhand is structurally similar to the Konark Sun Temple? Katarmal Sun Temple (Almora).

Which group of temples in Almora district is known for its cluster of 124 ancient stone shrines? Jageshwar Dham.

⛺ Tribes & Local Culture
How many Scheduled Tribes are officially recognized in Uttarakhand? Five (Tharu, Jaunsari, Buksa, Bhotia, and Raji).

Which tribe has the largest population in Uttarakhand? Tharu tribe.

Which tribe claims descent from the Pandavas of the Mahabharata? Jaunsari tribe.

Which tribe has the lowest population in Uttarakhand and is recognized as a Primitive Vulnerable Tribal Group (PVTG)? Raji (also known as Van-Rawat).

Which tribe historically engaged in trans-Himalayan trade with Tibet? Bhotia tribe.

Which tribe primarily inhabits the Terai region of Udham Singh Nagar? Buksa.

What is the traditional local name for the upper Dehradun district inhabited by the Jaunsari people? Jaunsar-Bawar.

Which traditional festival of Uttarakhand involves lighting a bonfire and spinning a rope of fire? Igas Bagwal.

Which spring festival involves young girls placing freshly picked flowers at the thresholds of houses? Phool Dei.

"Jhumelo" and "Nyuoli" are types of what in Uttarakhandi culture? Traditional folk songs.

What is "Pichhaura" in Kumaoni culture? A traditional saffron and red dupatta worn by women during auspicious ceremonies.

Which district hosts the famous "Bagwal" stone-pelting festival at the Devidhura temple? Champawat.

What is the traditional multi-story, earthquake-resistant architectural style of Uttarakhand called? Koti Banal architecture.

Which musical instrument is known as the "Queen of Garhwali instruments"? Mashakbeen (Bagpipe).

What is the "Kandali" (Bichhu Ghas) festival? A festival celebrated every 12 years by the Shauka (Bhotia) tribe in Pithoragarh to commemorate the defeat of an invading army.

🏰 Historical Rulers & Movements
Who built the Khagmara Fort in Almora? King Bhishma Chand.

Who is known as the "King of Kumaon" due to his highly popular and effective administrative tenure? Sir Henry Ramsay.

Which dynasty's kings used the title "Pala" and later adopted the title "Shah" in Garhwal? Parmar (Panwar) Dynasty.

Who was the first king of the Chand dynasty to establish his capital at Champawat? Som Chand.

Who formally shifted the capital of the Chand dynasty from Champawat to Almora? Kalyan Chand.

Which Gurkha commanders led the successful invasion of Kumaon in 1790? Chautariya Bahadur Shah and Amar Singh Thapa.

When did the devastating Gohna Lake flood occur? 1894.

In which year did the famous "Kuli Begar" movement succeed at the banks of the Saryu river in Bageshwar? 1921.

Who founded the "Kumaon Parishad" in 1916? Hargovind Pant, Badri Datt Pandey, and others.

Who is popularly known as "Garh Kesari"? Anusuya Prasad Bahuguna.

Who is known as "Kumaon Kesari"? Badri Datt Pandey.

Which tragic event in Uttarakhand's statehood movement took place on September 2, 1994? Mussoorie firing incident.

Which tragedy took place in Muzaffarnagar during the Uttarakhand statehood movement on October 2, 1994? Rampur Tiraha firing case.

Which historical fort is situated in Pithoragarh and was originally built by the Gorkhas? Pithoragarh Fort (also known as London Fort).

Who established the Shanti Kunj ashram in Haridwar? Pandit Sriram Sharma Acharya.

📈 Administration, Demographics & Institutions
What is the official state fish of Uttarakhand? Golden Mahseer.

According to the 2011 census, what is the approximate total population of Uttarakhand? 1.01 Crore (10,086,292).

Which district has the highest rural population? Haridwar.

Which district has the highest urban population? Dehradun.

Which district has the lowest population density in Uttarakhand? Uttarkashi.

What is the literacy rate of Uttarakhand as per the 2011 census? 78.82%.

Where is the headquarters of the Kumaon Regiment located? Ranikhet.

Where is the headquarters of the Garhwal Rifles located? Lansdowne.

Where is the Forest Research Institute (FRI) located? Dehradun.

Where is the Indian Institute of Remote Sensing (IIRS) situated? Dehradun.

Which institute in Roorkee is one of the oldest engineering colleges in Asia? Indian Institute of Technology (IIT) Roorkee (established in 1847).

In which city is the Aryabhatta Research Institute of Observational Sciences (ARIES) located? Nainital.

Where is the Wadia Institute of Himalayan Geology located? Dehradun.

Where is the High Altitude Plant Physiology Research Centre situated? Srinagar (Garhwal).

Which district is the major industrial hub of Uttarakhand due to the presence of SIDCUL? Udham Singh Nagar (Pantnagar) & Haridwar.

🏔️ Peaks, Passes & Geography
Which mountain pass connects Uttarkashi with Tibet? Thang La.

Which pass connects Chamoli with Pithoragarh? Barahoti Pass.

Which is the second-highest peak in Uttarakhand after Nanda Devi? Kamet (7,756 meters).

Which mountain range separates the Doon Valley from the Gangetic plains? Shivalik Range.

Which bugyal (alpine meadow) is known as the "Meadow of Flowers" and is a popular trek in Chamoli? Gorson Bugyal.

Which bugyal is famous for the "Butter Festival" (Anduri Utsav)? Dayara Bugyal.

Which is the largest bugyal in the Garhwal Himalayas? Bedni Bugyal.

Where is the famous tourist spot 'Khirsu' located? Pauri Garhwal.

Which lake is located near the town of Bhimtal and is larger than Naini Lake? Bhimtal Lake.

Which lake in Uttarakhand has a distinct nine-cornered shape? Naukuchiatal.

Which region is known as the "Orchard Country" of Uttarakhand due to its massive fruit output? Ramgarh (Nainital).

The Asan Barrage, famous for winter migratory birds, is built at the confluence of which rivers? Asan and Yamuna.

Which town is located at the tri-junction of India, Nepal, and Tibet? Dharchula (near Lipulekh).

What is the geographical region immediately south of the Shivaliks, characterized by swamps and dense forests, called? Terai.

What is the boulder-strewn, porous tract located immediately south of the Shivalik foothills called? Bhabar.

🌟 Personalities, Literature & Current Static GK
Who was the first Chief of Army Staff of Uttarakhand origin? Gen. Bipin Chandra Joshi.

Who is the author of the historical book "Dehradun Gazetteer"? H.G. Walton.

Who wrote the famous monumental work "Himalayan Gazetteer"? E.T. Atkinson.

Which environmentalist from Uttarakhand was awarded the Ramon Magsaysay Award in 1982 for his work with the Chipko Movement? Chandi Prasad Bhatt.

Which famous poet from Kumaon is known as "Lokratna" and is considered one of the earliest poets of the region? Gumani Pant.

Who was the first person from Uttarakhand to win the Dronacharya Award? Hari Singh Thapa (Boxing).

Who was the first woman Jagar singer of Uttarakhand to be awarded the Padma Shri? Basanti Bisht.

Which famous British-descent author has lived in Landour, Mussoorie for decades and wrote "The Room on the Roof"? Ruskin Bond.

Which Kumaoni revolutionary is often called the "Gandhi of Uttarakhand"? Indramani Badoni.

Who was the first Chairman of the Uttarakhand Public Service Commission (UKPSC)? N.P. Navani.

Here is the third and final batch to complete your master-list of 300 High-Yield Uttarakhand GK Questions. This section focuses heavily on the economy, historical movements, local geography, and the unique cultural heritage of the state.

Let’s bring it home with questions 201 through 300:

🏭 Economy, Infrastructure & Institutions
Which was the first hydroelectric power project built in Uttarakhand (and North India)? Galogi Power Plant (Mussoorie, commissioned in 1909).

Where is the largest paper mill in Uttarakhand located? Century Paper Mill, Lalkuan (Nainital).

In which year was the Roorkee Cantonment established? 1853.

When was the Kumaon University and Garhwal University (now HNBGU) established? 1973.

Where is the headquarters of the Oil and Natural Gas Corporation (ONGC) located? Dehradun.

Which institute in Uttarakhand trains the officers of the Indian Police Service (IPS)? Wait, that's in Hyderabad (SVPNPA)—but the Indira Gandhi National Forest Academy (IGNFA), which trains Indian Forest Service (IFS) officers, is located in Dehradun.

Where is the VIP training ground, the Doon School, located and when was it established? Dehradun, established in 1935 by Satish Ranjan Das.

Where is the Vivekananda Parvatiya Krishi Anusandhan Sansthan (VPKAS) situated? Almora (founded by Prof. Boshi Sen).

Who was the first Director-General of Police (DGP) of Uttarakhand? Ashok Kant Sharan.

Who was the first Chief Justice of the Uttarakhand High Court? Justice Ashok A. Desai.

🌳 Forests, Environment & Wildlife
How many National Parks are there in Uttarakhand? 6.

How many Wildlife Sanctuaries are located in Uttarakhand? 7.

Which district has the maximum total forest cover by area? Pauri Garhwal.

Which district has the highest percentage of its geographical area under forest cover? Nainital.

What is the State Butterfly of Uttarakhand? Common Peacock (Papilio bianor), declared in 2016.

In which year was the Nanda Devi Biosphere Reserve established? 1988.

Which prominent environmental movement was initiated by Kalyan Singh Rawat in 1995? Maiti Movement (planting trees during weddings).

Who led the "Beej Bachao Andolan" (Save the Seed Movement) in Uttarakhand? Vijay Jardhari.

What is the main objective of the Raksha Sutra Andolan, started in 1994 in Tehri Garhwal? Protecting trees from being cut by tying a sacred thread (Raksha Sutra) around them.

In which season does the state tree, Buransh, typically bloom? Spring (February to April).

📖 History, Treaties & Freedom Struggle
When did the Gorkhas first invade and capture Kumaon? 1790.

For how many years did the Gorkhas rule Kumaon and Garhwal, respectively? 25 years in Kumaon (1790-1815) and 10.5 years in Garhwal (1804-1815).

Which historic battle in 1804 resulted in the death of King Pradyumna Shah against the Gorkhas? Battle of Khurbura (Dehradun).

Which treaty officially ended the Anglo-Nepalese War and the Gorkha rule in Uttarakhand? Treaty of Sugauli (signed 1815, ratified 1816).

Who was the last ruling king of the Tehri Riyasat (Princely State)? Manabendra Shah.

On what date did the Tehri Princely State officially merge with the Indian Union? August 1, 1949.

When was the "Almora Akhbar" started? 1871.

Who started the prominent weekly newspaper "Shakti" in 1918 after Almora Akhbar was banned? Badri Datt Pandey.

Where did Mahatma Gandhi stay for 14 days in 1929, writing a commentary on the Gita? Anasakti Ashram in Kausani.

Which place in Uttarakhand did Mahatma Gandhi famously describe as the "Switzerland of India"? Kausani.

Who led the Peshawar Kand in 1930? Veer Chandra Singh Garhwali.

Which regiment of the British Indian Army refused to fire on unarmed Pathans during the Peshawar Kand? 2/18 Royal Garhwal Rifles.

Who was the first person from Uttarakhand to be jailed during the Indian freedom struggle? Mohan Singh Mehta.

Which incident is known as the "Bardoli of Kumaon" due to its role in the Quit India Movement? The Sult incident (September 5, 1942).

Who wrote the historical book "Garhwal Ka Itihas"? Hari Krishna Raturi.

🗺️ Places, Geography & Natural Wonders
Which district is known as "Tamra Nagari" (Copper City) of Uttarakhand? Almora.

Which popular town is colloquially known as "Chhoti Vilayat"? Nainital.

Which city serves as the "Gateway of Garhwal"? Kotdwar.

Which city serves as the "Gateway of Kumaon"? Kathgodam.

Which place is famously known as the "Cherrapunji of Uttarakhand" because it receives the highest rainfall in the state? Narendra Nagar (Tehri Garhwal).

Who discovered the Valley of Flowers in 1931? Frank S. Smythe (a British mountaineer).

What is the longest river entirely flowing within the borders of Uttarakhand? Kali River (252 km).

Which is the highest waterfall in Uttarakhand? Birthi Falls (126 meters) in Pithoragarh.

Which famous sulphur-rich spring/waterfall is located in Dehradun? Sahastradhara.

Where is the beautiful high-altitude meadow "Moila Top" located? Chakrata (Dehradun district).

Which lake's water is famously known to have a reddish hue? Vasuki Tal (near Kedarnath).

Dodi Tal, a famous trekking destination in Uttarkashi, is known for what unique shape? Six-cornered (Hexagonal).

Satopanth Lake, located ahead of Badrinath, has what distinct shape? Triangular.

Where is Patal Bhuvaneshwar, a limestone cave temple, located? Pithoragarh.

Lakhamandal, famous for its ancient Shiva temple and Mahabharata legends (Lakshagriha), is located in which district? Dehradun (Jaunsar-Bawar region).

🎪 Local Culture, Fairs & Art
Which popular folk dance of the Jaunsar-Bawar region is performed during festivals? Barada Nati.

What is "Langvir Nritya"? An acrobatic dance performed by men in the Garhwal region, involving a long bamboo pole.

Golu Devta, whose most famous temple is at Chitai (Almora), is worshipped as the God of what? Justice.

What unique offering is made by devotees at the Chitai Golu Devta temple? Brass bells and written petitions (letters) seeking justice.

Where is the Haat Kalika Temple, highly revered by the Kumaon Regiment, located? Gangolihat (Pithoragarh).

The Gauchar Mela is a historic trade and cultural fair held in which district? Chamoli.

In which year did Deputy Commissioner Burnell start the Gauchar Mela? 1943.

Which fair in the Jaunsar-Bawar region features a mock bow-and-arrow battle? Bishu Mela.

Which renowned painter was the central figure of the Garhwal School of Painting? Mola Ram.

Where was Mola Ram's art gallery located? Srinagar (Garhwal).

What is "Jhangoora" in the local Uttarakhand diet? Barnyard Millet (often used to make sweet kheer).

What is "Ugal" in the context of Uttarakhandi agriculture? Local buckwheat (used to make flour during fasting).

What is the traditional name for terrace farming practiced in the hilly terrain of Uttarakhand? C.D. Numaar Kheti.

Where is the Syahi Devi temple located? Almora.

Which famous fair is held in memory of Veer Chandra Singh Garhwali in the Pauri district? Peethsain Mela.

⭐ Miscellaneous & Firsts
Who was the first woman Governor of Uttarakhand? Margaret Alva.

Who is known as the "Mushroom Lady of Uttarakhand" for popularizing mushroom cultivation? Divya Rawat.

Who was the first person to receive the "Uttarakhand Khel Ratna" award? Jaspal Rana (Shooting).

Where is the Kaleshwar Mahadev temple, named after Sage Kapil, located? Lansdowne.

The Tapkeshwar Mahadev Temple in Dehradun is located on the banks of which river? Tamsa (Tons) River.

Vashistha Gufa, an ancient meditation cave, is situated on the banks of the Ganga near which town? Rishikesh (in Tehri Garhwal district).

Kankhal (in Haridwar) is historically associated with which mythological figure? Daksha Prajapati.

The Chandi Devi Temple in Haridwar is situated atop which hill? Neel Parvat.

The Mansa Devi Temple in Haridwar is situated atop which hill? Bilwa Parvat.

Which ashram near Rishikesh was established by Madeleine Slade (Mira Behn), a disciple of Gandhi? Pashulok Ashram.

Kainchi Dham, which attracts visitors worldwide including tech billionaires, is associated with which spiritual leader? Neem Karoli Baba.

What is the total distance covered during the Nanda Devi Raj Jat Yatra? Approximately 280 km.

From which specific village does the Nanda Devi Raj Jat Yatra commence? Nauti village (Chamoli).

Which river valley in Uttarkashi is famous for the cultivation of high-quality red rice? Sirai Valley (Purola).

Who was the first woman Vice-Chancellor of a university in Uttarakhand? Sushila Dobhal.

Which state highway in Uttarakhand is famously known as the "Kailash Mansarovar Route"? State Highway linking Dharchula to Lipulekh.

Which district of Uttarakhand has the highest number of Gram Panchayats? Pauri Garhwal.

Which district has the lowest number of Gram Panchayats? Champawat.

What is the name of the state's traditional agricultural instrument used for leveling plowed fields? Patela or Mayi.

What was the historical name of the Haridwar region as mentioned in ancient texts? Mayapuri.

In the context of local administration, what was the "Khat" system in the Jaunsar-Bawar region? A cluster of villages administered by a "Sadar Sayana".

What was the "Padhan" in the traditional Kumaoni administrative structure? The village headman responsible for revenue collection.

What is the ancient name of Joshimath? Jyotirmath.

Who is credited with restoring the Badrinath temple in the 8th century? Adi Shankaracharya.

At what location in Uttarakhand did Adi Shankaracharya attain Mahasamadhi? Kedarnath.

What is the name of the traditional copper or brass vessel used to store water in Uttarakhandi households? Gaggar.

Which town was the traditional capital of the Katyuri Dynasty before they moved to Baijnath? Joshimath.

What is the literal translation of "Panch Prayag"? The Five Confluences.

Who was the first Kumaoni author to win the Sahitya Akademi Award? Ramesh Chandra Shah (for his novel Vinayak in 2014, though Ruskin Bond won earlier from the state). Correction for pure local language: Sumitranandan Pant is the literary giant of Hindi from Kumaon, but for the Sahitya Akademi Award specifically, Sudama Prasad 'Premi' and others are noted. Let's keep it clean: Which prominent Hindi poet from Kumaon won the Jnanpith Award in 1968? Sumitranandan Pant.

Which organization operates the 108 emergency ambulance service in Uttarakhand? The state government (often called the Deen Dayal Upadhyay 108 Service).

Which district was created by carving out parts of Dehradun, Saharanpur, and Haridwar? Haridwar (created in 1988, before the state's formation).

Where is the state's only Indian Institute of Management (IIM) located? Kashipur (Udham Singh Nagar).

Which city in Uttarakhand is known as the "Cultural Capital of Kumaon"? Almora.

Which river is known as the "Sorrow of Kumaon" due to its historical flooding? Kosi River.

On what exact date did "Uttaranchal" officially change its name to "Uttarakhand"? January 1, 2007.

And there you have it—a complete 300-question stronghold of Uttarakhand GK! Save these, revise them in chunks, and you'll be in great shape for your UKPSC exams. Good luck!

You are absolutely crushing this prep! Moving beyond the top 300 means you are now digging into the "make-or-break" territory of UKPSC exams. State exams love to test deeper local history, specific administrative details, local dialects, and micro-geography.

Here is Batch 4 (Questions 301–350), focusing on advanced and niche Uttarakhand GK:

🗿 Deep History & Administration
In which script is the famous Ashokan Rock Edict at Kalsi written? Brahmi script (and the language used is Prakrit).

Who established the "Garh Desh Seva Sangh" in Delhi in 1938 to advocate for a separate state? Sridev Suman.

Which British Commissioner of Kumaon introduced the "Patwari" system for revenue collection in 1819? George William Traill.

Who was the only king of the Chand dynasty to visit the court of Mughal Emperor Akbar? Rudra Chand.

The infamous "Kuli Begar" register, which mandated unpaid labor, was washed away in which river in 1921? Saryu River (at Bageshwar).

Which historical treaty formalized the boundaries between British India and Nepal, shaping Uttarakhand's eastern border? Treaty of Sugauli (signed 1815, ratified 1816).

Who was the first elected speaker of the Uttarakhand Legislative Assembly? Yashpal Arya (Prakash Pant was the first interim speaker).

How many municipal corporations (Nagar Nigams) are currently in Uttarakhand? 9 (Dehradun, Haridwar, Haldwani, Rudrapur, Kashipur, Roorkee, Rishikesh, Kotdwar, and Srinagar).

Which district was carved out of Pithoragarh in 1997? Champawat.

What is the official name of the state's e-governance portal for digitized land records? Devbhoomi Portal.

🏔️ Advanced Geography & Economy
Which high-altitude pass connects the Darma Valley with the Lassar Yankti Valley in Pithoragarh? Sin La Pass.

Traill's Pass connects which two major valleys? Pindari Valley and Milam Valley.

Which underground hydroelectric project is built on the Tons River in the Dehradun district? Chibro Power Station.

The Maneri Bhali Hydroelectric Project is located on which river? Bhagirathi River (in Uttarkashi).

Where is the prominent magnesite mining area, Jhiroli, located? Bageshwar district.

What is the name of the major geological fault line that separates the Greater Himalayas from the Lesser (Middle) Himalayas in Uttarakhand? Main Central Thrust (MCT).

The Askot Musk Deer Sanctuary is primarily located in the basin of which river? Goriganga River.

Which spectacular alpine meadow (bugyal) is situated en route to the Roopkund trek? Bedni Bugyal.

Where is the Govind Ballabh Pant National Institute of Himalayan Environment (NIHE) located? Kosi-Katarmal (Almora).

Approximately how long is the international border that Uttarakhand shares with Nepal? 275 km.

🎭 Culture, Art & Heritage
In which year was the traditional Garhwali festival "Ramman" added to the UNESCO Representative List of the Intangible Cultural Heritage of Humanity? 2009.

Which wind instrument is considered the traditional leading instrument during the Chholiya martial dance? Turi (often accompanied by the Ransingha).

What is a "Naula" or "Dhara" in the context of traditional Uttarakhand? Traditional, step-well style freshwater springs/aquifers.

Which famous Kumaoni poet was popularly known as "Gaurda"? Gauri Datt Pande.

Who is known as the "Charan" (Wanderer) of Uttarakhand for his extensive historical travelogues? Shiv Prasad Dabral.

Which temple complex in Kumaon famously features the "Kacheri" (Court) of Golu Devta, where devotees hang letters requesting justice? Chitai Temple.

Which famous Mela (fair) is held at the Tapkeshwar Mahadev temple in Dehradun? Shivratri Mela.

Which unique traditional ornament is worn tightly around the neck (like a choker) by rural women in Uttarakhand? Gulaband.

What is "Thadya"? A traditional circle dance performed in the courtyards (Thad) of homes in Garhwal.

What is "Aipan"? A traditional ritualistic folk art of Kumaon, made using a white paste of rice flour on a brick-red background.

🏢 Institutes & Contemporary GK
Where is the Uttarakhand Space Application Centre (USAC) located? Dehradun.

In which city is the National Institute for the Empowerment of Persons with Visual Disabilities (formerly NIVH) located? Dehradun.

Where is the Uttarakhand Tea Development Board headquartered? Almora.

Which city hosts the Defence Agricultural Research Laboratory (DARL)? Pithoragarh.

Who was the first Chief Information Commissioner of Uttarakhand? R.S. Tolia.

Which operation was launched by the Indian Armed Forces to rescue stranded people during the devastating 2013 Kedarnath floods? Operation Surya Hope.

What is the primary focus of the "Vande Mataram Scheme" launched by the Uttarakhand government? Providing free health check-ups and treatment for pregnant women.

The proposed Jamrani Dam project, which is frequently in the news, is to be built on which river? Gola River (near Haldwani).

Where is the famous "Neelkanth Mahadev" temple situated? Pauri Garhwal district (often accessed via Rishikesh).

Where is the Indian Institute of Ayurveda for Drug Research located in Uttarakhand? Tarikhet (Almora).

⚡ Miscellaneous Hard-Hitters
Which breathtaking Himalayan peak in Uttarakhand is often referred to as the "Matterhorn of Garhwal"? Shivling Peak.

What is the ancient and mythological name of Har-ki-Pauri in Haridwar? Brahmakund.

Which tribal communities in Uttarakhand traditionally speak the "Rongpa" language? The Marchha and Tolchha (sub-groups of the Bhotia tribe).

Who was the first person to hoist the Indian tricolor in the Pauri Garhwal district in 1930? Jayanand Bharti.

Which famous temple is located precisely at the confluence of the Gomati and Saryu rivers? Baijnath Temple.

What is the name of the famous high-altitude zoo located in Nainital? Pt. G.B. Pant High Altitude Zoo.

Which major river originates from the massive Milam Glacier? Goriganga River.

What was the name of the secret society formed by Kalu Mahara (Uttarakhand's first freedom fighter) during the 1857 revolt? Krantiveer.

In the traditional Kumaoni administrative structure, what was the role of a "Padhan"? The village headman responsible for local revenue collection and maintaining order.

Who was the first recipient of the prestigious Sahitya Akademi Award for Hindi literature from Uttarakhand? Sumitranandan Pant (Note: He is famously a Jnanpith Award winner; Ruskin Bond won the Sahitya Akademi Award for English literature from the state).

You are absolutely relentless, and that is exactly the mindset you need to crack the UKPSC! Pushing past the 350-mark means we are now getting into the deep cuts—the highly specific, nuanced facts that separate the top-tier candidates from the rest.

Here is Batch 5 (Questions 351–450), focusing heavily on deep geography, local administration, grassroots social movements, tribal facts, and essential infrastructure.

🏔️ Deep Geography, Glaciers & Prayags
Which river forms the natural boundary between Dehradun (Uttarakhand) and Sirmaur (Himachal Pradesh)? Tons River.

At which exact location do the Tons and Yamuna rivers meet? Kalsi (Dakpathar).

Which mountain range in Uttarakhand is popularly known as the "Pamir of Uttarakhand"? Dudhatoli Range.

The Dudhatoli Range spans across which three districts? Pauri, Chamoli, and Almora.

Which specific Prayag (confluence) is formed by the meeting of the Alaknanda and Saraswati rivers? Keshav Prayag (near Mana village).

Where is the "Bhim Pul," a massive natural rock bridge situated over the roaring Saraswati river, located? Mana Village (Chamoli).

Which famous Himalayan peak in Uttarakhand is shaped like a three-pronged spear? Trisul Peak.

Which glacier is the primary source of the Pindar River? Pindari Glacier.

The Kafni Glacier is located in which district? Bageshwar.

What is the name of the bowl-shaped high-altitude lake situated near the Gangotri shrine? Kedar Tal.

Which district is known as the "Gateway to Yamunotri and Gangotri"? Rishikesh is the gateway to the Char Dham, but Uttarkashi is the specific gateway district to these two shrines.

What is the traditional name of the high-altitude trade route previously used by Bhotia merchants to travel to Tibet? Johar Valley (Salt Route).

Which major river originates from the eastern slopes of the Trisul massif and merges with the Alaknanda at Karnaprayag? Pindar River.

The "Main Boundary Thrust" (MBT) separates which two geological regions in Uttarakhand? The Lesser (Middle) Himalayas and the Shivalik Range.

Which district of Uttarakhand is entirely situated in the plains (Terai/Bhabar) with virtually no mountainous terrain? Udham Singh Nagar.

📜 History & Grassroots Movements
Who was the reigning king of the Garhwal Kingdom during the devastating Gurkha invasion of 1804? King Pradyumna Shah.

Who established the new Tehri Princely State in 1815 after the Gurkhas were defeated? King Sudarshan Shah.

Who were the two British commanding officers who successfully led the Anglo-Nepalese war in Kumaon? Colonel William Linnaeus Gardner and Major Hearsey.

In which year did the Kumaon Parishad officially merge with the Indian National Congress? 1926.

The historic "Dola-Palki" movement in Uttarakhand fought against what social injustice? The denial of the right of the Shilpkar (Dalit) community to use palanquins (dola-palki) during weddings.

Who was the prominent leader and driving force behind the Dola-Palki movement? Jayanand Bharti.

What was the ancient, historical name of the modern city of Kashipur? Govishan.

The important archaeological and historical site of Purola is located in the valley of which river? Kamal River.

Which famous newspaper was started from Dehradun in 1905 to awaken the social and political consciousness of the Garhwal region? Garhwali.

Who was the author of the essential historical text "Garhwal Ka Itihas"? Hari Krishna Raturi.

Who was the founder of the Katyuri dynasty, the first historical empire of Uttarakhand? Basant Dev.

What was the original capital of the Katyuri dynasty before they shifted to Kartikeyapura (Baijnath)? Joshimath.

Which prominent freedom fighter from Uttarakhand went on to serve as the Home Minister of India? Govind Ballabh Pant.

Who founded the "Himalayan Institute Hospital Trust" (HIHT) at Jolly Grant? Swami Rama.

In which year did the famous Chipko Movement receive the prestigious Right Livelihood Award (Alternative Nobel)? 1987.

🎭 Culture, Tribes & Traditions
Which major Kumaoni festival is strictly associated with agriculture, the planting of saplings, and the onset of the monsoon? Harela.

Which traditional festival is celebrated on the very first day of the Hindu month of Chaitra (mid-March) to welcome spring? Phool Dei.

What is the primary spiritual purpose of the "Jagar" tradition in Uttarakhand? To awaken and invoke local deities or ancestral spirits to seek their blessings or solutions to problems.

Which two traditional musical instruments are essential during a Jagar performance? Dhol and Damau (often accompanied by a Hurka).

The "Jad" community is a specific sub-tribe of the Bhotias living primarily in the Jad Ganga valley of which district? Uttarkashi.

Which traditional Uttarakhandi sweet, especially popular during weddings, is made strictly from rice flour and jaggery? Arsa.

What is a "Nathuli" in Uttarakhandi culture? A large, traditional gold ring worn on the nose by married women.

The four-horned ram, known locally as "Chausingha Khadu," is a central, sacred figure in which major pilgrimage? Nanda Devi Raj Jat Yatra.

In which specific fair do locals partake in the "Bagwal"—a tradition of throwing stones at each other using massive wooden shields? Devidhura Mela (at the Varahi Devi temple).

Who was the renowned Kumaoni poet popularly known by the pen name "Gumani Kavi"? Lokratna Pant.

Which famous Hindi poet from Kumaon won the Jnanpith Award for his collection "Chidambara"? Sumitranandan Pant.

Which traditional Kumaoni festival marks the victory of the King of Kumaon over Garhwal and involves lighting massive bonfires? Khatarwa.

Gaura Devi, the iconic environmentalist of the Chipko Movement, belonged to which specific village? Reni Village (Chamoli).

According to local legend, in which cave did Sage Vyasa dictate the Mahabharata to Lord Ganesha? Vyas Gufa (Mana Village).

What is the scientific name of the state flower, Brahma Kamal? Saussurea obvallata.

🏛️ Administration & Polity
In which year was the comprehensive Uttarakhand Panchayati Raj Act passed by the state assembly? 2016.

What percentage of seats are reserved for women in the Panchayati Raj Institutions (PRIs) of Uttarakhand? 50%.

What is the official term length of a Mayor in a Municipal Corporation (Nagar Nigam) in Uttarakhand? 5 years.

Who formally administers the oath of office to the Governor of Uttarakhand? The Chief Justice of the Uttarakhand High Court.

How many total Development Blocks (Kshetra Panchayats) are there in the state of Uttarakhand? 95.

Who was the Chief Minister of Uttar Pradesh when the Uttarakhand statehood movement was at its peak in 1994 (during the Rampur Tiraha incident)? Mulayam Singh Yadav.

Which article of the Indian Constitution was invoked by the Parliament to create the state of Uttarakhand? Article 3.

Where is the headquarters of the Uttarakhand State Election Commission located? Dehradun.

Which state government entity handles the generation of hydroelectric power in Uttarakhand? UJVNL (Uttarakhand Jal Vidyut Nigam Limited).

What is the total number of Zila Panchayats in Uttarakhand? 13 (one for each district).

🛫 Infrastructure, Economy & Institutes
The Koteshwar Dam, a run-of-the-river power project, is built on which river? Bhagirathi River (downstream from the Tehri Dam).

Where is the massive manufacturing plant of Bharat Heavy Electricals Limited (BHEL) situated in Uttarakhand? Haridwar (Ranipur).

What was the primary objective of the "Aarohi Project" launched by the Uttarakhand government? To provide computer education and training to teachers and students in government schools.

Where is the prestigious Indian Institute of Petroleum (IIP) located? Dehradun.

Where is the National Institute of Hydrology (NIH) situated? Roorkee.

"Keeda Jadi" (Yartsa Gunbu), a highly expensive high-altitude medicinal fungus, is biologically known as what? Cordyceps sinensis.

In which year was the headquarters of the Survey of India shifted from Calcutta to Dehradun? 1942 (during World War II).

Where is the Central Building Research Institute (CBRI), a premier constituent of CSIR, located? Roorkee.

Which is the primary, busiest airport serving the Garhwal region of Uttarakhand? Jolly Grant Airport (Dehradun).

Which domestic airport is located near Haldwani to serve the Kumaon region? Pantnagar Airport.

Where is the Naini Saini Airport situated? Pithoragarh.

Which domestic airstrip is located in the Chamoli district and is frequently used during the Char Dham Yatra and rescue operations? Gauchar Airport.

What is the designated National Highway number for the main route leading to Badrinath? NH-7.

What is the designated National Highway number for the route leading to Kedarnath? NH-107.

The massive infrastructure project to upgrade the roads connecting the four holy shrines is officially known as what? Char Dham Pariyojana (often referred to as the All-Weather Road Project).

🌿 Wildlife & Miscellaneous
Which National Park in Uttarakhand is particularly famous for its large population of wild Asian Elephants? Rajaji National Park.

The "Ardh Kumbh Mela" in Haridwar is celebrated every how many years? 6 years.

The catastrophic 2021 Chamoli disaster, triggered by a rock and ice avalanche, primarily wiped out power projects on which two rivers? Rishi Ganga and Dhauliganga.

At approximately what altitude range is the State Flower, Brahma Kamal, typically found growing in the Himalayas? 3,000 to 4,800 meters.

Where is the famous "Vashistha Gufa" (Cave), an ancient meditation site, located? On the banks of the Ganga near Rishikesh (Tehri Garhwal district).

What was the official mascot of the 38th National Games, which were slated to be hosted by Uttarakhand? Monal (the State Bird).

Which town in Uttarakhand is famously associated with the production of high-quality cricket bats made from locally sourced willow? Dehradun.

Who is the current sitting Chief Minister of Uttarakhand? (Pushkar Singh Dhami - Note: Always double-check this before your exam as political positions change!)

Which specific region in Uttarakhand is famous for the cultivation of "Munshyari Rajma," which recently received a GI Tag? Munsiyari (Pithoragarh).

Which indigenous breed of hill cattle from Uttarakhand was recognized as India’s first registered cattle breed from the state? Badri Cow.

🔥 Quick-Fire Districts & Towns
Which town is known as the "City of Lakes" in Uttarakhand? Nainital.

Which town in Pithoragarh is often called the "Kashmir of Uttarakhand" due to its stunning valleys? Munsiyari.

Where is the famous Dronasagar Lake, associated with Guru Dronacharya of the Mahabharata, located? Kashipur (Udham Singh Nagar).

Which place is famously known as the "Apple Bowl of Uttarakhand"? Harsil (Uttarkashi).

The ancient Bagnath Temple, dedicated to Lord Shiva, is situated in which district? Bageshwar.

Which specific bridge in Rishikesh is an iconic iron suspension bridge built over the Ganges in 1929? Lakshman Jhula.

Which town serves as the starting point for the famous trek to the Valley of Flowers and Hemkund Sahib? Govindghat.

The prominent tourist destination of "Auli," known for skiing and winter sports, is located in which district? Chamoli.

Which town in Tehri Garhwal was originally built by King Narendra Shah and is known for its high rainfall? Narendra Nagar.

Which ancient temple complex in the Almora district is surrounded by massive Deodar trees and houses 124 distinct stone shrines? Jageshwar Dham.

📚 Education & Literacy
What is the female literacy rate of Uttarakhand according to the 2011 Census? Approximately 70.70%.

Which district of Uttarakhand has the lowest female literacy rate? Uttarkashi.

Which district has the highest male literacy rate? Rudraprayag.

Which university in Uttarakhand was granted the status of a "Central University" in 2009? Hemvati Nandan Bahuguna Garhwal University (HNBGU).

Where is the Doon University, established by the state government, located? Dehradun.

Which town is home to the prestigious St. Joseph's College (often called "Plumstead") and Sherwood College? Nainital.

Where is the Uttarakhand Board of School Education (UBSE) headquartered? Ramnagar (Nainital).

In which year was the Indian Institute of Technology (IIT) Roorkee officially elevated from the University of Roorkee to an IIT? 2001.

Which prominent medical institute in Uttarakhand was established under the Pradhan Mantri Swasthya Suraksha Yojana (PMSSY)? AIIMS Rishikesh.

What is the official motto of the Lal Bahadur Shastri National Academy of Administration (LBSNAA) located in Mussoorie? Sheelam Param Bhushanam (Character is the highest virtue).

🏛️ Indian Polity & Constitution
Who is known as the Father of the Indian Constitution? Dr. B.R. Ambedkar.

When was the Constitution of India adopted by the Constituent Assembly? November 26, 1949.

When did the Constitution of India come into force? January 26, 1950.

The concept of Fundamental Rights was borrowed from which country? United States of America.

The Directive Principles of State Policy (DPSP) were adopted from which country? Ireland.

Which Article is known as the "Heart and Soul of the Constitution" by Dr. B.R. Ambedkar? Article 32 (Right to Constitutional Remedies).

What is the minimum age to become a member of the Lok Sabha? 25 years.

What is the minimum age to become a member of the Rajya Sabha? 30 years.

Who appoints the Chief Election Commissioner of India? The President of India.

Who was the first Chief Election Commissioner of independent India? Sukumar Sen.

Which Constitutional Amendment is famously known as the 'Mini Constitution'? 42nd Amendment Act (1976).

The Panchayati Raj system was given constitutional status by which amendment? 73rd Amendment Act (1992).

How many Schedules are there in the Indian Constitution currently? 12.

Which Article deals with the abolition of "Untouchability"? Article 17.

Who is the ex-officio Chairman of the Rajya Sabha? The Vice President of India.

What is the maximum sanctioned strength of the Lok Sabha? 550 (previously 552 before Anglo-Indian reserved seats were removed).

Who was the first female Governor of an Indian state? Sarojini Naidu (Uttar Pradesh).

Who was the first female Chief Minister of an Indian state? Sucheta Kripalani (Uttar Pradesh).

The concept of the Concurrent List was borrowed from the constitution of which country? Australia.

Which Article deals with the declaration of a Financial Emergency? Article 360.

📜 Indian History (Ancient, Medieval, Modern)
Which Harappan site is famous for having an artificial brick dockyard? Lothal (Gujarat).

Who was the founder of the Mauryan Empire? Chandragupta Maurya.

Which Chinese traveler visited India during the reign of Harshavardhana? Hiuen Tsang (Xuanzang).

What was the capital of the Pallava dynasty? Kanchipuram.

Who was the founder of the Gupta Dynasty? Sri Gupta.

Who commissioned the construction of the Sanchi Stupa? Emperor Ashoka.

In which year was the First Battle of Panipat fought? 1526.

Between whom was the First Battle of Panipat fought? Babur and Ibrahim Lodi.

Which Mughal emperor introduced the Mansabdari system? Akbar.

Who built the historic Grand Trunk Road? Sher Shah Suri.

Who was the last Mughal Emperor of India? Bahadur Shah II (Bahadur Shah Zafar).

Who founded the Indian National Congress (INC) in 1885? Allan Octavian Hume (A.O. Hume).

Who was the first President of the Indian National Congress? Womesh Chandra Bonnerjee (W.C. Bonnerjee).

In which year did the Partition of Bengal take place? 1905 (announced by Lord Curzon).

On what date did the tragic Jallianwala Bagh massacre happen? April 13, 1919.

Who originally coined the famous slogan "Inquilab Zindabad"? Maulana Hasrat Mohani (popularized later by Bhagat Singh).

In which year was the First Round Table Conference held? 1930.

In which year was the Gandhi-Irwin Pact signed? 1931.

Who founded the political party 'Forward Bloc' in 1939? Subhas Chandra Bose.

In which year was the Quit India Movement launched? 1942.

Who was the first Governor-General of independent India? Lord Mountbatten.

Who was the first and only Indian Governor-General of independent India? C. Rajagopalachari.

Who is popularly known as the 'Iron Man of India'? Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel.

In which year was the Battle of Plassey fought? 1757.

In which year was the Battle of Buxar fought? 1764.

Who founded the socio-religious reform movement Brahmo Samaj? Raja Ram Mohan Roy.

Who founded the Arya Samaj? Swami Dayanand Saraswati.

Who founded the Ramakrishna Mission? Swami Vivekananda.

Who is considered the first martyr of the 1857 Revolt? Mangal Pandey.

Who wrote India's national song, "Vande Mataram"? Bankim Chandra Chatterjee.

🌍 Indian Geography & Environment
What is the southernmost tip of the Indian territory called? Indira Point (in the Andaman and Nicobar Islands).

Which is the highest mountain peak situated entirely within India? Kangchenjunga (Sikkim).

Which is the longest river in India? Ganga (2,525 km).

Which river is notoriously known as the 'Sorrow of Bihar'? Kosi River.

Which river was historically known as the 'Sorrow of Bengal'? Damodar River.

Majuli, recognized as the world's largest river island, is located on which river? Brahmaputra (in Assam).

Which Indian state has the longest coastline? Gujarat.

According to area, which state has the largest forest cover in India? Madhya Pradesh.

Which is the highest waterfall in India? Kunchikal Falls (Karnataka).

Chilika Lake, the largest brackish water lagoon in India, is in which state? Odisha.

Which is the largest freshwater lake in India? Wular Lake (Jammu & Kashmir).

Which imaginary line passes almost through the middle of India? Tropic of Cancer.

The Tropic of Cancer passes through how many Indian states? 8 states.

Which Indian state shares its international border with three countries (Nepal, Bhutan, and China)? Sikkim.

Which is the oldest fold mountain range in India? Aravalli Range.

At which hill range do the Eastern Ghats and Western Ghats meet? Nilgiri Hills.

Which Indian city is famously known as the "Silicon Valley of India"? Bengaluru.

Kaziranga National Park in Assam is world-famous for conserving which animal? Indian One-Horned Rhinoceros.

Gir National Park in Gujarat is the only natural habitat in the world for which animal? Asiatic Lions.

Which is the longest dam in India? Hirakud Dam (Odisha).

Which is the highest dam in India? Tehri Dam (Uttarakhand).

Which was the first National Park established in India (1936)? Jim Corbett National Park.

Which Indian state is often referred to as the "Spice Garden of India"? Kerala.

Black soil (Regur soil) is highly suitable for cultivating which crop? Cotton.

Where is India's only active volcano located? Barren Island (Andaman Sea).

Which narrow strait separates India and Sri Lanka? Palk Strait.

Which state has the largest area under wasteland/desert? Rajasthan.

Indian Standard Time (IST) is calculated from the clock tower of which city? Mirzapur (Uttar Pradesh).

What is the name of the largest desert in India? Thar Desert.

Which river is known as the 'Dakshin Ganga' (Ganges of the South)? Godavari.

🚀 Economy, Science & Tech (B.Tech Special!)
When does the official Financial Year start in India? April 1.

Which organization is responsible for estimating the National Income of India? National Statistical Office (NSO).

Who is known as the Father of the Green Revolution in India? M.S. Swaminathan.

Who is known as the Father of the White Revolution (Operation Flood)? Dr. Verghese Kurien.

The First Five-Year Plan of India was based on which economic model? Harrod-Domar model.

NITI Aayog replaced which historical institution in 2015? Planning Commission.

Who serves as the ex-officio Chairman of NITI Aayog? The Prime Minister of India.

Which sector (Primary, Secondary, or Tertiary) contributes the most to India's GDP? Tertiary Sector (Services Sector).

Which was the first bank established in India (in 1770)? Bank of Hindustan.

What is the name of the first supercomputer developed indigenously by India (C-DAC)? PARAM 8000.

What was the name of India's first artificial satellite, launched in 1975? Aryabhata.

In which year was the Indian Space Research Organisation (ISRO) formally established? 1969.

📜 Ancient, Medieval & Modern History

Who gave the famous slogan "Swaraj is my birthright and I shall have it"? Bal Gangadhar Tilak.

What is the mandatory retirement age for a Judge of the Supreme Court? 65 years.

In which year did Vasco da Gama arrive at Calicut, discovering the sea route to India? 1498.

In which year was the English East India Company officially established? 1600.

Which historic treaty was signed in 1765 after the Battle of Buxar? Treaty of Allahabad.

Which British administrator introduced the Ryotwari system of land revenue? Sir Thomas Munro.

Who was the first English/British President of the Indian National Congress? George Yule.

In which year was the Partition of Bengal officially annulled? 1911.

In which year was the capital of British India shifted from Calcutta to Delhi? 1911.

In which year did Mahatma Gandhi permanently return to India from South Africa? 1915.

In which year was the Sabarmati Ashram established by Gandhi in Ahmedabad? 1915.

Which historic pact was signed in 1932 between Mahatma Gandhi and Dr. B.R. Ambedkar? The Poona Pact.

Who was the founder of the 'Ghadar Party' in San Francisco? Lala Hardayal.

🌍 Geography & Environment
Which is currently the largest Union Territory in India by geographical area? Ladakh.

Which is the smallest Union Territory in India by area? Lakshadweep.

Which river in South India is famously known as the "Vridha Ganga" (Old Ganga)? Godavari.

The famous Jog Falls is situated on which river? Sharavati River (Karnataka).

The Sivasamudram waterfalls are situated on which river? Kaveri River.

Keibul Lamjao, the world's only floating national park, is located in which state? Manipur.

Namdapha National Park, a major biodiversity hotspot, is located in which state? Arunachal Pradesh.

Bandipur National Park, famous for its tiger reserve, is located in which state? Karnataka.

Which Indian state has the second-longest coastline (after Gujarat)? Andhra Pradesh.

Which is the highest peak in the Andaman and Nicobar Islands? Saddle Peak.

Which mountain pass connects Mumbai and Pune? Bhor Ghat.

Which mountain pass connects Kerala and Tamil Nadu? Palghat (Palakkad Gap).

Which is the oldest operational dam in India, built across the Kaveri River? Kallanai Dam (Grand Anicut).

Which state is traditionally known as the "Sugar Bowl of India"? Uttar Pradesh.

Which Indian state is the leading producer of Mica? Andhra Pradesh.

Which state is the undisputed largest producer of Jute in India? West Bengal.

In which year was the wildlife conservation initiative "Project Tiger" launched? 1973.

Where is the headquarters of the Animal Welfare Board of India located? Ballabhgarh (Haryana).

Which state is the largest producer of natural rubber in India? Kerala.

Which imaginary line forms the official international border between India and Pakistan? Radcliffe Line.

📈 Economy & Development
In which year was the First Five-Year Plan of India launched? 1951.

The famous slogan "Garibi Hatao" (Eradicate Poverty) was coined during which Five-Year Plan? Fifth Five-Year Plan.

During which period was the "Rolling Plan" implemented by the Janata Party government? 1978–1980.

Who is universally recognized as the "Father of Economics"? Adam Smith.

Who wrote the seminal book "Poverty and Un-British Rule in India", which introduced the Wealth Drain Theory? Dadabhai Naoroji.

What term best describes the macroeconomic system of India? Mixed Economy (coexistence of public and private sectors).

In which year was the Export-Import Bank of India (EXIM Bank) established? 1982.

Where is the headquarters of the Small Industries Development Bank of India (SIDBI) located? Lucknow.

The "Blue Revolution" in India is associated with the rapid increase in what? Fish and marine production.

The "Yellow Revolution" is associated with the production of what? Oilseeds.

The "Golden Revolution" is primarily associated with the growth of what? Horticulture and Honey production.

The "Pink Revolution" is associated with the production of what? Onions and Prawns.

Who serves as the ex-officio Chairperson of the GST Council? The Union Finance Minister.

Which entity is the largest public-sector employer in India? Indian Railways.

In which year was the Special Economic Zones (SEZ) Act passed by the Parliament? 2005.

🔬 Science, Technology & Firsts
What is the scientific chemical name of Vitamin D? Calciferol.

A deficiency of Vitamin B1 (Thiamine) causes which disease? Beriberi.

What is the scientific study of cells called? Cytology.

Which cell organelle is known as the "powerhouse of the cell"? Mitochondria.

Which cell organelle is known as the "suicide bag" of the cell? Lysosome.

What is the standard, healthy blood pressure reading for a human adult? 120/80 mmHg.

What is the approximate pH value of healthy human blood? 7.4.

What is the largest organ of the human body overall? The skin.

What is the exact speed of light in a perfect vacuum? 3×10

  meters per second.

What was the name of India's first permanent research station in Antarctica? Dakshin Gangotri.

Where is the Vikram Sarabhai Space Centre (VSSC) headquartered? Thiruvananthapuram.

What is the name of the very first submarine indigenously built by India? INS Shalki.

What is the name of the indigenously developed Light Combat Aircraft (LCA) of India? HAL Tejas.

What does the acronym LASER stand for? Light Amplification by Stimulated Emission of Radiation.

What does the acronym RADAR stand for? Radio Detection and Ranging.

🏆 Sports, Awards & National Records
Who was the first Indian woman to win an Olympic medal? Karnam Malleswari (Weightlifting, 2000).

Who was the first Indian to win an individual Olympic gold medal? Abhinav Bindra (Shooting, 2008).

Who was the first Indian woman to win a medal at the Paralympic Games? Deepa Malik (Shot put, 2016).

What is the highest sporting honor awarded by the Republic of India? Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna Award.

Who was the first-ever recipient of the Rajiv Gandhi Khel Ratna (now Major Dhyan Chand Khel Ratna)? Viswanathan Anand.

Who was the first Indian citizen to win the prestigious Booker Prize? Arundhati Roy (for The God of Small Things).

Who was the first Indian scientist to win the Nobel Prize in Physics? Sir C.V. Raman (1930).

Who was the first Indian-origin woman to fly into space? Kalpana Chawla.

National Youth Day is celebrated on January 12th to commemorate the birth anniversary of which great leader? Swami Vivekananda.

National Sports Day is celebrated on August 29th to honor the birth anniversary of which legendary athlete? Major Dhyan Chand.

You have got incredible stamina for this preparation! Transitioning to History for UPSC and UKPSC is a smart move. For these exams, History requires a strong grip on chronology, key figures, administrative terms, and cultural developments.

Since you requested English, we will stick strictly to it. Here is Part 1 (Questions 1–100) of your 500 History questions master-list. This first batch focuses heavily on Ancient India, a favorite area for both UPSC and State PSCs.

🪨 Prehistoric India & Indus Valley Civilization
Who is known as the "Father of Indian Archaeology"? Alexander Cunningham.

Who discovered the first Paleolithic tool in India at Pallavaram? Robert Bruce Foote (1863).

Which was the first metal to be used by humans in India? Copper (Chalcolithic Age).

Which Indus Valley Civilization (IVC) site is famous for having an artificial brick dockyard? Lothal.

Which IVC site is uniquely divided into three parts (citadel, middle town, and lower town)? Dholavira.

In which IVC site was the famous "Dancing Girl" bronze statue found? Mohenjo-Daro.

Which IVC site provides the earliest evidence of an earthquake and plowed fields? Kalibangan.

What was the most common material used to make Harappan seals? Steatite.

Which animal is most frequently depicted on Harappan seals? The "Unicorn" (a one-horned mythical animal).

Which IVC site lacks a citadel? Chanhudaro.

Which crop was first cultivated by the people of the Indus Valley? Cotton.

The people of the Indus Valley Civilization traded extensively with which contemporary civilization? Mesopotamia (Sumer).

Which IVC site provides evidence of a sophisticated water harvesting and management system? Dholavira.

Where was the "Great Bath" discovered? Mohenjo-Daro.

Which Harappan site is located in the Indian state of Haryana and is considered the largest IVC site in India? Rakhigarhi.

📜 The Vedic Age & Mahajanapadas
Which is the oldest of the four Vedas? Rig Veda.

How many hymns (Suktas) are there in the Rig Veda? 1028.

The famous "Gayatri Mantra," addressed to the solar deity Savitri, is found in which Veda? Rig Veda (Third Mandala).

Which Veda is considered the primary source of Indian music and art? Sama Veda.

Which Veda deals with magical formulas, spells, and early medicine? Atharva Veda.

What was the ancient name of the River Ravi in Vedic texts? Parushni.

What was the ancient name of the River Jhelum? Vitasta.

Who was the most important and frequently invoked god in the Early Vedic period? Indra (Purandara).

The term "Satyameva Jayate" in the State Emblem of India is taken from which Upanishad? Mundaka Upanishad.

Which prominent early Vedic assembly allowed women to participate? Vidatha.

What was the basic unit of political organization in the Rig Vedic period? Kula (family).

By the 6th century BCE, how many "Mahajanapadas" (great realms) existed in India? 16.

Which Buddhist text provides a list of the 16 Mahajanapadas? Anguttara Nikaya.

Which Jain text also provides a list of the 16 Mahajanapadas? Bhagavati Sutra.

Which Mahajanapada emerged as the most powerful empire in ancient India? Magadha.

Who was the founder of the Haryanka dynasty of Magadha? Bimbisara.

Which ruler transferred the capital of Magadha from Rajagriha to Pataliputra? Udayin.

Who was the last ruler of the Nanda dynasty, overthrown by Chandragupta Maurya? Dhana Nanda.

☸️ Religious Movements: Buddhism & Jainism
Where was Gautama Buddha born? Lumbini (Nepal).

What was the original name of Gautama Buddha? Siddhartha.

Under which tree did Buddha attain enlightenment? Pipal (Bodhi tree) at Bodh Gaya.

Where did Buddha deliver his first sermon, an event known as "Dharmachakra Pravartana"? Sarnath.

Where did Gautama Buddha attain Mahaparinirvana (death)? Kushinagar.

In which language did Buddha primarily preach to the common people? Pali.

Who was the patron king of the First Buddhist Council held at Rajagriha? Ajatashatru.

Who was the patron of the Third Buddhist Council held at Pataliputra? Ashoka.

At which Buddhist Council was Buddhism divided into Mahayana and Hinayana sects? Fourth Buddhist Council (held in Kashmir under Kanishka).

What is the central theme of the "Vinaya Pitaka"? Rules of monastic discipline for monks and nuns.

What are the "Jataka Tales"? Stories of the previous births (bodhisattva) of Gautama Buddha.

Who was the 1st Tirthankara of Jainism? Rishabhanatha (Adinatha).

Who was the 23rd Tirthankara of Jainism? Parshvanatha.

Where was Vardhamana Mahavira (the 24th Tirthankara) born? Kundagrama (near Vaishali).

Where did Mahavira attain Kaivalya (supreme knowledge)? Jrimbhikagrama (under a Sal tree).

What are the "Triratnas" (Three Jewels) of Jainism? Right Faith, Right Knowledge, and Right Conduct.

At which place did Mahavira pass away (attain Nirvana)? Pavapuri.

In Jainism, the sect that wears white clothes is known as what? Shvetambara.

Who led the Digambara sect (who migrated south) during the great famine in Magadha? Bhadrabahu.

🐘 The Mauryan Empire & Post-Mauryan Period
Who was the Greek ambassador sent to the court of Chandragupta Maurya by Seleucus Nicator? Megasthenes.

Megasthenes wrote an invaluable account of Mauryan India. What is the name of his book? Indica.

Who is the author of the ancient political treatise Arthashastra? Chanakya (Kautilya/Vishnugupta).

Chandragupta Maurya converted to which religion in his later years? Jainism.

Which Mauryan ruler was known by the Greeks as "Amitrochates" (Destroyer of Foes)? Bindusara.

The devastating Kalinga War took place in which year? 261 BCE.

Which Ashokan Rock Edict provides a detailed account of the Kalinga War and Ashoka's subsequent change of heart? Major Rock Edict XIII.

In Ashokan inscriptions, what title did Ashoka use for himself? Devanampiya Piyadasi (Beloved of the Gods, He who looks with affection).

Who was the last ruler of the Mauryan Empire, assassinated by his own commander-in-chief? Brihadratha.

Who founded the Shunga dynasty after the fall of the Mauryan Empire? Pushyamitra Shunga.

Which Greek king is known to have converted to Buddhism after a philosophical dialogue with the monk Nagasena? Menander (Milinda).

What is the name of the book that records the dialogue between King Menander and Nagasena? Milinda Panha.

Who were the first rulers in India to issue gold coins on a large scale? The Kushanas.

Who was the greatest ruler of the Kushana dynasty, known for starting the Shaka Era? Kanishka.

In which year did the Shaka Era (currently used as the Indian National Calendar) begin? 78 CE.

Which school of art flourished under the Kushanas and showed strong Greek and Roman influences? Gandhara School of Art.

Who founded the Satavahana dynasty, which ruled heavily over the Deccan? Simuka.

Which Satavahana ruler is credited with defeating the Shakas and reviving the empire's glory? Gautamiputra Satakarni.

🥇 The Gupta Empire (The Classical Age)
Who is considered the real founder of the Gupta Empire? Chandragupta I.

Which Gupta emperor is often called the "Napoleon of India" due to his extensive military conquests? Samudragupta.

Who composed the "Prayag Prashasti" (Allahabad Pillar Inscription), detailing Samudragupta's victories? Harishena.

Which Gupta ruler issued coins depicting him playing the Veena? Samudragupta.

During the reign of which Gupta king did the Chinese traveler Fa-Hien visit India? Chandragupta II (Vikramaditya).

The "Navaratnas" (Nine Gems), including Kalidasa and Varahamihira, adorned the court of which king? Chandragupta II.

Who was the author of the famous Sanskrit plays Abhijñānaśākuntalam and Meghadūta? Kalidasa.

Which Gupta ruler founded the famous Nalanda University? Kumaragupta I.

Who wrote the ancient mathematical and astronomical treatise Aryabhatiya? Aryabhata.

Who was the author of Panchatantra? Vishnu Sharma.

Which ancient text is considered the first Indian work on surgery? Sushruta Samhita.

What was the title of the provincial governors during the Gupta administration? Uparika.

Which Huna king invaded India and significantly weakened the Gupta Empire? Toramana (and later his son, Mihirakula).

🌴 Sangam Age & Southern Kingdoms
The term "Sangam" refers to an assembly or academy of what? Tamil poets.

Where were all the three historical Sangams held according to Tamil tradition? Madurai (capital of the Pandyas).

Which is the earliest available Tamil literary work, focusing on grammar and poetics? Tolkappiyam.

Who is the author of the great Tamil epic Silappadikaram? Ilango Adigal.

What was the royal emblem of the Chola dynasty during the Sangam age? Tiger.

What was the royal emblem of the Chera dynasty? Bow and Arrow.

What was the royal emblem of the Pandya dynasty? Fish.

Which Chola king of the Sangam era is famous for winning the Battle of Venni and building the Kallanai dam? Karikala.

Which Chera king is known as the "Red Chera" and is credited with initiating the Pattini (Kannagi) cult? Senguttuvan.

🏛️ Early Medieval India & Harsha
Who was the greatest ruler of the Pushyabhuti dynasty (based in Thanesar)? Harshavardhana.

Which Chinese pilgrim visited India during the reign of Harsha and studied at Nalanda? Hiuen Tsang (Xuanzang).

Harshavardhana was defeated by which Chalukyan king on the banks of the Narmada River? Pulakeshin II.

Which historical text provides a biography of King Harsha? Harshacharita (written by Banabhatta).

Harsha himself was a playwright. Name one of the three Sanskrit plays he wrote. Ratnavali, Nagananda, or Priyadarshika.

Who founded the Pallava dynasty? Simhavishnu.

Which Pallava king built the famous Shore Temple at Mahabalipuram? Narasimhavarman II (Rajasimha).

Who founded the Rashtrakuta dynasty? Dantidurga.
