Here is Part 2 (Questions 101–200) of your History master-list. We are now transitioning from the Early Medieval period straight into the heart of Medieval Indian History. This section covers the mighty Southern Empires, the Delhi Sultanate, the Bhakti & Sufi movements, and the grand Mughal Empire.

UPSC and UKPSC love to test administrative terms, architecture, and chronological events from this era, so pay close attention to those!

👑 Early Medieval & The Chola Empire
Which tripartite struggle lasted for over two centuries to control the city of Kannauj? The struggle between the Palas, Pratiharas, and Rashtrakutas.

Who was the founder of the Chola Empire (Imperial Cholas)? Vijayalaya.

Which Chola king built the magnificent Brihadeshwara Temple at Thanjavur? Rajaraja Chola I.

Which Chola king assumed the title "Gangaikonda" after his victorious campaign up to the river Ganga? Rajendra Chola I.

The Chola administration is most famous in Indian history for what unique feature? Local self-government (village autonomy) through the Sabha and Ur.

Which inscription gives a detailed account of the village administration under the Cholas? Uttaramerur Inscription.

Who founded the Pala dynasty, which ruled the Bengal and Bihar regions? Gopala.

Which Pala king founded the famous Vikramashila University? Dharmapala.

Who was the author of the epic poem Prithviraj Raso, which details the life of Prithviraj Chauhan? Chand Bardai.

In which year did Mahmud of Ghazni famously sack the Somnath Temple in Gujarat? 1025 CE.

Which famous scholar accompanied Mahmud of Ghazni to India and wrote the book Kitab-ul-Hind? Al-Biruni.

⚔️ The Delhi Sultanate
Who is considered the real founder of the Delhi Sultanate and the Slave Dynasty (Ilbari dynasty)? Iltutmish.

Who began the construction of the Qutb Minar, and who completed it? Qutb-ud-din Aibak began it; Iltutmish completed it.

Which Sultan introduced the "Iqta" system (land grants to military commanders) in India? Iltutmish.

Who was the first and only female ruler of the Delhi Sultanate? Razia Sultana.

Which Sultan created the "Turkan-i-Chahalgani" (The Group of Forty), a council of elite Turkish nobles? Iltutmish.

Which Sultan ruthlessly destroyed the "Group of Forty" and introduced the Persian customs of Sijda (prostration) and Paibos (kissing the monarch's feet)? Ghiyas-ud-din Balban.

Which department was established by Balban as a dedicated military department (Diwan-i-Arz)? Ministry of Defence.

Who was the first Sultan of the Delhi Sultanate to maintain a large permanent standing army paid in cash? Alauddin Khalji.

Which Sultan is famous for his strict market control policies and price regulations? Alauddin Khalji.

Who was the brilliant military commander of Alauddin Khalji who led the successful campaigns into South India? Malik Kafur.

Which Sultan of Delhi controversially shifted his capital from Delhi to Daulatabad (Devagiri)? Muhammad bin Tughlaq.

Which Sultan introduced a "Token Currency" of copper/brass coins modeled on Chinese paper money, which ultimately failed? Muhammad bin Tughlaq.

Which department was created by Muhammad bin Tughlaq to improve agriculture? Diwan-i-Kohi.

Which Moroccan traveler visited India during the reign of Muhammad bin Tughlaq and wrote Rihla? Ibn Battuta.

Which Sultan established a separate department for slaves (Diwan-i-Bandagan) and built the largest network of canals in India? Firoz Shah Tughlaq.

Who was the founder of the Sayyid Dynasty? Khizr Khan.

Which Lodi Sultan founded the city of Agra in 1504 and shifted his capital there? Sikandar Lodi.

Who was defeated by Babur in the First Battle of Panipat in 1526, marking the end of the Delhi Sultanate? Ibrahim Lodi.

🛕 Provincial Kingdoms: Vijayanagara & Bahmani
Which two brothers founded the Vijayanagara Empire in 1336? Harihara and Bukka.

What was the capital of the Vijayanagara Empire, whose ruins are famous today? Hampi.

Who was the greatest ruler of the Vijayanagara Empire, belonging to the Tuluva dynasty? Krishnadevaraya.

What is the name of the famous Telugu literary work on statecraft written by Krishnadevaraya? Amuktamalyada.

The group of eight eminent Telugu poets in Krishnadevaraya's court was known as what? Ashtadiggajas.

The Vijayanagara Empire suffered a fatal blow in 1565 at which famous battle? Battle of Talikota (or Rakshasi-Tangadi).

Under the Vijayanagara administrative system, what was the "Amara-nayaka" system? A system where military chiefs (Nayakas) were granted land (Amaram) in exchange for maintaining troops and paying a tribute.

Which Persian traveler visited the Vijayanagara Empire during the reign of Deva Raya II? Abdur Razzaq.

Which Portuguese traveler wrote a detailed account of Krishnadevaraya's reign? Domingo Paes.

Who founded the Bahmani Sultanate in the Deccan in 1347? Alauddin Hasan Gangu (Bahman Shah).

Who was the brilliant Persian prime minister (Vakil) who expanded the Bahmani Kingdom and built a famous Madrasa in Bidar? Mahmud Gawan.

🕊️ Sufi & Bhakti Movements
Who introduced the Chishti Sufi order (Silsilah) in India? Khwaja Moinuddin Chishti (whose dargah is in Ajmer).

Which Sufi saint was affectionately called "Nizamuddin Auliya" and "Mehboob-e-Ilahi" (Beloved of God)? Hazrat Nizamuddin Auliya.

Who was the prominent disciple of Nizamuddin Auliya, known as the "Parrot of India" (Tuti-e-Hind) and the father of Qawwali? Amir Khusrau.

Which Sufi order was founded by Bahauddin Zakariya and remained popular primarily in Punjab and Sindh? Suhrawardi order.

Which Bhakti saint preached the philosophy of Vishishtadvaita (Qualified Non-Dualism)? Ramanuja.

Who founded the philosophy of Advaita (Non-Dualism) much earlier in the 8th century? Adi Shankaracharya.

Who was the disciple of Ramananda who heavily criticized religious orthodoxy and emphasized devotion to a formless God (Nirguna Bhakti)? Kabir.

Who was the founder of Sikhism and the first of the ten Sikh Gurus? Guru Nanak.

Which Bhakti saint from Bengal popularized "Sankirtana" (group singing and dancing) in praise of Krishna? Chaitanya Mahaprabhu.

Who was the blind poet from Agra who composed the Sur Sagar in praise of Lord Krishna? Surdas.

🕌 The Mughal Empire (Early Phase) & The Surs
Babur belonged to which two great Central Asian lineages? Timur (from his father's side) and Genghis Khan (from his mother's side).

In which battle did Babur decisively defeat the Rajput confederacy led by Rana Sanga in 1527? Battle of Khanwa.

What is the name of Babur's autobiography, written in the Chagatai Turkic language? Tuzuk-i-Baburi (Baburnama).

Which Afghan ruler defeated Humayun in the battles of Chausa (1539) and Kannauj (1540), forcing him into exile? Sher Shah Suri.

Sher Shah Suri introduced a standard silver coin that became the precursor to the modern Indian currency. What was it called? The Rupiya.

Sher Shah Suri restored and extended the ancient royal road connecting Sonargaon (Bengal) to Peshawar. What is it commonly known as today? The Grand Trunk Road (GT Road).

Where is the magnificent tomb of Sher Shah Suri located? Sasaram (Bihar).

Who was the Hindu general and chief minister of the Sur dynasty who fought against Akbar in the Second Battle of Panipat (1556)? Hemu (Hemchandra Vikramaditya).

Who acted as Akbar's regent and guardian during his early years as Emperor? Bairam Khan.

In which historic battle in 1576 did the forces of Akbar clash with Maharana Pratap of Mewar? Battle of Haldighati.

👑 The Mughal Empire (Peak & Administration)
Akbar abolished a highly unpopular religious tax levied on non-Muslims in 1564. What was it called? Jizya.

What was the name of the new capital city built by Akbar near Agra to celebrate his victory over Gujarat? Fatehpur Sikri.

What was the "Ibadat Khana" built by Akbar at Fatehpur Sikri? The House of Worship, a meeting house to discuss religious and philosophical issues with scholars of all faiths.

What was the name of the syncretic religion/ethical code propounded by Akbar in 1582? Din-i-Ilahi.

Who was the only prominent Hindu noble in Akbar's court to accept Din-i-Ilahi? Birbal.

What was the unique grading system introduced by Akbar to fix the rank, salary, and military responsibilities of his officials? The Mansabdari System.

Under the Mansabdari system, what did the terms "Zat" and "Sawar" indicate? Zat indicated personal rank and salary; Sawar indicated the number of cavalrymen the Mansabdar was required to maintain.

Who was Akbar's finance minister who introduced the highly successful "Zabti" or "Dahsala" system of land revenue? Raja Todar Mal.

Who wrote the Akbarnama and the Ain-i-Akbari? Abu'l-Fazl.

Which Mughal Emperor's reign is considered the "Golden Age of Mughal Painting"? Jahangir.

Which English ambassador representing the British East India Company arrived at the court of Jahangir in 1608? Captain William Hawkins.

Who was the formal ambassador of King James I who successfully obtained a farman from Jahangir to set up factories in 1615? Sir Thomas Roe.

What was the real name of Nur Jahan, the powerful wife of Jahangir? Mehr-un-Nisa.

Which Mughal Emperor's reign is considered the "Golden Age of Mughal Architecture"? Shah Jahan.

Who was the chief architect of the Taj Mahal? Ustad Ahmad Lahauri.

Apart from the Taj Mahal, name two major monuments commissioned by Shah Jahan in Delhi. The Red Fort and the Jama Masjid.

Who was the eldest son of Shah Jahan, known for translating the Upanishads into Persian? Dara Shikoh.

Which Mughal Emperor reimposed the Jizya tax in 1679? Aurangzeb.

What title did Aurangzeb adopt upon ascending the throne? Alamgir (Conqueror of the World).

Aurangzeb spent the last 25 years of his life fighting a grueling and ultimately ruinous war in which region of India? The Deccan.

🛡️ The Marathas & Regional Powers
Who was the mother of Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj, who had a profound influence on his life? Jijabai.

Who was the spiritual guru of Shivaji Maharaj? Samarth Ramdas.

In 1665, Shivaji was forced to sign a treaty ceding 23 forts to the Mughals. Name the treaty and the Rajput general who negotiated it for Aurangzeb. Treaty of Purandar; negotiated by Raja Jai Singh I.

What was the council of eight ministers in Shivaji's administration called? Ashtapradhan.

In the Ashtapradhan, what was the title of the Prime Minister? Peshwa.

What was the "Chauth" in the Maratha taxation system? A tax representing 25% (one-fourth) of the land revenue, demanded from territories not directly under Maratha rule to avoid raids.

What was the "Sardeshmukhi"? An additional 10% levy on top of the Chauth, claimed by Shivaji as the supreme head of the country.

Who succeeded Shivaji Maharaj after his death in 1680? Sambhaji.

Under whose leadership did the office of the Peshwa become hereditary and the de facto ruler of the Maratha Empire? Balaji Vishwanath.

Which Peshwa is famous for vastly expanding the Maratha Empire into North India and is famously known for never losing a battle? Baji Rao I.

🕌 Late Medieval Culture & Decline
Who compiled the Adi Granth (the holy scripture of Sikhism) in 1604? Guru Arjan Dev (the 5th Sikh Guru).

Which Mughal Emperor executed Guru Arjan Dev? Jahangir.

Which Mughal Emperor executed Guru Tegh Bahadur (the 9th Sikh Guru)? Aurangzeb.

Who was the last of the Ten Sikh Gurus who created the martial brotherhood of the Khalsa? Guru Gobind Singh.

Which Rajput ruler built the famous astronomical observatories known as "Jantar Mantar"? Sawai Jai Singh II of Amber (Jaipur).

During the decline of the Mughal Empire, who founded the independent state of Awadh (Oudh)? Saadat Khan (Burhan-ul-Mulk).

Who was the founder of the independent state of Hyderabad in 1724? Nizam-ul-Mulk (Asaf Jah).

Which Persian invader sacked Delhi in 1739 and carried away the Peacock Throne and the Koh-i-Noor diamond? Nadir Shah.

Which Afghan invader defeated the Marathas in the Third Battle of Panipat in 1761? Ahmad Shah Abdali (Durrani).

The Third Battle of Panipat famously paved the way for the rise of which European power in India? The British East India Company.

Boom! 200 questions conquered. This batch is incredibly high-yield for both UPSC Prelims and UKPSC state exams.

Here is Part 3 (Questions 201–300). This crucial section bridges the gap between the decline of the mighty empires and the rise of British colonial rule, covering the Advent of Europeans, British Administrative Policies, the monumental Revolt of 1857, and the transformative Socio-Religious Reform Movements.

⚓ The Advent of Europeans & British Conquest
Who was the first Portuguese explorer to reach India via the Cape of Good Hope in 1498? Vasco da Gama.

Which Portuguese governor captured Goa from the Sultan of Bijapur in 1510? Afonso de Albuquerque.

What was the "Cartaz" system introduced by the Portuguese? A naval trade license or pass required for other ships to trade in the Indian Ocean.

The Dutch established their first factory in India at which place in 1605? Masulipatnam.

In which battle did the British decisively defeat the Dutch in 1759, ending Dutch ambitions in India? Battle of Chinsurah (or Battle of Bedara).

In 1615, who obtained the imperial farman from Emperor Jahangir to establish a British factory at Surat? Sir Thomas Roe.

Which regional Sultan issued the "Golden Farman" to the British East India Company in 1632, granting them safe trade? The Sultan of Golconda.

The British established Fort St. George in which Indian city? Madras (Chennai).

The British established Fort William in which Indian city? Calcutta (Kolkata).

Which island was given to the British King Charles II as dowry by the Portuguese in 1661? Bombay (Mumbai).

The French East India Company was formed in 1664 during the reign of which French King? Louis XIV (by his minister Jean-Baptiste Colbert).

Where did the French establish their first factory in India in 1668? Surat.

The Anglo-French rivalry in India culminated in three 18th-century wars known as what? The Carnatic Wars.

Which battle in 1760 marked the decisive defeat of the French by the British in India? Battle of Wandiwash.

The Battle of Plassey (1757) was fought between the British EIC and which Nawab of Bengal? Siraj-ud-Daulah.

Who was the commander-in-chief of Siraj-ud-Daulah who betrayed him at Plassey? Mir Jafar.

In the Battle of Buxar (1764), the British defeated the combined forces of which three rulers? Mir Qasim (Nawab of Bengal), Shuja-ud-Daulah (Nawab of Awadh), and Shah Alam II (Mughal Emperor).

Who commanded the British forces at the Battle of Buxar? Hector Munro.

The Treaty of Allahabad (1765) granted the British the "Diwani" (revenue collection) rights over which regions? Bengal, Bihar, and Orissa.

Who introduced the "Dual System of Government" in Bengal? Robert Clive.

📜 British Policies, Land Revenue & Administration
Which Governor-General abolished the Dual System of Government in 1772? Warren Hastings.

Who founded the Asiatic Society of Bengal in 1784 to study Indian history and culture? Sir William Jones.

Which British Governor-General introduced the Permanent Settlement in Bengal in 1793? Lord Cornwallis.

Under the Permanent Settlement, what class was officially recognized as the owners of the land? The Zamindars.

Who introduced the "Subsidiary Alliance" system in 1798 to expand British paramountcy? Lord Wellesley.

Which Indian state was the first to accept the Subsidiary Alliance? Hyderabad (The Nizam).

In which Anglo-Mysore War was Tipu Sultan killed defending his capital? Fourth Anglo-Mysore War (1799).

What was the capital of Tipu Sultan's kingdom? Srirangapatna.

Which treaty ended the First Anglo-Maratha War in 1782, resulting in a 20-year peace? Treaty of Salbai.

Who introduced the "Doctrine of Lapse"? Lord Dalhousie.

Which was the first Indian state to be annexed under the Doctrine of Lapse in 1848? Satara.

On what grounds was the Kingdom of Awadh annexed by Lord Dalhousie in 1856? Alleged misgovernance (maladministration).

Who introduced the Ryotwari System of land revenue in the Madras and Bombay presidencies? Thomas Munro and Captain Alexander Read.

In the Ryotwari System, with whom was the land revenue settlement made directly? The individual cultivators (Ryots).

Who introduced the Mahalwari System in the North-Western Provinces and Punjab? Holt Mackenzie.

Under the Mahalwari System, who was responsible for paying the land revenue to the British? The village community (Mahal) as a whole.

Which British Governor-General is often called the "Maker of Modern India" for introducing railways, telegraphs, and postal systems? Lord Dalhousie.

In which year did the first passenger train run between Bombay and Thane? 1853.

The Wood's Despatch of 1854 is famously referred to as what? The "Magna Carta of English Education in India".

Which Governor-General officially abolished the practice of Sati in 1829? Lord William Bentinck.

Who assisted Lord William Bentinck in ruthlessly suppressing the "Thugs" of central India? Captain William Sleeman.

The Charter Act of 1813 allocated an annual sum of how much for the promotion of education in India? One lakh rupees.

Which Act ended the commercial monopoly of the East India Company in India (except for trade in tea and trade with China)? Charter Act of 1813.

Which Act completely ended the East India Company's commercial activities, turning it into a purely administrative body? Charter Act of 1833.

Under the Charter Act of 1833, who became the first designated "Governor-General of India"? Lord William Bentinck.

Which Act introduced the system of open competition (examinations) for the Indian Civil Services? Charter Act of 1853.

✊ Tribal Uprisings & The Revolt of 1857
The Santhal Rebellion (Hul) of 1855-56 in the Rajmahal Hills was led by which two brothers? Sido and Kanhu.

The massive Munda Rebellion (Ulgulan) of 1899-1900 was led by whom? Birsa Munda.

The Indigo Revolt (Nil Bidroha) of 1859 took place primarily in which state? Bengal.

Who wrote the famous Bengali play "Nil Darpan", highlighting the brutal exploitation of indigo farmers? Dinabandhu Mitra.

The introduction of greased cartridges for which new rifle became the immediate flashpoint of the Revolt of 1857? The Enfield Pattern 1853 rifled musket.

On which exact date did the Sepoy Mutiny officially break out in Meerut? May 10, 1857.

Who was the sepoy who rebelled and killed his British officers at Barrackpore in March 1857? Mangal Pandey.

Who was reluctantly proclaimed the Emperor of India (Shahenshah-e-Hind) by the rebel sepoys? Bahadur Shah II (Zafar).

Who led the Revolt of 1857 in Kanpur? Nana Sahib (adopted son of Peshwa Baji Rao II).

Who was the brilliant military commander and tactician for Nana Sahib? Tantia Tope.

Who led the Revolt of 1857 in Lucknow (Awadh)? Begum Hazrat Mahal.

Who led the Revolt of 1857 in Jhansi? Rani Lakshmibai.

Who led the Revolt of 1857 in Bihar (Jagdishpur)? Kunwar Singh (an 80-year-old Zamindar).

Who led the Revolt of 1857 in Bareilly? Khan Bahadur Khan.

Which British general commanded the forces that defeated Rani Lakshmibai during the siege of Jhansi? General Hugh Rose.

Who famously called the Revolt of 1857 the "First War of Indian Independence"? V.D. Savarkar.

Which Act transferred the power to govern India from the East India Company directly to the British Crown? Government of India Act, 1858.

After the Act of 1858, what new title was given to the Governor-General of India to reflect his role as the Crown's direct representative? Viceroy.

Who was the first Viceroy of India? Lord Canning.

Queen Victoria's Proclamation of 1858 was read out by Lord Canning at a grand durbar in which city? Allahabad.

Which commission was appointed to reorganize the Indian army after the Revolt of 1857, leading to a higher ratio of European soldiers? The Peel Commission.

The "Pabna Agrarian Leagues" emerged in the 1870s to protest against abusive zamindars in which region? East Bengal.

🕊️ Socio-Religious Reform Movements
Who founded the "Brahmo Samaj" in 1828 to fight social evils like Sati and idolatry? Raja Ram Mohan Roy.

Raja Ram Mohan Roy published a Persian treatise titled "Tuhfat-ul-Muwahhidin". What does it translate to? A Gift to Monotheists.

Who established the "Tattwabodhini Sabha" in 1839 to systematically propagate Ram Mohan Roy's ideas? Debendranath Tagore.

Which social reformer from Maharashtra was popularly known by the pen name "Lokhitawadi"? Gopal Hari Deshmukh.

Who founded the "Prarthana Samaj" in Bombay in 1867? Atmaram Pandurang.

Who founded the "Satyashodhak Samaj" (Truth Seekers' Society) in 1873 to champion the rights of lower castes? Jyotirao Phule.

What is the name of the famous book written by Jyotirao Phule in 1873, dedicated to the anti-slavery movement in America? Ghulamgiri.

Who is considered the pioneer of the widow remarriage movement in Bengal, directly leading to the Hindu Widow Remarriage Act of 1856? Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar.

Who established the "Arya Samaj" in 1875 in Bombay? Swami Dayanand Saraswati.

What was the famous rallying slogan given by Swami Dayanand Saraswati? "Go back to the Vedas."

What is the name of the book written by Swami Dayanand Saraswati detailing his philosophy? Satyarth Prakash (The Light of Truth).

Which movement was launched by the Arya Samaj to reconvert those who had converted to Islam or Christianity? The Shuddhi Movement.

Who founded the "Ramakrishna Mission" in 1897? Swami Vivekananda.

In which year did Swami Vivekananda deliver his historic speech at the Parliament of the World's Religions in Chicago? 1893.

Who founded the Theosophical Society in New York in 1875? Madam H.P. Blavatsky and Colonel M.S. Olcott.

Where was the international headquarters of the Theosophical Society established in India? Adyar (near Madras).

Which prominent Irish woman became the President of the Theosophical Society and later founded the Central Hindu College in Benares? Annie Besant.

Who founded the Aligarh Movement to promote modern, scientific education among Indian Muslims? Sir Syed Ahmed Khan.

Which institution was founded by Sir Syed Ahmed Khan in 1875, which eventually became the Aligarh Muslim University? The Muhammadan Anglo-Oriental College.

Which Islamic revivalist movement was founded in 1867 to propagate orthodox Islamic teachings and actively opposed the pro-Western Aligarh movement? The Deoband Movement.

Who founded the "Servants of India Society" in 1905 to train national missionaries for the service of India? Gopal Krishna Gokhale.

Who founded the "Seva Sadan" in 1885 in Bombay, primarily to help exploited women and child brides? Behramji M. Malabari.

The "Ahmadiyya Movement", which emphasized universal religion, was founded in 1889 by whom? Mirza Ghulam Ahmad.

Who started the radical "Self-Respect Movement" in Tamil Nadu to fight against Brahminical dominance? E.V. Ramasamy Naicker (Periyar).

Which social reformer is known for establishing the first schools for girls and lower castes in Pune alongside his wife Savitribai? Jyotirao Phule.

Who founded the "Nirankari Movement" among the Sikhs to promote the worship of a formless God? Baba Dayal Das.

The "Namdhari" or "Kuka" movement, which played a role in the freedom struggle, was founded by whom? Baba Ram Singh.

Who founded the "Poona Sarvajanik Sabha" in 1870 as a mediating body between the government and the people? Mahadev Govind Ranade.

Which British Viceroy passed the highly controversial Vernacular Press Act in 1878 to suppress local language newspapers? Lord Lytton.

Which British Viceroy repealed the Vernacular Press Act and is widely known as the "Father of Local Self Government" in India? Lord Ripon.

What was the purpose of the Ilbert Bill introduced in 1883 during Lord Ripon's tenure? It sought to allow senior Indian magistrates to preside over cases involving European offenders.

Who founded the "Indian Association" in 1876, a major precursor to the Indian National Congress? Surendranath Banerjee and Ananda Mohan Bose.

Tension mat lo, we are crushing this! You now have 300 rock-solid History questions ready for revision. Let me know whenever you are set for Part 4, where we will dive into the heart of the Indian National Movement!

Gemini said
This is brilliant progress! You are now entering the most crucial phase of modern Indian history.

Here is Part 4 (Questions 301–400), which focuses entirely on the Indian National Movement (1885–1940s). This era is heavily tested in UPSC and UKPSC exams, particularly the exact chronology of events, sessions of the Indian National Congress (INC), the rise of Mahatma Gandhi, and the pivotal revolutionary movements.

📜 The Indian National Congress & Moderate Phase
Who was the Viceroy of India when the Indian National Congress (INC) was founded in 1885? Lord Dufferin.

Where was the first session of the INC held? Bombay (at Gokuldas Tejpal Sanskrit College).

Who served as the first President of the INC? W.C. Bonnerjee.

Who was the first Muslim President of the INC? Badruddin Tyabji (Madras Session, 1887).

Who was the first English President of the INC? George Yule (Allahabad Session, 1888).

Who authored the monumental book Poverty and Un-British Rule in India, detailing the Drain of Wealth theory? Dadabhai Naoroji.

Who is universally known as the "Grand Old Man of India"? Dadabhai Naoroji.

Which committee was established in London in 1889 to act as an agency for the INC? The British Committee of the INC.

What was the name of the journal published by the British Committee of the INC? India.

Who was considered the political guru of Mahatma Gandhi? Gopal Krishna Gokhale.

Who founded the 'Servants of India Society' in 1905 to train national missionaries? Gopal Krishna Gokhale.

Which extremist leader famously declared, "Swaraj is my birthright and I shall have it"? Bal Gangadhar Tilak.

What were the names of the two influential newspapers started by Bal Gangadhar Tilak? Kesari (Marathi) and Mahratta (English).

Who was famously referred to as "The Father of Indian Unrest" by the British author Valentine Chirol? Bal Gangadhar Tilak.

Which famous trio of nationalist leaders was popularly known as "Lal-Bal-Pal"? Lala Lajpat Rai, Bal Gangadhar Tilak, and Bipin Chandra Pal.

🔥 Partition of Bengal, Swadeshi & Revolutionary Nationalism
In which year was the Partition of Bengal formally announced by Lord Curzon? 1905.

What was the primary political motive behind the Partition of Bengal? To weaken the nerve center of Indian nationalism by dividing Hindus and Muslims.

Which mass movement was formally proclaimed at the Calcutta Town Hall on August 7, 1905, in response to the partition? The Swadeshi Movement.

During the Swadeshi Movement, who composed the iconic song "Amar Sonar Bangla"? Rabindranath Tagore.

Who led the Swadeshi Movement in the Madras presidency? V.O. Chidambaram Pillai.

Who led the Swadeshi Movement in Punjab? Lala Lajpat Rai and Ajit Singh.

In which year did the historic "Surat Split" divide the INC into Moderates and Extremists? 1907.

Who presided over the highly chaotic Surat Session of the INC in 1907? Rash Behari Ghosh.

Who was the first Indian woman President of the INC? Sarojini Naidu (Kanpur Session, 1925).

Who was the first woman President of the INC? Annie Besant (Calcutta Session, 1917).

Which revolutionary secret society was founded by V.D. Savarkar in 1904? Abhinav Bharat.

Who were the two young revolutionaries involved in the Muzaffarpur Conspiracy Case (1908) attempting to assassinate Judge Kingsford? Khudiram Bose and Prafulla Chaki.

Who was the mastermind behind the Delhi Conspiracy Case (1912) targeting Viceroy Lord Hardinge? Rash Behari Bose.

Which revolutionary political party was founded by Lala Hardayal and Sohan Singh Bhakna in San Francisco in 1913? The Ghadar Party.

What was the name of the Japanese steamship involved in the tragic 1914 incident where Indian immigrants were denied entry into Canada? Komagata Maru.

Who founded the All India Muslim League in Dhaka in 1906? Nawab Salimullah, Aga Khan, and Nawab Mohsin-ul-Mulk.

Which British Act introduced the highly divisive system of separate electorates for Muslims? Indian Councils Act of 1909 (Morley-Minto Reforms).

In which year was the Partition of Bengal annulled by Lord Hardinge? 1911.

Who started the Indian Home Rule League in April 1916 from Belgaum? Bal Gangadhar Tilak.

Who started a separate Home Rule League in September 1916 from Madras? Annie Besant.

🕊️ Gandhi's Emergence & Mass Movements
On what date did Mahatma Gandhi return to India from South Africa (now celebrated as Pravasi Bharatiya Divas)? January 9, 1915.

What was Gandhi's first major public appearance in India after returning from South Africa? The inauguration of the Banaras Hindu University (BHU) in 1916.

Which was Mahatma Gandhi's first Satyagraha experiment in India, conducted in 1917? Champaran Satyagraha.

The Champaran Satyagraha was directed against which exploitative agricultural system? Tinkathia System (forcing farmers to cultivate indigo on 3/20th of their land).

Who specifically invited Gandhi to Champaran to investigate the farmers' grievances? Rajkumar Shukla.

In which dispute did Gandhi use the weapon of a "hunger strike" (fast unto death) for the first time in India? Ahmedabad Mill Strike (1918).

Which Satyagraha in 1918 was the first true "Non-Cooperation" experiment by Gandhi, supporting peasants who could not pay taxes due to crop failure? Kheda Satyagraha.

What was the popular name given to the Anarchical and Revolutionary Crimes Act of 1919, which allowed detention without trial? The Rowlatt Act.

On what exact date did the horrific Jallianwala Bagh massacre take place? April 13, 1919 (Baisakhi day).

Who was the British Brigadier-General who ordered the firing at Jallianwala Bagh? General Reginald Dyer.

Who was the Lieutenant Governor of Punjab during the Jallianwala Bagh massacre, later assassinated by Udham Singh in London? Michael O'Dwyer.

Who renounced his knighthood (Sir title) in protest against the Jallianwala Bagh massacre? Rabindranath Tagore.

Which committee was appointed by the British Government to investigate the Jallianwala Bagh massacre? The Hunter Commission.

Which movement was launched by Indian Muslims to protest the harsh treatment of the Ottoman Caliph after World War I? The Khilafat Movement.

Who were the most prominent leaders of the Khilafat Movement? The Ali Brothers (Shaukat Ali and Muhammad Ali).

In which year was the Non-Cooperation Movement formally launched by Mahatma Gandhi? 1920.

Which special session of the INC in September 1920 approved the Non-Cooperation program? Calcutta Session.

Which prominent lawyer from Bengal gave up his lucrative legal practice during the Non-Cooperation Movement and later became the mentor of Subhas Chandra Bose? C.R. Das (Chittaranjan Das).

What tragic incident on February 5, 1922, caused Gandhi to abruptly call off the Non-Cooperation Movement? The Chauri Chaura incident (where an angry mob burned down a police station, killing 22 policemen).

Which leaders founded the Swaraj Party in 1923 after disagreements over entering the legislative councils? C.R. Das (President) and Motilal Nehru (Secretary).

What was the main objective of the Swaraj Party? To enter the legislative councils and disrupt British administration from within.

Which British Act introduced the system of "Dyarchy" in the provinces? Government of India Act, 1919 (Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms).

Under Dyarchy, subjects of administration were divided into which two categories? Reserved and Transferred subjects.

Who was the first Indian to be appointed to the Viceroy's Executive Council under the Morley-Minto Reforms (1909)? Satyendra Prasanna Sinha.

In 1924, Mahatma Gandhi presided over the INC session for the first and only time. Where was this session held? Belgaum.

✊ Simon Commission, Civil Disobedience & Round Table Conferences
Why was the Simon Commission (1927) heavily boycotted by Indians? Because it was an "All-White" commission with no Indian members representing India's future.

Who drafted the "Nehru Report" in 1928, demanding Dominion Status for India? Motilal Nehru.

Which prominent Muslim leader rejected the Nehru Report and put forward his own "Fourteen Points"? Muhammad Ali Jinnah.

In which historic INC session was the resolution for "Purna Swaraj" (Complete Independence) passed? Lahore Session (1929).

Who presided over the historic Lahore Session of the INC in 1929? Jawaharlal Nehru.

On which date was the first "Independence Day" observed by hoisting the tricolor on the banks of the Ravi River? January 26, 1930.

The Civil Disobedience Movement was launched by Gandhi with which famous event? The Dandi March (Salt Satyagraha).

From where did the Dandi March begin on March 12, 1930? Sabarmati Ashram (Ahmedabad).

How many chosen followers accompanied Gandhi on the Dandi March initially? 78.

Who led the Salt Satyagraha in Tamil Nadu (marching from Trichy to Vedaranyam)? C. Rajagopalachari.

Who was the prominent leader of the Red Shirt movement (Khudai Khidmatgars) in the North-West Frontier Province? Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan (Frontier Gandhi).

Who led the non-violent raid on the Dharasana Salt Works after Gandhi's arrest? Sarojini Naidu, Imam Saheb, and Manilal Gandhi.

How many Round Table Conferences were held in London to discuss constitutional reforms in India? Three (1930, 1931, 1932).

Which pact in 1931 resulted in the suspension of the Civil Disobedience Movement and the INC's participation in the Second Round Table Conference? The Gandhi-Irwin Pact.

Who attended all three Round Table Conferences representing the depressed classes? Dr. B.R. Ambedkar.

Which British Prime Minister announced the controversial "Communal Award" in 1932? Ramsay MacDonald.

What did the Communal Award propose that Mahatma Gandhi vehemently opposed by fasting unto death? Separate electorates for the depressed classes.

Which agreement replaced the Communal Award, reserving seats for depressed classes within the general Hindu electorate? The Poona Pact (1932).

The Poona Pact was signed between Dr. B.R. Ambedkar and which leader representing the Hindu upper castes? Madan Mohan Malaviya.

Who founded the Harijan Sevak Sangh in 1932 to eradicate untouchability? Mahatma Gandhi.

Which revolutionary organization carried out the daring Kakori Train Robbery in 1925? Hindustan Republican Association (HRA).

Name the prominent revolutionaries hanged for the Kakori conspiracy. Ram Prasad Bismil, Ashfaqulla Khan, Roshan Singh, and Rajendra Lahiri.

Who reorganized the HRA into the Hindustan Socialist Republican Association (HSRA) in 1928? Chandrashekhar Azad.

Who assassinated the British police officer John Saunders in Lahore to avenge the death of Lala Lajpat Rai? Bhagat Singh, Shivaram Rajguru, and Sukhdev Thapar.

Bhagat Singh and Batukeshwar Dutt threw non-lethal bombs in the Central Legislative Assembly in 1929 to protest against which two bills? The Public Safety Bill and the Trade Dispute Bill.

🏛️ Provincial Autonomy & The Path to World War II
Who led the daring Chittagong Armoury Raid in 1930? Surya Sen (affectionately called Master Da).

Which British Act proposed the establishment of an "All India Federation" and introduced provincial autonomy? Government of India Act, 1935.

Under the Government of India Act 1935, which region was separated from India? Burma (Myanmar).

In the provincial elections of 1937, the INC formed ministries in how many provinces out of 11? Eight.

Why did the Congress ministries resign en masse in October 1939? In protest against the British government dragging India into World War II without consulting Indian leaders.

Who defeated Pattabhi Sitaramayya (Gandhi's chosen candidate) to become the INC President at the Tripuri Session in 1939? Subhas Chandra Bose.

After resigning from the INC presidency in 1939 due to ideological differences, which political faction did Subhas Chandra Bose form within the Congress? The Forward Bloc.

In 1940, at which session did the All India Muslim League officially pass the resolution demanding a separate state for Muslims (Pakistan Resolution)? The Lahore Session.

Who drafted the Lahore Resolution (Pakistan Resolution) of 1940? Sikandar Hayat Khan (it was presented by Fazlul Huq).

Who first coined the term "Pakistan" in a 1933 pamphlet titled Now or Never? Choudhry Rahmat Ali.

In response to the British "August Offer" of 1940, what movement did Gandhi launch? Individual Satyagraha.

Who was chosen by Gandhi as the first "Individual Satyagrahi"? Acharya Vinoba Bhave.

Who was chosen as the second Individual Satyagrahi? Jawaharlal Nehru.

Which British mission visited India in 1942 to secure Indian cooperation for the war effort, offering Dominion Status after the war? The Cripps Mission.

Who famously described the Cripps Mission's proposals as "a post-dated cheque on a crashing bank"? Mahatma Gandhi.

Here is Part 5 (Questions 401–500), the final stretch of your History master-list. This section covers the climax of the freedom struggle (Quit India, INA, Partition), the painful process of integration, the framing of the Constitution, and high-yield reviews of books, titles, and Viceroys.

✊ The Final Struggle: Quit India & The INA
Which historic resolution was passed by the INC on August 8, 1942, at the Gowalia Tank Maidan in Bombay? The Quit India Resolution.

What famous mantra did Mahatma Gandhi give to the nation during the Quit India Movement? "Do or Die" (Karo Ya Maro).

Who secretly broadcasted the message of the Quit India Movement on the underground Congress Radio to bypass British censorship? Usha Mehta.

Who led the underground movement and formed the guerrilla "Azad Dasta" in Nepal during the 1942 revolt? Jayaprakash Narayan.

Name the highly successful parallel government set up in Satara, Maharashtra, during the Quit India Movement. Prati Sarkar (led by Nana Patil).

Who set up the parallel government "Jatiya Sarkar" in Tamluk, Bengal? Satish Chandra Samanta.

Who was the first commander of the Indian National Army (INA), having originally formed it with Indian POWs in Malaya? Captain Mohan Singh.

Subhas Chandra Bose took over the leadership of the Indian Independence League and the INA in 1943 in which city? Singapore.

What was the name of the exclusive women's regiment of the INA? The Rani of Jhansi Regiment.

Who was the commander of the Rani of Jhansi Regiment? Captain Lakshmi Sahgal.

What famous slogan did Subhas Chandra Bose give to the INA soldiers to motivate their march towards India? "Dilli Chalo" (Onward to Delhi).

The highly publicized INA trials (Red Fort Trials) of 1945–46 involved which three prominent officers? Prem Kumar Sahgal, Shah Nawaz Khan, and Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon.

Who was the chief defense counsel for the INA prisoners during the Red Fort trials? Bhulabhai Desai (assisted by Tej Bahadur Sapru, Jawaharlal Nehru, and others).

In February 1946, a massive naval mutiny broke out against the British on which ship in Bombay? HMIS Talwar (The Royal Indian Navy Mutiny).

The 1944 "C.R. Formula" (Rajagopalachari formula) was an attempt to resolve the political deadlock between which two entities? The INC and the Muslim League.

🗺️ Independence, Partition & Integration
Which British plan of 1945 proposed the complete Indianization of the Viceroy's Executive Council (except the Viceroy and Commander-in-Chief)? The Wavell Plan.

Where was the conference held in 1945 to discuss the Wavell Plan? Shimla.

Which British Prime Minister sent the Cabinet Mission to India in 1946? Clement Attlee.

Who were the three members of the Cabinet Mission? Lord Pethick-Lawrence, Sir Stafford Cripps, and A.V. Alexander.

Did the Cabinet Mission accept the demand for a separate, sovereign state of Pakistan? No, it rejected it and proposed a loose union of India.

Which date did the Muslim League declare as "Direct Action Day" to force the demand for Pakistan, leading to massive communal riots? August 16, 1946.

Who was the de facto head of the Interim Government formed in September 1946? Jawaharlal Nehru (as Vice President of the Executive Council).

Who served as the Finance Minister in the Interim Government, famously obstructing Congress-led initiatives? Liaquat Ali Khan (representing the Muslim League).

On February 20, 1947, Prime Minister Clement Attlee declared that the British would quit India by what deadline? June 30, 1948.

Who was sent to India in March 1947 as the last British Viceroy to ensure the transfer of power? Lord Mountbatten.

What was the name of the plan proposed on June 3, 1947, which officially outlined the partition of India? The Mountbatten Plan (or 3rd June Plan).

Which British Act gave legal effect to the Mountbatten Plan? Indian Independence Act, 1947.

Who was appointed as the chairman of the Boundary Commission to hastily demarcate the borders of India and Pakistan? Sir Cyril Radcliffe.

What was the exact midnight hour when India formally achieved its independence? Midnight of August 14-15, 1947.

Who was the first Governor-General of the newly independent Dominion of India? Lord Mountbatten.

Who was the first (and only) Indian Governor-General of independent India? C. Rajagopalachari.

Who was the first Governor-General of the Dominion of Pakistan? Muhammad Ali Jinnah.

Who was the first Prime Minister of Pakistan? Liaquat Ali Khan.

Who was the Minister of Home Affairs who brilliantly and forcefully integrated over 500 princely states into the Indian Union? Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel.

Who was the Secretary of the States Department who closely assisted Sardar Patel in the integration of princely states? V.P. Menon.

Which major princely state required a "police action" named Operation Polo in 1948 to force its integration into India? Hyderabad.

Which princely state integrated into India after a public plebiscite heavily favored joining the union? Junagadh.

The ruler of Kashmir, Maharaja Hari Singh, signed what document on October 26, 1947, to legally accede to India? The Instrument of Accession.

Which French enclave in India was formally merged with the Indian Union in 1954? Puducherry (Pondicherry).

Which Portuguese enclave was liberated by the Indian armed forces through Operation Vijay in 1961? Goa.

🏛️ Framing the Constitution & Post-Independence
Who served as the President of the Constituent Assembly of India? Dr. Rajendra Prasad.

Who moved the "Objective Resolution" in the Constituent Assembly, which laid the philosophical foundation for the Preamble? Jawaharlal Nehru.

Exactly how long did it take the Constituent Assembly to frame the Constitution of India? 2 years, 11 months, and 18 days.

The design of the National Flag was officially adopted by the Constituent Assembly on what date? July 22, 1947.

Who assassinated Mahatma Gandhi on January 30, 1948, at Birla House in Delhi? Nathuram Godse.

The Bhoodan Movement (Land Gift Movement) was initiated in 1951 by whom to address land inequality? Acharya Vinoba Bhave.

Which village in Telangana was the starting point of the Bhoodan Movement? Pochampally.

Which commission was established in 1953 to recommend the reorganization of state boundaries? States Reorganisation Commission (Fazl Ali Commission).

Who was the prominent Telugu leader who fasted to death in 1952 demanding a separate state of Andhra? Potti Sriramulu.

Which Act formally reorganized the states of India on linguistic lines in 1956? States Reorganisation Act, 1956.

🎖️ High-Yield Review: Viceroys, Books, & Titles
Which British Governor-General introduced the English language as the medium of higher education in 1835? Lord William Bentinck.

Who was the author of the controversial "Minute on Indian Education" (1835) that pushed for Western education? Thomas Babington Macaulay.

Which Viceroy passed the Arms Act of 1878, restricting Indians from carrying firearms? Lord Lytton.

The Ancient Monuments Preservation Act of 1904 was passed during the tenure of which Viceroy? Lord Curzon.

Who was the Viceroy when the Rowlatt Act was passed and the Jallianwala Bagh massacre occurred? Lord Chelmsford.

The first regular, synchronized census in India was conducted in 1881 during the tenure of which Viceroy? Lord Ripon.

Who wrote the patriotic poem "Sare Jahan Se Achha"? Muhammad Iqbal.

Who composed the national song "Vande Mataram"? Bankim Chandra Chatterjee.

In which novel was "Vande Mataram" originally published? Anandamath.

The Sanyasi Rebellion of the late 18th century forms the historical backdrop of which famous novel? Anandamath.

Who wrote the fundamental critique of modern civilization titled Hind Swaraj in 1909? Mahatma Gandhi.

Who wrote Gita Rahasya while imprisoned in the Mandalay jail? Bal Gangadhar Tilak.

Who wrote the heavily censored book The Indian War of Independence, 1857? V.D. Savarkar.

Who was the author of the nationalist book Unhappy India? Lala Lajpat Rai.

Who wrote the classic book The Discovery of India while incarcerated in the Ahmednagar fort prison? Jawaharlal Nehru.

Who was the author of the political memoir India Wins Freedom? Maulana Abul Kalam Azad.

Which prominent leader was affectionately known as "Loknayak"? Jayaprakash Narayan.

Who was given the titles "Deshratna" (Jewel of the Country) and "Ajatashatru" (One with no enemies)? Dr. Rajendra Prasad.

The "Deshbandhu" title was given to Chittaranjan Das. Who was given the title "Deenbandhu" (Friend of the Poor) by Gandhi? Charles Freer Andrews.

Who founded the "All India Depressed Classes League" in 1935? Jagjivan Ram.

🔥 Final Mix: Movements & Milestones
The historic "August Declaration" of 1917, which promised the gradual development of self-governing institutions, was made by whom? Edwin Montagu (Secretary of State for India).

The Tebhaga Movement (1946) was a massive peasant uprising demanding a two-thirds share of the harvest in which province? Bengal.

The Worli Revolt of 1945 by indigenous tribes against landlords took place in which state? Maharashtra.

The Punnapra-Vayalar uprising of 1946 was a communist-led movement in which princely state? Travancore (Kerala).

The Telangana peasant armed struggle (1946-1951) was directed against the oppressive rule of whom? The Nizam of Hyderabad and his paramilitary Razakars.

Who was the youngest person ever to be elected as the President of the Indian National Congress? Maulana Abul Kalam Azad (at age 35 in 1923).

In 1938, a National Planning Committee was set up by the INC. Who was appointed as its chairman? Jawaharlal Nehru.

Who was the INC President during the 1938 Haripura Session who initiated the formation of the National Planning Committee? Subhas Chandra Bose.

The Eka Movement (1921) was a peasant uprising demanding the conversion of produce rent into cash, primarily situated in which region? Awadh (Uttar Pradesh).

Who was the primary grassroots leader of the Eka Movement? Madari Pasi.

The famous "Karachi Session" of the INC in 1931 is historically known for passing a resolution on what? Fundamental Rights and the National Economic Programme.

Who presided over the historic Karachi Session of 1931? Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel.

Which young Indian woman revolutionary boldly fired at the Governor of Bengal, Stanley Jackson, during the convocation at Calcutta University in 1932? Bina Das.

Who was the prominent leader known as the "Kerala Gandhi" who played a key role in the Vaikom and Guruvayur Satyagrahas? K. Kelappan.

Which British Prime Minister participated in and chaired the Round Table Conferences? Ramsay MacDonald.

During British rule, who was the first Indian to become a Member of Parliament (MP) in the British House of Commons? Dadabhai Naoroji (in 1892, representing the Liberal Party).

Who founded the first major Indian women's organization, "Bharat Stree Mahamandal", in 1910? Sarla Devi Chaudhurani.

The "Mahad Satyagraha" of 1927, fighting for the right of Dalits to draw water from a public tank, was led by whom? Dr. B.R. Ambedkar.

Who published the influential Marathi fortnightly newspaper Mooknayak (Leader of the Voiceless)? Dr. B.R. Ambedkar.

Who started the English weekly newspaper New India and the daily Commonweal to promote the Home Rule movement? Annie Besant.

Which prominent Indian leader was the founding editor of the newspaper National Herald? Jawaharlal Nehru.

The "Dickie Bird Plan" (or Plan Balkan) was an early, quickly rejected partition proposal drafted by whom? Lord Mountbatten.

Who was the first Indian to win the prestigious Ramon Magsaysay Award for community leadership? Acharya Vinoba Bhave (1958).

Which act passed by the British is widely considered the blueprint for the Constitution of independent India? The Government of India Act, 1935.

Who was the defense lawyer who successfully defended Aurobindo Ghosh in the Alipore Bomb Case? Chittaranjan Das (C.R. Das).

In which year did the British shift their summer capital to Shimla? 1864 (under Viceroy John Lawrence).

Which revolutionary group was responsible for the Kakori Train Robbery in 1925? Hindustan Republican Association (HRA).

Who was the founder of the "Khudai Khidmatgars" (Servants of God), a non-violent Pashtun movement? Khan Abdul Ghaffar Khan.

Which British official was assassinated by Madan Lal Dhingra in London in 1909? Sir William Hutt Curzon Wyllie.

Who was the final Governor-General of India, whose retirement in 1950 officially abolished the position as India became a Republic? C. Rajagopalachari.

Let’s map this out!

🌍 Earth's Interior & Geomorphology
What is the widely accepted theory regarding the origin of the universe? The Big Bang Theory.

What is the approximate age of the Earth? 4.6 billion years.

What are the three main concentric layers of the Earth? Crust, Mantle, and Core.

Earth's internal structure, AI generated
Shutterstock
What is the boundary or discontinuity between the Earth's Crust and the Mantle called? Mohorovičić (Moho) Discontinuity.

What is the boundary between the Mantle and the Outer Core called? Gutenberg Discontinuity.

Which is the most abundant chemical element in the Earth's crust? Oxygen (followed by Silicon).

Which is the most abundant metal in the Earth's crust? Aluminum.

Who proposed the Continental Drift Theory in 1912? Alfred Wegener.

According to the Continental Drift Theory, what was the name of the single supercontinent? Pangaea.

What was the name of the massive superocean surrounding Pangaea? Panthalassa.

Who proposed the theory of Seafloor Spreading? Harry Hess (1960).

The San Andreas Fault in California is an example of which type of plate boundary? Transform boundary.

tectonic plate boundaries, AI generated
Shutterstock
Explore
Which region is known as the "Ring of Fire" due to its high concentration of active volcanoes and earthquakes? The Pacific Ocean basin.

What is the exact point within the Earth where an earthquake originates called? Focus (or Hypocenter).

What is the point on the Earth's surface directly above the earthquake's focus called? Epicenter.

Which seismic waves are the fastest and reach the surface first? Primary waves (P-waves).

Which seismic waves can travel through solids but cannot pass through liquid media (like the outer core)? Secondary waves (S-waves).

Which logarithmic scale measures the magnitude (energy released) of an earthquake? Richter Scale.

Which scale measures the intensity (observable damage) of an earthquake? Mercalli Scale.

Basalt and Pumice are examples of which type of rock? Extrusive Igneous Rock.

Granite is an example of which type of rock? Intrusive Igneous Rock.

Sandstone, limestone, and shale belong to which rock family? Sedimentary Rocks.

When subjected to intense heat and pressure, limestone metamorphoses into which rock? Marble.

Which is the largest active volcano on Earth in terms of volume and area? Mauna Loa (Hawaii).

What is a triangular landform created by the deposition of sediment at the mouth of a river called? Delta.

V-shaped valleys are carved by rivers. What carves U-shaped valleys? Glaciers.

What is the term for extensive wind-blown deposits of fine silt and clay? Loess.

"Mushroom rocks" are a typical landform created by the erosional action of what? Wind (in deserts).

Stalactites and stalagmites are prominent depositional features of which type of topography? Karst (Limestone) topography.

What is a "Moraine"? An accumulation of dirt and rocks that have fallen onto the glacier surface or have been pushed along by the glacier as it moves.

🌤️ Climatology & The Atmosphere
Which is the most abundant gas in the Earth's atmosphere? Nitrogen (approx. 78%).

In which atmospheric layer do almost all weather phenomena (clouds, rain, storms) occur? Troposphere.

Earth's atmospheric layers, AI generated
Shutterstock
Which layer contains the ozone layer that protects us from harmful UV radiation? Stratosphere.

In which atmospheric layer do meteors typically burn up upon entering Earth? Mesosphere.

Which layer contains electrically charged particles and reflects radio waves back to Earth? Ionosphere (part of the Thermosphere).

What is the standard atmospheric pressure at sea level? 1013.25 millibars (mb).

Which instrument is used to measure wind speed? Anemometer.

Which instrument is used to measure relative humidity? Hygrometer.

On a weather map, what are the lines joining places of equal temperature called? Isotherms.

What are the lines joining places of equal atmospheric pressure called? Isobars.

What is the apparent force caused by Earth's rotation that deflects moving objects (like wind) to the right in the Northern Hemisphere? The Coriolis Effect.

In the Northern Hemisphere, in which direction do winds blow around a Low-Pressure center (Cyclone)? Counter-clockwise.

In the Northern Hemisphere, in which direction do winds blow around a High-Pressure center (Anticyclone)? Clockwise.

Which planetary winds blow steadily from the Subtropical High-Pressure belts toward the Equatorial Low-Pressure belt? Trade Winds.

What are the "Roaring Forties", "Furious Fifties", and "Shrieking Sixties"? Extremely strong Westerly winds in the Southern Hemisphere.

Which warm, dry local wind blowing down the eastern slopes of the Rocky Mountains is known as the "Snow Eater"? Chinook.

Which similar warm and dry local wind blows down the northern slopes of the Alps in Europe? Foehn.

Which dry, dusty wind blowing from the Sahara Desert to the West African coast is famously known as "The Doctor"? Harmattan.

Which hot, dry, and oppressive wind blows in the plains of Northern India and Pakistan during summer? Loo.

Which hot, dust-laden wind blows from the Sahara Desert across the Mediterranean to Southern Europe? Sirocco.

Intense tropical cyclones in the Atlantic Ocean and the eastern Pacific are called what? Hurricanes.

What are tropical cyclones in the Northwest Pacific Ocean (near China and Japan) called? Typhoons.

What is the local Australian name for a severe tropical cyclone? Willy-Willy.

Which type of tall, dense clouds are heavily associated with thunderstorms, lightning, and heavy rain? Cumulonimbus clouds.

Cumulonimbus clouds, AI generated
Shutterstock
Which are the highest altitude clouds, looking like thin, wispy white feathers made of ice crystals? Cirrus clouds.

What is the "Albedo" of the Earth? The proportion of incident solar radiation reflected back into space by the Earth's surface and atmosphere (approx. 30%).

In meteorology, what is the transition zone or boundary between two different air masses called? A Front.

Which global climate type is uniquely characterized by dry, hot summers and mild, wet winters? Mediterranean Climate.

Which climate type is characterized by high temperatures and heavy rainfall throughout the year, with daily afternoon showers? Equatorial Climate.

Under the Köppen climate classification, what does 'Aw' represent? Tropical Savanna climate (wet and dry).

🌊 Oceanography
Which is the largest and deepest ocean on Earth? The Pacific Ocean.

Which ocean is roughly 'S-shaped' and is the second-largest in the world? The Atlantic Ocean.

Which is the smallest and shallowest of the world's oceans? The Arctic Ocean.

Which is the deepest known trench in the world's oceans? The Mariana Trench.

What is the name of the deepest point located within the Mariana Trench? Challenger Deep (approx. 10,900 meters).

Which is the longest continuous mountain range on Earth, located entirely underwater? The Mid-Atlantic Ridge.

What is the average salinity of the global oceans? 35 parts per thousand (35‰).

Which is the most abundant salt dissolved in ocean water? Sodium Chloride (NaCl).

Which water body in the world boasts one of the highest recorded salinities? Lake Van (Turkey) or the Dead Sea.

What primary celestial force is responsible for the formation of ocean tides? The gravitational pull of the Moon and the Sun on the Earth.

What are the exceptionally high tides that occur during the Full Moon and New Moon phases called? Spring Tides.

What are the exceptionally low tides that occur during the first and third quarter moon phases called? Neap Tides.

Which powerful, warm ocean current flows along the eastern coast of the United States? The Gulf Stream.

global ocean currents map, AI generated
Shutterstock
Explore
Which cold ocean current flows northward along the western coast of South America and is associated with the El Niño phenomenon? The Peru Current (or Humboldt Current).

Which warm ocean current flows northward along the eastern coast of Japan? The Kuroshio Current.

Which cold ocean current flows southward along the western coast of North America? The California Current.

The meeting point of the warm Gulf Stream and the cold Labrador Current creates ideal conditions for what major economic activity? Commercial fishing (e.g., the Grand Banks of Newfoundland).

What is a massive piece of freshwater ice that has broken off a glacier or an ice shelf and is floating in open water? An Iceberg.

What is the geological term for isolated underwater volcanic mountains with flat tops? Guyots.

What is the gently sloping, shallow, submerged edge of a continent called? The Continental Shelf.

🪐 Planetary Earth, Latitudes & Longitudes
Which planet is closest to the Sun? Mercury.

Which is the hottest planet in our solar system due to a runaway greenhouse effect? Venus.

Which planet is frequently referred to as "Earth's Twin" due to its similar size and mass? Venus.

Which is the largest planet in our solar system? Jupiter.

Which planet is famous for its massive, prominent ring system? Saturn.

Which planet rotates on its side, with its axis of rotation tilted almost parallel to its orbital plane? Uranus.

Approximately how long does it take for light from the Sun to reach the Earth? 8 minutes and 20 seconds.

What is the exact scientific term for the shape of the Earth (bulging at the equator and flattened at the poles)? Oblate Spheroid (or Geoid).

What is the exact latitude of the Tropic of Cancer? 23.5° North.

What is the exact latitude of the Tropic of Capricorn? 23.5° South.

What is the total number of longitudes on the globe? 360.

What is the total number of latitudes on the globe (including the equator)? 181.

What is the 0° line of longitude, which passes through the Royal Observatory in London, called? The Prime Meridian (or Greenwich Meridian).

The 180° line of longitude roughly corresponds to which global time-keeping boundary? The International Date Line.

The Earth rotates 360 degrees in 24 hours. Therefore, 1 degree of longitude corresponds to a time difference of how many minutes? 4 minutes.

In which direction does the Earth rotate on its axis? From West to East.

What is the term for the point in Earth's orbit when it is closest to the Sun (occurring around January 3rd)? Perihelion.

What is the term for the point in Earth's orbit when it is farthest from the Sun (occurring around July 4th)? Aphelion.

On which two days of the year is the Sun directly over the Equator, resulting in equal days and nights worldwide? The Equinoxes (March 21 - Vernal, and September 23 - Autumnal).

What is the longest day of the year in the Northern Hemisphere, occurring when the Sun is directly over the Tropic of Cancer? The Summer Solstice (June 21).

🗺️ World Geography: Straits, Canals & Water Bodies
Which famous strait connects the Mediterranean Sea to the Atlantic Ocean and separates Europe from Africa? Strait of Gibraltar.

Which strait connects the Andaman Sea to the South China Sea, serving as a crucial global shipping lane? Strait of Malacca.

Which strait separates Asia (Russia) from North America (Alaska) and connects the Arctic and Pacific Oceans? Bering Strait.

Which strategic strait connects the Persian Gulf to the Gulf of Oman and the Arabian Sea? Strait of Hormuz.

Which strait connects the Red Sea to the Gulf of Aden? Bab-el-Mandeb (Gate of Tears).

Which man-made canal connects the Red Sea to the Mediterranean Sea, heavily reducing travel time between Europe and Asia? Suez Canal.

Which man-made waterway connects the Atlantic Ocean to the Pacific Ocean? Panama Canal.

Which is the largest lake in the world by surface area (often classified as an inland sea)? Caspian Sea.

Which is the deepest and largest freshwater lake by volume in the world? Lake Baikal (Russia).

Which is the largest freshwater lake in the world by surface area? Lake Superior (North America).

Which is the highest commercially navigable lake in the world, located in the Andes? Lake Titicaca (border of Peru and Bolivia).

Which is the longest river in the world? The Nile River (Africa).

Which is the largest river in the world by discharge volume of water? The Amazon River (South America).

Which is the longest river in Asia? The Yangtze River (China).

Which is the longest river in Europe? The Volga River (Russia).

Which major river in Africa crosses the Equator twice? The Congo River (Zaire River).

Which river in Africa crosses the Tropic of Capricorn twice? The Limpopo River.

Which is the world's largest hot desert? The Sahara Desert (Africa).

Which is considered the driest non-polar desert in the world? The Atacama Desert (South America).

Which famous cold winter desert is located in Northern China and Southern Mongolia? The Gobi Desert.

🏔️ World Geography: Continents & Superlatives
Which is the longest continental mountain range in the world? The Andes (South America).

Andes mountain range map, AI generated
Getty Images
Which is the highest mountain peak outside of Asia? Mount Aconcagua (in the Andes, Argentina).

Which mountain range acts as the traditional natural boundary dividing Europe and Asia? The Ural Mountains.

Which is the highest peak in North America? Denali (formerly Mount McKinley, USA).

Which is the highest peak in Africa, famous for being a dormant stratovolcano? Mount Kilimanjaro (Tanzania).

Which is the highest peak in Europe? Mount Elbrus (Russia).

Which is the highest peak in Antarctica? Vinson Massif.

Which is the only continent through which the Equator, Tropic of Cancer, and Tropic of Capricorn all pass? Africa.

Which continent is entirely within the Southern Hemisphere and is known as the "Island Continent"? Australia.

Which is the largest island in the world? Greenland.

Which European country is known as the "Land of a Thousand Lakes"? Finland.

Which country is known as the "Land of the Rising Sun"? Japan.

Which country is famously known as the "Land of the Midnight Sun"? Norway.

Which country's capital city (Quito) is situated almost exactly on the Equator? Ecuador.

Which is the world's highest uninterrupted waterfall? Angel Falls (Venezuela).

Which is the largest coral reef system in the world? The Great Barrier Reef (Australia).

Which spectacular gorge was carved out by the Colorado River in the United States? The Grand Canyon.

Which country has the longest total coastline in the world? Canada.

Which is the world's largest archipelago (group of islands) by area and population? Indonesia.

Which two countries share the longest international land border in the world? United States and Canada.

📍 Indian Geography: Location & Borders
What is India's rank in the world by total geographical area? 7th.

Approximately what percentage of the world's total land area does India occupy? 2.4%.

Which important line of latitude divides India into two almost equal climatic halves? The Tropic of Cancer (23.5° N).

How many Indian states does the Tropic of Cancer pass through? 8 states.

Name the 8 states that the Tropic of Cancer crosses from west to east. Gujarat, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tripura, and Mizoram.

Which longitude is used to determine the Indian Standard Time (IST)? 82.5° E (passing through Mirzapur, UP).

Indian Standard Time (IST) is ahead of Greenwich Mean Time (GMT) by how much? 5 hours and 30 minutes.

What is the southernmost tip of the Indian mainland? Kanyakumari (Cape Comorin).

What is the southernmost point of the entire Indian territory (including islands)? Indira Point (in the Andaman and Nicobar Islands).

What is the northernmost point of India, located in the Siachen Glacier region? Indira Col.

What is the easternmost point of India? Kibithu (Arunachal Pradesh).

What is the westernmost point of India? Ghuar Mota / Sir Creek (Gujarat).

What is the total approximate length of India's coastline (including the mainland, Lakshadweep, and Andaman & Nicobar)? 7,516.6 km.

Which Indian state boasts the longest coastline? Gujarat.

Which South Indian state has the longest coastline? Andhra Pradesh.

How many Indian states in total share a coastline? 9 states.

India shares its longest international land border with which country? Bangladesh (4,096 km).

India shares its shortest international land border with which country? Afghanistan (106 km).

What is the name of the official boundary line separating India and China (specifically in the eastern sector)? The McMahon Line.

McMahon Line map, AI generated
Getty Images
What is the boundary line separating India and Pakistan known as? The Radcliffe Line.

What is the boundary line originally drawn between British India and Afghanistan (now mostly between Pakistan and Afghanistan) called? The Durand Line.

Which two water bodies separate India from Sri Lanka? The Palk Strait and the Gulf of Mannar.

Which Indian state shares its international borders with three countries: Nepal, Bhutan, and China? Sikkim.

Which Indian state is surrounded by Bangladesh on three sides? Tripura.

Which maritime channel separates the Andaman Islands from the Nicobar Islands? The Ten Degree Channel.

Which maritime channel separates the Indian territory of Lakshadweep from the Maldives? The Eight Degree Channel.

⛰️ Indian Geomorphology: Peaks, Passes & Plateaus
Which is the highest mountain peak in the Indian subcontinent (currently located in PoK)? K2 (Mount Godwin-Austen).

Which is the highest peak situated within undisputed Indian territory? Kangchenjunga (Sikkim).

Which is the highest mountain peak situated entirely within the borders of India? Nanda Devi (Uttarakhand).

Which is the oldest fold mountain range in India? The Aravalli Range.

Which is the highest peak of the Aravalli Range? Guru Shikhar (Mount Abu).

Which mountain system in India represents the youngest and highest fold mountains in the world? The Himalayas.

The Himalayas are divided into three parallel ranges. What is the outermost, lowest-elevation range called? The Shiwaliks (Outer Himalayas).

What is the innermost, highest-elevation continuous range of the Himalayas called? The Himadri (Greater Himalayas).

The famous Valley of Kashmir is situated between which two mountain ranges? The Zanskar Range and the Pir Panjal Range.

Which mountain range in Central India acts as a water divide between the Narmada and Tapti river systems? The Satpura Range.

Which is the highest peak of the Satpura Range? Dhupgarh (Pachmarhi).

At which geographical point do the Eastern Ghats and the Western Ghats meet? The Nilgiri Hills.

Nilgiri Hills map, AI generated
Getty Images
Which is the highest peak in the Nilgiri Hills? Doddabetta.

Which is the highest peak in the Western Ghats (and the whole of South India)? Anamudi (Kerala).

Which is generally considered the highest peak in the Eastern Ghats? Jindhagada Peak (Arma Konda).

The famous hill station of Kodaikanal is located in which hill range? The Palani Hills (Tamil Nadu).

Which highly strategic mountain pass connects Srinagar to Leh? Zoji La.

Which pass connects Himachal Pradesh (Shimla) to Tibet, through which the Sutlej River enters India? Shipki La.

Which famous pass connects the Kullu Valley with the Lahaul and Spiti Valleys in Himachal Pradesh? Rohtang Pass.

Which pass in Uttarakhand is actively used by pilgrims for the Kailash Mansarovar Yatra? Lipulekh Pass.

Which pass connects Sikkim with Tibet (China) and is a major border trade route? Nathu La.

Which pass connects Arunachal Pradesh with Lhasa, the capital of Tibet? Bom Di La.

Which mountain pass (ghat) in the Western Ghats connects Mumbai to Pune? Bhor Ghat.

Which pass in the Western Ghats connects Mumbai to Nashik? Thal Ghat.

Which pass acts as a major communication route connecting Kerala (Palakkad) with Tamil Nadu (Coimbatore)? Palghat (Palakkad Gap).

Which major plateau region is known as the "Mineral Heart of India" due to its rich coal and iron ore reserves? The Chota Nagpur Plateau.

The Deccan Plateau of Peninsular India is primarily composed of which type of igneous rock? Basalt.

Which is the largest physiographic division of India by area? The Peninsular Plateau.

The Meghalaya Plateau is geologically an eastward extension of which larger Indian landmass? The Peninsular Plateau.

Name the three prominent hill ranges that make up the Meghalaya Plateau (from west to east). Garo, Khasi, and Jaintia Hills.

Mawsynram, globally recognized as the wettest place on Earth, is located in which of these hill ranges? The Khasi Hills.

What is the vast, arid sandy desert located in Northwestern India called? The Thar Desert (Great Indian Desert).

What is the geographical term for the crescent-shaped sand dunes prominently found in the Indian desert? Barchans.

What is the name of the only confirmed active volcano in India (and South Asia)? Barren Island (Andaman Sea).
