🏢 Non-Constitutional & Statutory Bodies
When was NITI Aayog officially established? January 1, 2015.

What does the acronym NITI stand for? National Institution for Transforming India.

NITI Aayog replaced which historical body? The Planning Commission.

Who acts as the ex-officio Chairperson of NITI Aayog? The Prime Minister of India.

Who appoints the Vice-Chairperson of NITI Aayog? The Prime Minister.

Is the National Human Rights Commission (NHRC) a constitutional body? No, it is a statutory body (created by the Protection of Human Rights Act, 1993).

Who can be appointed as the Chairperson of the NHRC? A retired Chief Justice of India or a Judge of the Supreme Court.

What is the term of office for the Chairperson of the NHRC? 3 years or until they attain the age of 70 years, whichever is earlier.

Which committee recommends the appointment of the NHRC Chairperson? A 6-member committee headed by the Prime Minister.

To whom does the State Human Rights Commission (SHRC) submit its annual report? The State Government.

The Central Information Commission (CIC) was established under the provisions of which Act? The Right to Information (RTI) Act, 2005.

The Central Vigilance Commission (CVC) was set up in 1964 on the recommendation of which committee? The Santhanam Committee (on Prevention of Corruption).

Is the CVC a statutory body? Yes, it was granted statutory status by the CVC Act, 2003.

Who appoints the Central Vigilance Commissioner? The President (on the recommendation of a 3-member committee headed by the PM).

The institution of the "Ombudsman" (citizens' defender) was first created in which country in 1809? Sweden.

The historic Lokpal and Lokayuktas Act was passed by the Indian Parliament in which year? 2013.

Who was appointed as the very first Chairperson of the Lokpal in India? Justice Pinaki Chandra Ghose.

Does the Prime Minister of India come under the investigative jurisdiction of the Lokpal? Yes (with certain specific exceptions related to international relations, security, etc.).

Which Indian state was the first to establish the institution of the Lokayukta (in 1971)? Maharashtra.

🗳️ Elections, Anti-Defection & Political Parties
Which Part of the Constitution deals with Elections and the Election Commission? Part XV.

Which Article guarantees the right to vote based on Universal Adult Suffrage? Article 326.

Is the right to vote in India a Fundamental Right or a Constitutional/Legal Right? It is a Constitutional Right.

Electronic Voting Machines (EVMs) were first used on an experimental basis in 1982 in which state? Kerala (Paravur constituency).

In the context of Indian elections, what does VVPAT stand for? Voter Verifiable Paper Audit Trail.

Which Schedule of the Constitution contains the Anti-Defection Law? The Tenth Schedule.

Which Constitutional Amendment Act added the Tenth Schedule in 1985? The 52nd Amendment Act.

Who is the final authority to decide questions of disqualification under the Anti-Defection Law? The Presiding Officer of the House (Speaker/Chairman).

Is the decision of the Presiding Officer under the Anti-Defection Law subject to judicial review? Yes (established by the Supreme Court in the Kihoto Hollohan case, 1992).

Under the current Anti-Defection Law, what fraction of a party's legislators must merge with another party to avoid disqualification? Two-thirds (2/3rd).

Who is responsible for registering political parties and granting them the status of National or State parties? The Election Commission of India.

To be recognized as a "National Party," a political party must be a recognized state party in at least how many states? Four states.

When does the Model Code of Conduct (MCC) come into effect during an election? Immediately after the Election Commission announces the election schedule.

Who is responsible for redrawing the boundaries of electoral constituencies based on recent census data? The Delimitation Commission.

How many Delimitation Commissions have been set up in independent India so far? Four (1952, 1962, 1973, and 2002).

The total number of seats in the Lok Sabha is currently frozen based on the population figures of which Census year? 1971 Census.

Which Constitutional Amendment Act froze the allocation of seats in the Lok Sabha until the year 2026? The 84th Amendment Act (2001).

The "NOTA" (None of the Above) option was introduced in Indian EVMs following a Supreme Court directive in which year? 2013.

⚖️ Governance, Rights & Key Legislations
The landmark Right to Information (RTI) Act came into force on which exact date? October 12, 2005.

The Right to Information is derived implicitly from which Fundamental Right? Freedom of Speech and Expression (Article 19(1)(a)).

Under the standard provisions of the RTI Act, what is the maximum time limit for a Public Information Officer (PIO) to provide the requested information? 30 days.

If an RTI query concerns the life or liberty of a person, within how much time must the information be provided? 48 hours.

The Right to Education (RTE) Act was enacted by the Parliament in which year? 2009 (came into effect on April 1, 2010).

Under the RTE Act, what percentage of seats in private, unaided schools must be reserved for children from economically weaker sections (EWS)? 25%.

The Protection of Children from Sexual Offences (POCSO) Act was passed in which year? 2012.

Which Act provides legal protection to individuals who expose alleged wrongdoing, fraud, or corruption in government bodies? The Whistle Blowers Protection Act (2014).

The National Food Security Act (NFSA), which converts welfare programs into legal entitlements, was enacted in which year? 2013.

Under the NFSA, what maximum percentage of the rural population is legally entitled to receive highly subsidized food grains? Up to 75%.

Under the NFSA, what maximum percentage of the urban population is legally entitled to subsidized food grains? Up to 50%.

In the context of administration, what is a "Citizen's Charter"? A document that outlines the service commitments of an organization to its citizens.

Is a Citizen's Charter legally enforceable in Indian courts? No, it is a non-justiciable document.

Which day is officially celebrated as "Good Governance Day" in India? December 25th (the birth anniversary of former PM Atal Bihari Vajpayee).

The flagship e-governance platform "PRAGATI" stands for what? Pro-Active Governance And Timely Implementation.

🛡️ Flagship Welfare Schemes & Policies
Which scheme is recognized as the world's largest government-funded healthcare program? Ayushman Bharat (Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana - PM-JAY).

Under PM-JAY, what is the maximum health insurance cover provided per family per year? Rs. 5 Lakhs.

The PM-KISAN (Pradhan Mantri Kisan Samman Nidhi) scheme provides an annual income support of how much to eligible farmers? Rs. 6,000.

In how many equal installments is the Rs. 6,000 under PM-KISAN disbursed annually? Three installments of Rs. 2,000 each.

The Pradhan Mantri Ujjwala Yojana (PMUY) focuses on providing what to women of BPL families? Free LPG (liquefied petroleum gas) connections.

The "Beti Bachao Beti Padhao" campaign was launched in 2015 from which district that had a critically low child sex ratio? Panipat (Haryana).

What was the primary objective of the Swachh Bharat Mission (Gramin) launched on October 2, 2014? To make rural India Open Defecation Free (ODF).

Which scheme is the National Mission for Financial Inclusion, aiming to ensure access to banking facilities with at least one basic bank account for every household? Pradhan Mantri Jan Dhan Yojana (PMJDY).

What is the overdraft facility available to eligible account holders under the PMJDY after six months of operation? Rs. 10,000.

The "Stand-Up India" scheme was launched to facilitate bank loans specifically for which categories of entrepreneurs? Scheduled Castes (SC), Scheduled Tribes (ST), and Women.

The Atal Pension Yojana (APY) provides a guaranteed minimum monthly pension to workers in the unorganized sector. What is the eligible age group to join? 18 to 40 years.

The Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana (PMAY) has an overarching vision to provide what? Housing for All.

Which is the flagship scheme under the Skill India Mission designed to provide industry-relevant skill training to youth? Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana (PMKVY).

Which scheme provides a renewable one-year life insurance cover of Rs. 2 lakh for an annual premium of Rs. 436? Pradhan Mantri Jeevan Jyoti Bima Yojana (PMJJBY).

Which scheme provides accidental death and disability insurance cover of Rs. 2 lakh for a nominal annual premium of Rs. 20? Pradhan Mantri Suraksha Bima Yojana (PMSBY).

The AMRUT mission aims to provide basic civic amenities to cities. What does AMRUT stand for? Atal Mission for Rejuvenation and Urban Transformation.

What is the overarching goal of POSHAN Abhiyaan (the National Nutrition Mission)? To achieve a malnutrition-free India (reducing stunting, under-nutrition, and anemia).

Which massive mission was launched to provide safe and adequate drinking water through individual household tap connections by 2024 to all rural households? Jal Jeevan Mission (Har Ghar Jal).

The Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana (SSY), a small deposit scheme for the girl child, was launched as part of which campaign? Beti Bachao Beti Padhao.

What is the maximum age of a girl child for opening an account under the Sukanya Samriddhi Yojana? 10 years.

What is the primary objective of the PM SVANidhi scheme launched during the COVID-19 pandemic? To provide affordable micro-credit working capital loans to street vendors.

The Deen Dayal Antyodaya Yojana - National Rural Livelihoods Mission (DAY-NRLM) heavily focuses on organizing rural poor women into what? Self-Help Groups (SHGs).

The "PM Gati Shakti" National Master Plan is primarily focused on transforming which sector? Multi-modal connectivity and infrastructure logistics.

What is the objective of the Pradhan Mantri Mudra Yojana (PMMY)? To provide collateral-free loans up to Rs. 10 lakhs to non-corporate, non-farm small and micro-enterprises.

Mudra loans are divided into three categories based on the loan amount: Shishu (up to 50k), Tarun (5 lakh to 10 lakh), and what is the middle category (50k to 5 lakh) called? Kishore.

The historic Mid-Day Meal Scheme in schools has been officially renamed to what? PM POSHAN Scheme (Pradhan Mantri Poshan Shakti Nirman).

Under the PM Fasal Bima Yojana (Crop Insurance), what is the uniform premium payable by farmers for all Kharif crops? 2%.

Under PM Fasal Bima Yojana, what is the uniform premium payable by farmers for all Rabi crops? 1.5%.

Under PM Fasal Bima Yojana, what is the premium payable for annual commercial and horticultural crops? 5%.

The "UDAN" scheme aims to stimulate regional air connectivity and make air travel affordable. What does UDAN stand for? Ude Desh ka Aam Naagrik.

The SMILE scheme is designed for the comprehensive rehabilitation of which two marginalized groups? Transgender persons and persons engaged in the act of begging.

Which recent scheme provides end-to-end support, including collateral-free credit and skill training, to traditional artisans and craftspeople? PM Vishwakarma Yojana.

Which scheme was launched to provide up to 300 units of free electricity every month to households through rooftop solar installations? PM Surya Ghar: Muft Bijli Yojana.

The "Vibrant Villages Programme" focuses on the comprehensive infrastructure development of border villages located where? Along the northern land border with China.

Which portal was launched by the Ministry of Labour to create a National Database of Unorganized Workers and provide them with a Universal Account Number (UAN)? e-Shram Portal.

The "Lakhpati Didi" initiative targets the economic empowerment of women participating in what local organizations? Self-Help Groups (aiming to increase their annual household income to at least Rs. 1 Lakh).

What is the full form of MGNREGA, the act that guarantees 100 days of wage employment? Mahatma Gandhi National Rural Employment Guarantee Act.

Under MGNREGA, if work is not provided within 15 days of demanding it, what is the applicant entitled to? A daily unemployment allowance.

What is the primary objective of the PM-KUSUM scheme? To support farmers in installing solar pumps and grid-connected solar power plants.

The "Namami Gange" Programme is an integrated conservation mission. What are its twin objectives? Effective abatement of pollution, and conservation/rejuvenation of the National River Ganga.

Which scheme aims to issue a unique 14-digit identification number for every plot of land in the country? Bhu-Aadhaar (ULPIN - Unique Land Parcel Identification Number).

The "Kisan Credit Card" (KCC) scheme was introduced in which year to provide adequate and timely credit support to farmers? 1998.

The "Digital India" campaign rests on how many "pillars" of growth? Nine pillars (including broadband highways, universal access to phones, e-governance, etc.).

The "Make in India" initiative was launched in 2014 to transform India into what? A global design and manufacturing hub.

Under the National Social Assistance Programme (NSAP), which scheme provides a central pension to the elderly BPL population? Indira Gandhi National Old Age Pension Scheme (IGNOAPS).

Which scheme aims to provide physical aids and assisted-living devices to Senior citizens belonging to the BPL category? Rashtriya Vayoshri Yojana (RVY).

What is the primary focus of the "Kavach" system developed by the Indian Railways? It is an automatic train protection (ATP) system to prevent train collisions.

The "Mission Indradhanush" launched by the Ministry of Health primarily focuses on what? Achieving full immunization coverage for children and pregnant women.

Here is Part 5 (Questions 401–500), the Grand Finale! We are locking down Landmark Supreme Court Judgments, Important Amendments, Constitutional Doctrines, Committees, and Parliamentary Terminology.

Let’s secure these final, high-yield points!

⚖️ Landmark Supreme Court Judgments
In which landmark 1973 case did the Supreme Court propound the "Basic Structure Doctrine"? Kesavananda Bharati v. State of Kerala.

Which 1980 Supreme Court judgment ruled that the Indian Constitution is founded on the bedrock of the balance between Fundamental Rights and Directive Principles? Minerva Mills case.

Which 1994 Supreme Court case established strict guidelines for imposing President's Rule (Article 356) to prevent its misuse? S.R. Bommai v. Union of India.

In which 2017 landmark case did the Supreme Court declare the Right to Privacy as an intrinsic part of the Right to Life and Personal Liberty under Article 21? Justice K.S. Puttaswamy (Retd.) v. Union of India.

Which 1992 case is famously known as the "Mandal Commission case", officially capping caste-based reservations at 50%? Indra Sawhney v. Union of India.

Which 2014 landmark case officially recognized transgender persons as the "Third Gender" in India? NALSA v. Union of India.

Which 1978 case expanded the scope of Article 21 by introducing the concept of "due process of law" instead of just "procedure established by law"? Maneka Gandhi v. Union of India.

Which 1967 case ruled that Parliament does not have the power to amend Fundamental Rights? Golaknath case (later overturned by Kesavananda Bharati).

📜 Special Provisions, Co-operatives & Tribunals
Which Article grants special autonomous status to the state of Nagaland? Article 371A.

Article 371G contains special constitutional provisions with respect to which state? Mizoram.

Which Part of the Constitution deals specifically with Co-operative Societies? Part IX-B.

Which Constitutional Amendment Act added Part IX-B regarding Co-operative Societies? 97th Amendment Act (2011).

Which Part of the Constitution deals with Tribunals? Part XIV-A.

Which Article provides for the establishment of Administrative Tribunals for public service disputes? Article 323A.

Which Amendment Act introduced the provision for Tribunals into the Constitution? 42nd Amendment Act (1976).

🏛️ Services, Commissions & Bodies
Under Article 312, who has the exclusive power to authorize the creation of a new All-India Service? The Rajya Sabha (by a resolution supported by not less than two-thirds of members present and voting).

What are the three existing All-India Services today? Indian Administrative Service (IAS), Indian Police Service (IPS), and Indian Forest Service (IFoS).

Who is historically recognized as the "Father of All India Services"? Sardar Vallabhbhai Patel.

The National Green Tribunal (NGT) was established in which year for the expeditious disposal of cases relating to environmental protection? 2010.

Is the NGT strictly bound by the procedure laid down under the Code of Civil Procedure, 1908? No, it is guided by the principles of natural justice.

Who acts as the ex-officio Chairperson of the National Disaster Management Authority (NDMA)? The Prime Minister.

Who acts as the ex-officio Chairperson of the State Disaster Management Authority (SDMA)? The Chief Minister.

What is the apex appellate body at the national level set up under the Consumer Protection Act? The National Consumer Disputes Redressal Commission (NCDRC).

The Prevention of Money Laundering Act (PMLA) was enacted in which year to combat financial crimes? 2002.

Which central agency is primarily responsible for investigating offences under the PMLA and FEMA? The Enforcement Directorate (ED).

The Foreign Contribution (Regulation) Act, FCRA, which regulates foreign donations, is administered by which Union Ministry? Ministry of Home Affairs.

Which commission was established in 1953 as the First Backward Classes Commission? Kaka Kalelkar Commission.

Which commission was established in 1979 as the Second Backward Classes Commission? Mandal Commission (headed by B.P. Mandal).

Which committee's recommendations directly led to the establishment of the Central Vigilance Commission (CVC)? The Santhanam Committee.

✍️ Major Constitutional Amendments
Which Constitutional Amendment Act is widely known as the "Mini-Constitution"? The 42nd Amendment Act (1976).

Which Amendment Act facilitated the nationwide introduction of the Goods and Services Tax (GST)? 101st Amendment Act (2016).

Which Article was inserted to establish the GST Council? Article 279A.

Which Amendment Act provided a 10% reservation in education and public employment for the Economically Weaker Sections (EWS)? 103rd Amendment Act (2019).

Which Amendment Act granted official constitutional status to the National Commission for Backward Classes (NCBC)? 102nd Amendment Act (2018).

Which Amendment Act extended the reservation of seats for SCs and STs in the Lok Sabha and State Assemblies by another 10 years, up to 2030? 104th Amendment Act (2019).

Which Amendment Act (known as the Nari Shakti Vandan Adhiniyam) provided 33% reservation for women in the Lok Sabha and State Legislative Assemblies? 106th Amendment Act (2023).

🗣️ Parliamentary Terminology & Proceedings
In parliamentary terminology, what does "Sine Die" mean when a session is adjourned? Adjournment without fixing any definite date for the next meeting.

What is the term for the minimum number of members required to be present in the House before it can transact any business? Quorum (1/10th of the total membership).

What is a "Whip" in the context of Parliament? A written directive issued by a political party mandating its members to be present and vote in a specific way.

Who is a "Lame-duck" in parliamentary parlance? Members of the outgoing Lok Sabha who failed to get re-elected to the new Lok Sabha.

What is the "Guillotine" in parliamentary procedure? Putting all undiscussed clauses of a bill or demands for grants to a vote at once due to a shortage of time.

What is the name of the very first hour of a sitting in the Lok Sabha, which is devoted to asking questions? Question Hour.

In Question Hour, what is a "Starred Question"? A question that requires an oral answer on the floor of the House, allowing supplementary questions to follow.

What is an "Unstarred Question"? A question that requires a written answer, meaning no supplementary questions can be asked.

Who administers the oath of office to the Speaker of the Lok Sabha? The Speaker does not take a separate oath of office; they take the oath as a regular Member of Parliament.

Who is appointed to administer the oath to the newly elected members of the Lok Sabha and presides over the election of the new Speaker? The Speaker Pro Tem.

In a tie-breaking situation during a vote in the Lok Sabha, who casts the "Casting Vote"? The Speaker.

🛡️ Constitutional Doctrines & Principles
In the Indian context, what does "Asymmetric Federalism" mean? The concept that all states do not possess equal powers; some states enjoy special provisions or autonomy (e.g., under Article 371).

What was the original objective of the 9th Schedule, added by the 1st Amendment Act (1951)? To protect agrarian land reform laws from judicial review on the grounds of violating Fundamental Rights.

In the I.R. Coelho case (2007), the Supreme Court ruled that laws placed in the 9th Schedule are open to judicial review if they violate what? The Basic Structure of the Constitution (applied to laws inserted after April 24, 1973).

Which famous doctrine determines if a legislature has the actual competence to make a law, focusing on the law's true nature and substance rather than its form? Doctrine of Pith and Substance.

What is the "Doctrine of Colorable Legislation"? The principle that "what cannot be done directly, cannot be done indirectly" (preventing legislatures from bypassing their constitutional limits).

What is the main objective of the "Doctrine of Severability"? If a specific part of a statute is unconstitutional, only that part is declared void, leaving the rest of the statute intact (if it can stand alone).

What is the term for a law that is applied retroactively to acts committed before the law was passed? Ex post facto law.

Does the Indian Constitution allow ex post facto criminal laws? No (prohibited under Article 20(1)).

Which Article protects civil servants from arbitrary dismissal, stating they cannot be dismissed by an authority subordinate to that by which they were appointed? Article 311.

🔥 Final Rapid-Fire Review
Which fund is operated by executive action without the need for parliamentary approval for every single withdrawal? The Public Account of India (Article 266(2)).

Who holds the Contingency Fund of India on behalf of the President? The Finance Secretary.

A person who is not a member of either House of Parliament can be appointed as a Minister for a maximum continuous period of how many months? 6 months.

Can a nominated member of the Rajya Sabha become a Union Minister? Yes.

If the offices of both the President and Vice-President fall vacant, who discharges the functions of the President? The Chief Justice of India.

Who was the only Chief Justice of India to act as the President of India (in 1969)? Justice M. Hidayatullah.

Which part of the Constitution is related exclusively to the Union Territories? Part VIII.

Which three Union Territories currently possess their own Legislative Assemblies? Delhi, Puducherry, and Jammu & Kashmir.

Which Amendment Act formally designated Delhi as the "National Capital Territory" (NCT)? The 69th Amendment Act (1991).

Which Article makes special provisions for Delhi, creating a Legislative Assembly and Council of Ministers? Article 239AA.

The Lieutenant Governor is the administrative head of which types of territories? Union Territories.

Which official language is recognized as the authoritative working language of the Supreme Court and all High Courts? English (Article 348).

In India, who represents the government in all cases in the Supreme Court in which the Government of India is concerned? The Attorney General of India.

Which constitutional post is often referred to as the "Guardian of the Public Purse"? The Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG).

Which part of the Constitution deals exclusively with the Scheduled and Tribal Areas? Part X.

Under which schedule are legislative subjects explicitly distributed between the Union and the States? Seventh Schedule.

Who appoints the Delimitation Commission to redraw electoral boundaries? The President of India.

When a bill is passed by both Houses of Parliament, which Article gives the President the power to assent, withhold assent, or return the bill? Article 111.

Does the President of India possess "Pocket Veto" power? Yes (they can keep a bill pending indefinitely without taking action).

Which Indian President famously used the Pocket Veto in 1986 regarding the Indian Post Office (Amendment) Bill? Giani Zail Singh.

Does the President of India have a "Qualified Veto" (which can only be overridden by a higher majority)? No (The Indian President has a Suspensive Veto, which can be overridden by a simple, ordinary majority).

Which Article guarantees that no person shall be deprived of his property save by authority of law? Article 300A.

Who acts as the Chairman of the National Integration Council (NIC)? The Prime Minister.

Which body gave final approval to the Five-Year Plans in India before the Planning Commission was abolished? The National Development Council (NDC).

What is the primary function of the Central Information Commission (CIC)? To act as the final appellate authority enforcing the Right to Information (RTI) Act.

Which parliamentary committee is famously known as the "Twin Sister" of the Estimates Committee? The Public Accounts Committee (PAC).

Who typically serves as the Chairman of the Public Accounts Committee (PAC) by convention? A leader from the Opposition party.

Which parliamentary committee specifically examines whether the executive is properly exercising its delegated legislative powers? The Committee on Subordinate Legislation.

Which writ is issued to secure the immediate release of a person who has been detained unlawfully? Habeas Corpus.

Which writ literally translates to "We Command"? Mandamus.

What is the literal meaning of the writ "Certiorari"? "To be certified" or "To be informed".

In the Indian Constitution, the framework of "A Union of States" was heavily inspired by which historical Act? The British North America Act, 1867 (Canada).

In the Order of Precedence in India, who ranks immediately after the President and the Vice-President? The Prime Minister.

Who ranks fourth in the Indian Order of Precedence? Governors of States (within their respective States).

What is the term of office for a member of a State Public Service Commission (SPSC)? 6 years or until the age of 62 (whichever is earlier).

What is the term of office for a member of the Union Public Service Commission (UPSC)? 6 years or until the age of 65.

Who appoints the Chief Information Commissioner and Information Commissioners at the central level? The President (on the recommendation of a committee headed by the PM).

Which Article protects the vital "Freedom of Trade, Commerce, and Intercourse" throughout the territory of India? Article 301.

The concept of a "Welfare State" finds its clearest expression in which part of the Indian Constitution? The Directive Principles of State Policy (DPSP).

Which body conducts the audit of the accounts of the Panchayati Raj Institutions? The Comptroller and Auditor General (CAG), as authorized by the State Legislature.

Who has the authority to declare any area as a "Scheduled Area" under the Fifth Schedule? The President of India.

Which fundamental right was described by Dr. B.R. Ambedkar as the "very soul of the Constitution and the very heart of it"? Right to Constitutional Remedies (Article 32).

Who acts as the ultimate "Custodian of the Constitution" in India? The Supreme Court of India.

Ultimately, from where does the Constitution of India derive its absolute power and authority? The People of India (as boldly stated in the opening line of the Preamble: "We, the People of India... do hereby adopt, enact and give to ourselves this Constitution").

For UPSC and UKPSC, the Economy section is highly dynamic, requiring a solid grasp of macroeconomic fundamentals, banking, taxation, and state-specific economic drivers like Uttarakhand's tourism, agriculture, and infrastructure.

Just as we did before, we will break this 500-question marathon into five parts. Here is Part 1 (Questions 1–100), covering Macroeconomic Fundamentals, the Reserve Bank of India (RBI), Economic Planning, Taxation, and the Core Economy of Uttarakhand.

Let's build that economic foundation!

📊 Macroeconomic Fundamentals & National Income
Who is universally recognized as the "Father of Economics"? Adam Smith.

What term best describes the nature of the Indian economy? A Mixed Economy (coexistence of public and private sectors).

Which organization is responsible for estimating the National Income of India? National Statistical Office (NSO).

What is the official Financial Year of the Government of India? April 1 to March 31.

Agriculture, forestry, fishing, and mining belong to which sector of the economy? The Primary Sector.

Manufacturing, construction, and electricity generation belong to which sector? The Secondary Sector.

Banking, transport, tourism, and IT services belong to which sector? The Tertiary Sector (Services Sector).

Which sector currently contributes the highest percentage to India's Gross Domestic Product (GDP)? The Tertiary Sector.

Which sector currently employs the largest percentage of the Indian workforce? The Primary Sector (Agriculture).

What does GDP stand for? Gross Domestic Product (total monetary value of all final goods and services produced within a country's borders in a specific time period).

What is the difference between GDP and GNP (Gross National Product)? GNP includes the net factor income from abroad (income earned by citizens abroad minus income earned by foreigners within the country).

What is "Depreciation" in economic terms? The reduction in the value of an asset over time due to wear and tear.

How is Net Domestic Product (NDP) calculated? GDP minus Depreciation.

What is "Per Capita Income"? The average income earned per person in a given area (National Income divided by Total Population).

Who made the first attempt to calculate the National Income of India in 1867-68? Dadabhai Naoroji.

What is the current base year used for calculating India's GDP? 2011-12.

Which index is primarily used by the RBI to measure retail inflation in India? Consumer Price Index (CPI).

Which index measures the changes in the prices of goods sold and traded in bulk by wholesale businesses? Wholesale Price Index (WPI).

What is "Stagflation"? An economic situation characterized by slow economic growth, high unemployment, and high inflation.

What is a "Recession"? A significant decline in general economic activity extending over two consecutive quarters.

🏦 The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) & Banking
In which year was the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) established? 1935 (under the RBI Act, 1934).

Which commission recommended the establishment of the RBI? The Hilton Young Commission.

In which year was the RBI nationalized? 1949.

Who was the first Governor of the RBI? Sir Osborne Smith.

Who was the first Indian Governor of the RBI? C.D. Deshmukh.

Where is the headquarters of the RBI located? Mumbai (initially in Kolkata, moved to Mumbai in 1937).

What is the "Repo Rate"? The rate at which the RBI lends short-term money to commercial banks against government securities.

What is the "Reverse Repo Rate"? The rate at which the RBI borrows money from commercial banks.

What does CRR stand for? Cash Reserve Ratio (the percentage of total deposits a bank must keep as cash reserves with the RBI).

What does SLR stand for? Statutory Liquidity Ratio (the percentage of deposits banks must maintain in the form of liquid assets like gold or government securities).

If the RBI wants to control inflation, what will it generally do to the Repo Rate? Increase it (to make borrowing more expensive and reduce money supply).

Which committee determines the key interest rates in India? The Monetary Policy Committee (MPC).

How many members are there in the Monetary Policy Committee (MPC)? 6 members (3 from RBI and 3 appointed by the Central Government).

Who acts as the ex-officio Chairperson of the Monetary Policy Committee? The Governor of the RBI.

In which year were 14 major commercial banks nationalized in India? 1969 (during Indira Gandhi's tenure).

Which is the largest public sector bank in India? State Bank of India (SBI).

What was the SBI known as before its nationalization in 1955? The Imperial Bank of India.

Which specialized bank was established in 1982 to provide credit for agriculture and rural development? NABARD (National Bank for Agriculture and Rural Development).

Which organization regulates the securities and stock markets in India? SEBI (Securities and Exchange Board of India).

Which organization is the regulatory body for the insurance sector in India? IRDAI (Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India).

📈 Economic Planning, NITI Aayog & Taxation
The concept of "Five-Year Plans" in India was borrowed from which country? The USSR (Soviet Union).

The First Five-Year Plan (1951-56) was based on which economic model? The Harrod-Domar Model (focusing heavily on agriculture).

The Second Five-Year Plan (1956-61) was based on which model? The Mahalanobis Model (focusing on rapid industrialization and heavy industries).

Which period is referred to as the "Plan Holiday" in Indian economic history? 1966 to 1969 (due to the Indo-Pak war and severe droughts).

Which government introduced the "Rolling Plan" (1978-80)? The Janata Party government.

The famous slogan "Garibi Hatao" (Eradicate Poverty) was given during which Five-Year Plan? The Fifth Five-Year Plan.

On what exact date did NITI Aayog replace the Planning Commission? January 1, 2015.

Who serves as the ex-officio Chairman of NITI Aayog? The Prime Minister.

Who was the first Vice-Chairman of NITI Aayog? Arvind Panagariya.

What is the primary difference in the approach of NITI Aayog compared to the Planning Commission? NITI Aayog follows a "Bottom-Up" approach (cooperative federalism), whereas the Planning Commission followed a "Top-Down" approach.

What is a "Direct Tax"? A tax whose burden cannot be shifted to another person (e.g., Income Tax, Corporate Tax).

What is an "Indirect Tax"? A tax whose burden can be shifted to the final consumer (e.g., GST, Customs Duty).

Which Constitutional Amendment Act introduced the Goods and Services Tax (GST) in India? The 101st Amendment Act, 2016.

On which exact date was the GST implemented nationwide? July 1, 2017.

Who serves as the Chairman of the GST Council? The Union Finance Minister.

What is "Fiscal Deficit"? The difference between the government's total expenditure and its total receipts (excluding borrowings). It represents the total borrowing required by the government.

Which constitutional body recommends the distribution of tax revenues between the Centre and the States? The Finance Commission (Article 280).

What does FRBM stand for, an act designed to bring financial discipline to the government? Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management Act.

Which curve shows the relationship between tax rates and tax revenue collected by the government? The Laffer Curve.

What is a "Progressive Tax"? A tax system where the tax rate increases as the taxable amount (income) increases.

🏔️ Uttarakhand State Economy: Tourism, Agriculture & Industry
What are the two primary pillars of the Uttarakhand state economy? Tourism and Agriculture.

Which specific sector contributes the largest share to Uttarakhand's Gross State Domestic Product (GSDP)? The Secondary Sector (Manufacturing is massive due to industrial hubs), closely followed by the Tertiary (Services) sector.

What is SIDCUL? State Infrastructure and Industrial Development Corporation of Uttarakhand Limited.

Where is the headquarters of SIDCUL located? Dehradun.

Name three major industrial hubs developed by SIDCUL in Uttarakhand. Pantnagar, Haridwar, and Sitarganj.

What is the primary mode of agriculture practiced in the hilly terrains of Uttarakhand? Terrace farming (locally called C.D. Numaar Kheti).

Which high-altitude, highly expensive medicinal fungus is legally collected and traded by local communities in Uttarakhand? Keeda Jadi (Yartsa Gunbu / Cordyceps sinensis).

Which unique variety of Rajma (kidney beans) from Pithoragarh district recently received a Geographical Indication (GI) tag? Munsiyari Rajma.

The Sirai Valley in Uttarkashi is famous for cultivating which specific agricultural product? Red Rice.

Which traditional Uttarakhand millet is heavily promoted nationwide under the state's Millet Mission? Mandua (Finger Millet).

What is "Jhangora," another staple crop of the Uttarakhand hills? Barnyard Millet.

What is the overarching objective of the "Deendayal Upadhyay Kisan Kalyan Yojana"? To provide zero-interest (or low-interest) micro-loans to small and marginal farmers up to Rs. 3 Lakhs.

Which scheme was launched by the state government to boost rural economy by promoting homestays? Deendayal Upadhyay Homestay Scheme.

Which town in Uttarkashi is famous for its massive apple orchards and is known as the "Apple Bowl of Uttarakhand"? Harsil.

Which organization was specifically set up in Almora to commercialize and promote high-quality hill tea? Uttarakhand Tea Development Board.

Where was Uttarakhand's first Mega Food Park (Patanjali Food and Herbal Park) established? Haridwar.

What is the primary objective of the "Veer Chandra Singh Garhwali Tourism Self-Employment Scheme"? To provide financial assistance and subsidies to local youth for establishing tourism-related businesses or buying commercial vehicles.

Which mega infrastructure project aims to connect Yamunotri, Gangotri, Kedarnath, and Badrinath with all-weather roads? The Char Dham Pariyojana.

Which district in Uttarakhand generally registers the highest per capita income, driven by heavy industrialization? Haridwar (and Udham Singh Nagar).

Which district generally registers the lowest per capita income in the state? Rudraprayag.

What is the massive 300 MW multipurpose dam project proposed on the Gola River near Haldwani called? The Jamrani Dam Project.

Which specific type of tourism is promoted extensively in Rishikesh, drawing significant foreign exchange? Yoga, Wellness, and Adventure Tourism (River Rafting).

Auli in Chamoli district is developed specifically to boost which sector of tourism? Winter Sports and Skiing.

Which state government department manages the prestigious "108" emergency ambulance service, highly crucial for the state's healthcare economy? Department of Health and Family Welfare (operated via PPP mode).

What is "Kaphal" (Myrica esculenta)? A highly sought-after wild berry sold extensively by locals during the summer, forming a key micro-economic activity.

The local textile industry heavily relies on wool sourced from which local tribal community known for transhumance? The Bhotia tribe (Shaukas/Marchhas).

The massive Tehri Dam is operated by which state-central joint venture corporation? THDC India Limited (Tehri Hydro Development Corporation).

Which town in Uttarakhand is famously associated with the production of high-quality cricket bats? Dehradun.

What is the local term for the dense, mixed forests of oak and rhododendron that are vital to the rural economy for fodder and fuel? Banj Oak forests.

Which district has the highest net sown area for agriculture due to its plain topography? Udham Singh Nagar.

What is the primary focus of the "Aarohi Project" in the context of Uttarakhand's development? To provide computer education and IT infrastructure in government schools.

Which town is colloquially known as the "Gateway of Kumaon," serving as a major logistics and economic transit hub? Kathgodam.

Which town serves as the major transit and economic "Gateway of Garhwal"? Kotdwar.

The "Kumaon Mandal Vikas Nigam" (KMVN) and "Garhwal Mandal Vikas Nigam" (GMVN) are primarily responsible for developing what? Tourism infrastructure and organizing treks/pilgrimages.

Which major state tax provides the highest revenue to the Uttarakhand government, alongside State GST? State Excise Duty (alcohol tax).

The proposed Rishikesh-Karnaprayag railway line aims to primarily boost which two sectors? Religious tourism and border defense logistics.

Which region of Uttarakhand is known as the "Orchard Country" due to its massive output of peaches, plums, and apricots? Ramgarh (Nainital).

Which famous fair in Chamoli acts as a major economic trade center for handlooms and local crafts? The Gauchar Mela.

Which specific scheme by the Uttarakhand government provides a maternity benefit directly to pregnant women in hilly areas to reduce infant mortality? Vande Mataram Scheme.

What is the "Mukhya Mantri Swarojgar Yojana" (MMSY)? A state scheme to provide subsidized loans and margin money to local youth and returning migrants to start small enterprises.

📉 Poverty, Unemployment & Inequality
Which type of unemployment is most commonly found in the Indian agricultural sector, where more people are working than actually needed? Disguised Unemployment.

What type of unemployment occurs due to a mismatch between the skills of the workers and the skills needed for available jobs? Structural Unemployment.

What is the temporary unemployment called when a person is transitioning between jobs or searching for a new one? Frictional Unemployment.

Which type of unemployment is directly linked to the economic business cycle (e.g., rising during a recession)? Cyclical Unemployment.

Which economic curve is used to graphically represent income inequality or wealth distribution within a nation? The Lorenz Curve.

Which statistical measure, ranging from 0 to 1, quantifies income inequality based on the Lorenz Curve? The Gini Coefficient.

A Gini Coefficient of "0" represents what state of an economy? Perfect equality (everyone has the exact same income).

Which committee, constituted in 2009, shifted the poverty line estimation from a purely calorie-based model to a broader consumption expenditure model? The Tendulkar Committee.

Which committee was set up in 2012 to review the poverty line methodology recommended by the Tendulkar Committee? The Rangarajan Committee.

What is "Absolute Poverty"? A condition where a person lacks the minimum amount of income needed to meet basic living requirements (food, shelter, clothing).

What is "Relative Poverty"? A condition where people lack the minimum amount of income needed to maintain the average standard of living in the society in which they live.

Which organization periodically conducts large-scale sample surveys to estimate employment and unemployment rates in India? The National Sample Survey Office (NSSO), now merged into the NSO.

Which index is published by the UNDP and the Oxford Poverty and Human Development Initiative to measure overlapping deprivations in health, education, and standard of living? The Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI).

In the context of the Multidimensional Poverty Index, NITI Aayog publishes the national version. What is the baseline data source for NITI Aayog's MPI? The National Family Health Survey (NFHS).

What is the "Poverty Gap"? The average shortfall of the total population from the poverty line (measuring the depth of poverty).

💱 Financial Markets: Money Market & Capital Market
Which market deals with the lending and borrowing of short-term funds (maturity of less than one year)? The Money Market.

Which market deals with the trading of medium and long-term financial securities (maturity of more than one year)? The Capital Market.

Who regulates the Money Market in India? The Reserve Bank of India (RBI).

Who regulates the Capital Market in India? The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI).

What are short-term government debt instruments issued by the RBI on behalf of the Central Government called? Treasury Bills (T-Bills).

What are the three standard maturities for Treasury Bills in India? 91 days, 182 days, and 364 days.

What is a "Commercial Paper"? An unsecured, short-term debt instrument issued by large corporations to finance their short-term liabilities.

Which is the oldest stock exchange in Asia? The Bombay Stock Exchange (BSE), established in 1875.

What is the benchmark index of the Bombay Stock Exchange (BSE), comprising 30 financially sound companies? The Sensex (Sensitivity Index).

What is the benchmark index of the National Stock Exchange (NSE), comprising 50 large companies? The Nifty 50.

What is an Initial Public Offering (IPO)? The process by which a private company offers shares to the public for the first time to raise capital.

What is the term for a market condition where securities prices are rising, and investor sentiment is highly positive? A Bull Market.

What is the term for a market condition where securities prices are falling, and investor sentiment is negative? A Bear Market.

What are "Blue Chip" companies? Nationally recognized, well-established, and financially sound companies with a history of reliable performance.

What is "Foreign Direct Investment" (FDI)? An investment made by a firm or individual in one country into business interests located in another country, usually involving a lasting interest and control.

What is "Foreign Portfolio Investment" (FPI)? Investment by foreign entities in Indian financial assets (like stocks and bonds) without acquiring a controlling interest.

🌍 International Organizations, Trade & Balance of Payments
Where is the headquarters of the International Monetary Fund (IMF) located? Washington, D.C.

Where is the headquarters of the World Bank located? Washington, D.C.

Which two institutions are collectively known as the "Bretton Woods Twins"? The IMF and the World Bank.

What is the primary international reserve asset created by the IMF to supplement the official reserves of its member countries? Special Drawing Rights (SDRs).

Which five major currencies make up the SDR basket? US Dollar, Euro, Chinese Renminbi, Japanese Yen, and British Pound Sterling.

Which international organization replaced the General Agreement on Tariffs and Trade (GATT) in 1995? The World Trade Organization (WTO).

Where is the headquarters of the WTO located? Geneva, Switzerland.

What is the "Balance of Payments" (BoP)? A systematic record of all economic transactions between the residents of a reporting country and the rest of the world during a specific period.

The BoP consists of two main accounts. What are they? The Current Account and the Capital Account.

Trade in goods (exports and imports) and services is recorded in which BoP account? The Current Account.

FDI, FPI, and external commercial borrowings are recorded in which BoP account? The Capital Account.

What is a "Current Account Deficit" (CAD)? A situation where the value of a country's imports of goods and services exceeds the value of its exports.

What is the New Development Bank (NDB), and which bloc of countries established it? A multilateral development bank established by the BRICS states (Brazil, Russia, India, China, and South Africa).

Where is the headquarters of the New Development Bank (NDB)? Shanghai, China.

Where is the headquarters of the Asian Development Bank (ADB)? Manila, Philippines.

What does the term "Dumping" mean in international trade? When a country or company exports a product at a price that is lower in the foreign importing market than the price in the exporter's domestic market.

What is the primary objective of imposing "Anti-Dumping Duties"? To protect domestic industries from unfair competition caused by dumped imports.

What is "Currency Depreciation"? A decrease in the value of a country's currency relative to other currencies under a floating exchange rate system.

What is "Currency Devaluation"? A deliberate downward adjustment of the value of a country's currency relative to another currency under a fixed exchange rate system.

🏔️ Uttarakhand State Economy: Migration, Agriculture & Infrastructure
Which major socio-economic issue in the hilly districts of Uttarakhand is colloquially referred to as "Palayan"? Out-migration (rural-to-urban or hills-to-plains) due to lack of employment and basic amenities.

Which official commission was established by the Uttarakhand government in 2017 to study and mitigate this out-migration? The Uttarakhand Rural Development and Migration Commission (Palayan Aayog).

According to the Palayan Aayog, which district in Uttarakhand has witnessed the highest rate of out-migration? Pauri Garhwal.

What are "Ghost Villages" (Bhootaha Gaon) in the context of Uttarakhand's economy? Completely abandoned villages in the hills due to heavy out-migration.

Which state board was specifically created to promote organic farming and facilitate the certification of organic products in Uttarakhand? The Uttarakhand Organic Commodity Board (UOCB).

Uttarakhand was the first state in India to pass a dedicated law to promote what type of sustainable farming? Organic Agriculture (Organic Agriculture Act, 2019).

Which agricultural initiative aims to double farmers' income in the state by consolidating fragmented landholdings? Chakbandi (Land Consolidation).

The "Mukhyamantri Dal Poshit Yojana" was launched in Uttarakhand to achieve what dual economic goal? Providing subsidized pulses to ration cardholders and encouraging local farmers to grow more pulses.

Which state agency handles the procurement and distribution of food grains under the Public Distribution System (PDS) in Uttarakhand? The State Food and Civil Supplies Department.

Which central scheme has been economically vital in providing all-weather road connectivity to unconnected habitations in the Uttarakhand hills? Pradhan Mantri Gram Sadak Yojana (PMGSY).

What is the "Home Stay Scheme" (Deendayal Upadhyay Griha Awas Yojana) primarily aimed at? Retaining the rural population by providing them a steady income through rural tourism and reversing migration.

Under the MSME Policy of Uttarakhand, industries are categorized into different regions (A, B, C, D) based on what criteria? Their geographical location (plains vs. remote hilly areas), offering higher subsidies for industries set up in remote areas.

The "Solar Farming Scheme" (Mukhyamantri Saur Swarojgar Yojana) provides loans to youth and farmers for setting up solar plants up to what capacity? 25 Kilowatts (kW).

Which state corporation is responsible for generating hydroelectric power and managing state-owned dams in Uttarakhand? UJVN Limited (Uttarakhand Jal Vidyut Nigam).

The Maneri Bhali Hydroelectric Project, a major revenue generator for the state, is built on which river? The Bhagirathi River (Uttarkashi).

The Chibro Power Station, India's first underground powerhouse, is located on which river in Dehradun? The Tons River.

Which sector in Uttarakhand provides the highest potential for creating "Green Jobs"? Renewable Energy (Hydropower and Solar) and Ecotourism.

What is the overarching goal of the "Uttarakhand Wellness Summit" initiatives? To position the state as the global capital for Ayurveda, Yoga, and Wellness tourism, attracting foreign direct investment (FDI).

Which specific GI-tagged craft of Uttarakhand involves intricate copperware, primarily driving the local economy of Almora? Almora Copper Craft (Tamra Shilp).

Which GI-tagged handloom product, made from the bark of the Himalayan nettle, is a growing export commodity for Uttarakhand? Bichhu Buti (Nettle) fabrics/shawls.

Which organization in Uttarakhand is responsible for developing industrial estates and IT parks? SIDCUL (State Infrastructure and Industrial Development Corporation of Uttarakhand Ltd).

Where is the major IT Park of Uttarakhand located, serving as a hub for BPO and tech companies? Sahastradhara Road, Dehradun.

The proposed "Delhi-Dehradun Expressway" is expected to cut travel time between the two cities to approximately how many hours, massively boosting the state's economy? 2.5 hours.

Which state tax was subsumed into the GST, which previously formed a major chunk of the state's revenue from the manufacturing sector? Value Added Tax (VAT) and Central Sales Tax (CST).

Which central grant forms a crucial part of the Uttarakhand State Budget, meant to compensate for the disabilities of being a hilly state? Grants-in-aid under Article 275 (on the recommendation of the Finance Commission).

📊 Advanced Macro & Inflation Terminology
What is "Core Inflation"? A measure of inflation that excludes highly volatile items like food and energy prices to show the underlying long-term trend.

What is "Headline Inflation"? The raw inflation figure reported through the Consumer Price Index (CPI), which includes all items, including volatile food and energy.

What is "Deflation"? A general decline in the prices of goods and services, often associated with a contraction in the supply of money and credit.

What is "Disinflation"? A temporary slowing of the pace of price inflation (prices are still rising, but at a slower rate).

What does the acronym "FEMA" stand for, the act that regulates foreign exchange transactions in India? Foreign Exchange Management Act (1999).

FEMA replaced which older, more stringent regulatory act? FERA (Foreign Exchange Regulation Act).

What is a "Mixed Economy"? An economic system combining private and public enterprise, where market forces exist alongside government intervention.

What is "Capitalism"? An economic system characterized by private or corporate ownership of capital goods, and by investments that are determined by private decision.

What is "Socialism" in an economic context? An economic system where the means of production, distribution, and exchange are owned or regulated by the community as a whole (usually through the state).

What is a "Closed Economy"? An economy that does not interact with other economies in the world (no imports, no exports).

What is "Monetary Policy"? The macroeconomic policy laid down by the central bank (RBI) involving the management of money supply and interest rates.

What is "Fiscal Policy"? The means by which a government adjusts its spending levels and tax rates to monitor and influence a nation's economy.

What is an "Accommodative" monetary policy stance? A stance where the central bank is willing to lower interest rates to inject money into the economy and boost growth.

What is a "Hawkish" monetary policy stance? A stance focused primarily on keeping inflation low, typically by raising interest rates.

What are "Non-Performing Assets" (NPAs) in banking? Loans or advances for which the principal or interest payment has remained overdue for a period of 90 days.

What is a "Bad Bank"? An asset management company set up to buy the bad loans (NPAs) of commercial banks to help clear their balance sheets.

In India, what is the name of the National Asset Reconstruction Company Limited (NARCL) commonly referred to as? The Bad Bank.

What is the "Marginal Standing Facility" (MSF)? A window for scheduled banks to borrow overnight from the RBI in an emergency situation when inter-bank liquidity dries up completely.

What does "Open Market Operations" (OMO) mean? The buying and selling of government securities by the RBI in the open market to regulate money supply.

If the RBI buys government securities under OMO, does it inject liquidity or absorb liquidity from the market? It injects liquidity (puts money into the market).

What is "Crowding Out" in economics? A situation where increased government borrowing and spending leads to a decrease in private sector investment.

What is "Quantitative Easing" (QE)? An unconventional monetary policy where a central bank purchases large-scale government bonds to increase the money supply and encourage lending and investment.

What is a "Progressive Tax"? A tax structure where the tax rate increases as the taxable base amount increases (e.g., Income Tax).

What is a "Regressive Tax"? A tax structure that takes a larger percentage of income from low-income earners than from high-income earners (e.g., indirect taxes like sales tax on basic goods).

What is a "Proportional Tax" (Flat Tax)? A tax structure where the tax rate is fixed, regardless of the income level.

Here is Part 3 (Questions 201–300) of your Economy master-list. In this batch, we dive into the mechanics of the Union Budget, the Indian Taxation System, the Eight Core Industries, and vital Economic Indicators of Uttarakhand. Let's keep building that economic fortress!

📊 Public Finance & The Union Budget
Which Article of the Indian Constitution mandates the presentation of the "Annual Financial Statement" (commonly known as the Union Budget)? Article 112.

The Union Budget is broadly classified into which two main accounts? The Revenue Account and the Capital Account.

What is included in the "Revenue Receipts" of the government? Tax revenues (Direct and Indirect taxes) and Non-tax revenues (interest, dividends, fees) that neither create a liability nor reduce an asset.

What is included in "Capital Receipts"? Government borrowings, recovery of loans, and proceeds from disinvestment (these either create a liability or reduce an asset).

What is a "Revenue Deficit"? The excess of total revenue expenditure over total revenue receipts.

What is a "Fiscal Deficit"? The excess of total expenditure over total receipts excluding borrowings. (It essentially indicates the total borrowing requirement of the government).

What is a "Primary Deficit"? Fiscal Deficit minus interest payments on previous borrowings.

What does the Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management (FRBM) Act of 2003 primarily aim to achieve? To institutionalize financial discipline, reduce fiscal deficit, and eliminate revenue deficit.

Which fund contains all the revenues received by the government, loans raised, and receipts from recoveries of loans? The Consolidated Fund of India (Article 266).

Which fund is placed at the disposal of the President to meet unforeseen expenditures pending parliamentary approval? The Contingency Fund of India (Article 267).

Provident funds, small savings, and postal deposits belong to which government account? The Public Account of India (Article 266(2)).

What is "Zero-Based Budgeting" (ZBB)? A method of budgeting in which all expenses must be justified for each new period, starting from a "zero base," rather than basing it on the previous year's budget.

What is "Outcome Budgeting"? A budgeting practice that measures the development outcomes of all government programs rather than just the financial outlays.

Which department prepares the Economic Survey of India? The Department of Economic Affairs (under the Ministry of Finance).

Who presents the Economic Survey in Parliament, typically a day before the Union Budget? The Chief Economic Advisor (CEA).

💰 The Indian Taxation System & GST
Is Income Tax a direct or indirect tax? Direct Tax.

Is Corporate Tax a direct or indirect tax? Direct Tax.

What is "Minimum Alternate Tax" (MAT)? A direct tax imposed on "zero-tax companies" that show high book profits and pay dividends but pay little to no corporate tax due to various exemptions.

What is the difference between a "Cess" and a "Surcharge"? A Cess is a tax levied for a specific purpose (e.g., Education Cess), while a Surcharge is an additional tax on the existing tax liability used for general revenue.

Which major indirect tax reform was introduced on July 1, 2017, to create a unified national market? The Goods and Services Tax (GST).

Under the GST structure, what does CGST stand for? Central Goods and Services Tax (collected by the Centre on intra-state sales).

What does SGST stand for? State Goods and Services Tax (collected by the State on intra-state sales).

What does IGST stand for? Integrated Goods and Services Tax (collected by the Centre on inter-state sales, then apportioned to the destination state).

Which principle determines the allocation of GST revenue—is it an origin-based tax or a destination-based tax? It is a destination-based consumption tax.

Which Constitutional Article established the GST Council? Article 279A.

Who acts as the Chairperson of the GST Council? The Union Finance Minister.

What is the standard voting weightage of the Central Government in the GST Council? One-third (1/3rd) of the total votes cast.

What is the collective voting weightage of all the State Governments in the GST Council? Two-thirds (2/3rds) of the total votes cast.

Every decision of the GST Council requires what minimum majority to pass? Three-fourths (75%) majority of the weighted votes of members present and voting.

Name three major items currently kept outside the purview of GST. Crude oil, motor spirit (petrol), high-speed diesel, natural gas, aviation turbine fuel (ATF), and alcoholic liquor for human consumption.

What is "Customs Duty"? A tax imposed on goods when they are transported across international borders (imports and exports).

What is "Excise Duty"? A tax levied on the production, licensing, and sale of goods manufactured within the country.

What is a "Pigovian Tax"? A tax placed on any good which creates negative externalities (e.g., carbon tax on pollution or "sin taxes" on tobacco).

What does "Tax Evasion" mean? The illegal non-payment or underpayment of tax.

What is "Tax Avoidance"? The legal usage of the tax regime to one's own advantage to reduce the amount of tax payable (often exploiting loopholes).

🏭 Industry, Core Sectors & MSMEs
The Index of Industrial Production (IIP) measures the short-term changes in the volume of production of industrial products. Which office releases it? The National Statistical Office (NSO).

What is the base year currently used for calculating the IIP? 2011-12.

The Eight Core Industries comprise what percentage of the weight of items included in the IIP? 40.27%.

Name the Eight Core Industries of India. Coal, Crude Oil, Natural Gas, Refinery Products, Fertilizers, Steel, Cement, and Electricity.

Which of the Eight Core Industries holds the highest weightage in the index? Refinery Products.

Which of the Eight Core Industries holds the lowest weightage in the index? Fertilizers.

Under the revised MSME classification (2020), what defines a "Micro" enterprise? Investment up to Rs. 1 Crore and Turnover up to Rs. 5 Crore.

What defines a "Small" enterprise? Investment up to Rs. 10 Crore and Turnover up to Rs. 50 Crore.

What defines a "Medium" enterprise? Investment up to Rs. 50 Crore and Turnover up to Rs. 250 Crore.

What does the acronym PLI stand for in the context of India's manufacturing boost? Production-Linked Incentive scheme.

Which central public sector enterprises are granted "Maharatna" status? Mega-corporations with immense financial autonomy, having an average annual turnover of more than Rs. 25,000 crore over the last three years (e.g., BHEL, ONGC, SAIL).

What is the tier immediately below Maharatna for public sector enterprises? Navratna.

What is "Disinvestment"? The action of a government selling or liquidating its assets or stakes in public sector enterprises.

Which specific government department is responsible for managing the Centre's disinvestment program? Department of Investment and Public Asset Management (DIPAM).

What is "Foreign Direct Investment" (FDI) Cap? The maximum limit (percentage) up to which a foreign investor can invest in an Indian company in a specific sector.

🏔️ Uttarakhand Economy: Finances, Demographics & Special Status
Uttarakhand historically holds the status of a "Special Category State." What is the primary financial benefit of this status regarding Centrally Sponsored Schemes? The Centre-State funding ratio is highly favorable, usually 90:10 (Centre pays 90%, State pays 10%).

In normal states (non-Special Category), what is the typical funding ratio for Centrally Sponsored Schemes? 60:40 or 50:50.

Which Constitutional Article provides for the establishment of a State Finance Commission? Article 243-I.

What is the main function of the State Finance Commission in Uttarakhand? To review the financial position of the Panchayats and Municipalities and recommend the devolution of state taxes to them.

Which non-tax revenue source is a major contributor to the Uttarakhand exchequer due to its geography? Royalties from mining and water usage charges from Hydroelectric power generation.

The "Tehri Hydro Development Corporation" (THDC) is a joint venture between the Government of India and which state government? The Government of Uttar Pradesh (though located in Uttarakhand, it was originally a joint venture with UP before statehood, now involving the Centre, UP, and Uttarakhand).

Which agricultural product from Uttarakhand, known for its distinct aroma, is a highly exported GI-tagged commodity? Dehradun Basmati Rice.

"Aipan," a traditional ritualistic art form heavily commercialized for state tourism and women's empowerment, recently received a GI tag. It belongs to which region? Kumaon.

Which GI-tagged product of Uttarakhand is a traditional handcrafted woolen blanket heavily traded by the Bhotia community? Thulma.

"Ringal" craft, which also received a GI tag, involves weaving items out of what material? Dwarf Bamboo.

Which border-focused central program injects crucial funds into Uttarakhand to develop infrastructure in remote districts like Pithoragarh, Chamoli, and Uttarkashi? The Border Area Development Programme (BADP).

According to the 2011 Census, what percentage of Uttarakhand's population lives in rural areas, making rural economy central to state planning? Approximately 69.77% (roughly 70%).

Which economic phenomenon occurs when men migrate from the Uttarakhand hills to the plains for work, leaving agriculture entirely to women? "Feminization of Agriculture."

The Uttarakhand government launched the "Mukhyamantri Vatsalya Yojana" primarily to provide economic support to whom? Children who lost their parents during the COVID-19 pandemic.

Which massive industrial estate in Udham Singh Nagar serves as the primary automotive and manufacturing backbone of the state? Pantnagar Industrial Estate (SIDCUL).

What is the "Right of Way" (RoW) policy in Uttarakhand's economic context? A policy designed to ease the laying of optical fiber cables and telecom infrastructure to boost the IT and ITES sector in the hills.

The state government frequently organizes the "Destination Uttarakhand" summit for what primary purpose? To attract domestic and Foreign Direct Investment (FDI) into the state.

Which sector has been officially declared an "Industry" by the Uttarakhand government to facilitate easier bank loans and subsidies? Tourism.

Under the state's industrial policy, what are "Category A" areas? Highly remote and mountainous districts where the government provides the maximum financial subsidies to set up industries to curb migration.

Which scheme was launched to provide pensions to elderly farmers in Uttarakhand? Kisan Pension Yojana.

📈 Economic Curves, Theories & Terms
Which economic curve demonstrates the inverse relationship between the rate of unemployment and the rate of inflation? The Phillips Curve.

Which curve shows that as a country develops economically, income inequality first increases, then peaks, and subsequently decreases? The Kuznets Curve.

Which curve shows that environmental degradation increases with economic growth up to a certain point, after which environmental quality improves? The Environmental Kuznets Curve.

What is the "Multiplier Effect"? The proportional amount of increase in final income that results from an injection of spending (e.g., government infrastructure spending).

What is an "Opportunity Cost"? The potential benefit or value that is lost when you choose one alternative over another.

What is the "Giffen Good" paradox? An inferior good for which demand increases as its price increases, violating the standard law of demand (because the income effect outweighs the substitution effect).

What is a "Veblen Good"? A luxury good for which demand increases as the price increases, because its higher price makes it a status symbol.

What does "Ceteris Paribus" mean in economics? "All other things being equal" (used to isolate the effect of one economic variable on another).

What is "Fiat Money"? Currency established as money, often by government regulation, but that does not have intrinsic value (like a gold coin does) and is not backed by a physical commodity.

What is "Legal Tender"? Coins or banknotes that must be accepted if offered in payment of a debt, backed by law.

What is "Broad Money" (M3) in the Indian banking system? The total volume of money circulating in the economy (currency with the public + demand deposits + time deposits with banks).

What is "Narrow Money" (M1)? A highly liquid measure of money supply (currency with the public + demand deposits with the banking system).

What does the term "Hot Money" refer to? Capital that flows frequently and quickly between financial markets worldwide to earn the highest short-term interest rates or capital gains.

What is an "Angel Investor"? A high-net-worth individual who provides financial backing for small startups or entrepreneurs, typically in exchange for ownership equity.

What is "Venture Capital"? Financing that investors provide to startup companies and small businesses that are believed to have long-term growth potential.

What is a "Unicorn" in the business and economic world? A privately held startup company valued at over $1 billion.

What is the "Twin Balance Sheet Problem" previously faced by the Indian economy? The simultaneous financial distress of highly indebted corporate companies and heavily burdened public sector banks (with high NPAs).

What is "Furlough" in an economic/employment context? A temporary leave of absence from work, usually without pay, due to special needs of a company or economy (often seen during economic downturns).

What is a "Trade Deficit"? When a country imports more goods than it exports.

What is "Protectionism"? The economic policy of restricting imports from other countries through methods such as tariffs on imported goods, import quotas, and a variety of other government regulations.

What does "Autarky" mean? The quality of being self-sufficient; usually applied to political states or their economic systems that do not engage in international trade.

What is a "Tariff"? A tax imposed by a government on imported goods and services.

What is a "Quota" in international trade? A government-imposed trade restriction that limits the number or monetary value of goods that can be imported or exported during a particular time period.

What is the "Base Rate"? The minimum interest rate set by the RBI below which banks are not permitted to lend to their customers.

What is the "MCLR" (Marginal Cost of Funds based Lending Rate)? An internal benchmark rate used by banks to set the minimum interest rate for loans, taking into account the marginal cost of funds.

What is "Green Accounting"? A type of accounting that attempts to factor environmental costs (like resource depletion and pollution) into the financial results of operations.

What is "Carbon Trading"? A market-based system aimed at reducing greenhouse gases by allowing countries or companies to buy and sell permits to emit carbon dioxide.

What does "Amortization" mean? The process of spreading out a loan into a series of fixed payments over time (paying off debt).

What is an "Oligopoly"? A market structure in which a small number of firms has the large majority of market share (e.g., telecom sector in India).

What is a "Monopoly"? A market structure characterized by a single seller selling a unique product in the market with no close substitutes.
