🌲 Uttarakhand Environment & Conservation
Which National Park in Uttarakhand is famous as India's first National Park, established in 1936? Jim Corbett National Park.

Corbett National Park primarily protects which iconic endangered animal? The Royal Bengal Tiger.

Which globally recognized Biosphere Reserve in Uttarakhand is famous for its high-altitude alpine flora and the elusive Snow Leopard? Nanda Devi Biosphere Reserve.

Which National Park in Chamoli district is a UNESCO World Heritage site known for its endemic alpine flowers and outstanding natural beauty? Valley of Flowers National Park.

Rajaji National Park, spread across three districts of Uttarakhand, is highly renowned for a massive population of which animal? Asian Elephants.

Which high-altitude National Park in Uttarkashi contains the Gaumukh glacier, the origin of the river Ganga? Gangotri National Park.

Govind Pashu Vihar National Park is famous for the conservation of which magnificent, large bearded vulture? The Lammergeier.

Which wetland became Uttarakhand's very first designated Ramsar Site in 2020? Asan Conservation Reserve (Dehradun).

Who was the globally renowned environmentalist from Uttarakhand who led the "Chipko Movement" to prevent mass deforestation? Sunderlal Bahuguna.

Who was the grassroots pioneer woman of the Chipko Movement who rallied the women of Reni village in 1974 to hug the trees? Gaura Devi.

Which massive grassroots movement in Karnataka in 1983 was directly inspired by Uttarakhand's Chipko Movement? The Appiko Movement (led by Pandurang Hegde).

What is the state bird of Uttarakhand, known for its brilliantly colored plumage? The Himalayan Monal.

What is the state flower of Uttarakhand, which strictly blooms at very high altitudes and is revered in local mythology? Brahma Kamal (Saussurea obvallata).

What is the state tree of Uttarakhand, heavily relied upon by locals for fuel and fodder? Buransh (Rhododendron).

🚀 Defense, Space & Emerging Technology
In which year was the Defence Research and Development Organisation (DRDO) established in India? 1958.

Who is universally recognized as the "Missile Man of India" and the chief architect of the Integrated Guided Missile Development Programme (IGMDP)? Dr. A.P.J. Abdul Kalam.

Which is India's most advanced, nuclear-capable Intercontinental Ballistic Missile (ICBM) with a range of over 5,000 km? Agni-V.

The BrahMos missile, one of the fastest supersonic cruise missiles in the world, is a joint venture between India and which country? Russia.

What is "Tejas"? India's indigenous Light Combat Aircraft (LCA).

What is the name of India's first indigenous nuclear-powered ballistic missile submarine? INS Arihant.

What is the name of India's first indigenous aircraft carrier (IAC-1), commissioned in 2022? INS Vikrant.

Project 75 of the Indian Navy is dedicated to building which specific class of diesel-electric attack submarines? Scorpene-class submarines (e.g., INS Kalvari, INS Khanderi).

Which highly advanced long-range surface-to-air missile defense system did India procure from Russia? S-400 Triumf.

What is the name of the indigenous multi-barrel rocket launcher system developed by DRDO? Pinaka.

What is the fundamental unit of information in Quantum Computing, which can exist as a 0, a 1, or both simultaneously? Qubit (Quantum bit).

Which secure, decentralized digital ledger technology forms the absolute foundation of all cryptocurrencies? Blockchain.

What does the emerging technology "Li-Fi" stand for? Light Fidelity (using visible light from LED bulbs for high-speed wireless data transmission).

In computing, what does "IoT" stand for? Internet of Things (the network of physical objects embedded with sensors and software to exchange data).

What is the process of generating a three-dimensional solid object from a digital file by laying down successive layers of material called? 3D Printing (Additive Manufacturing).

Which upcoming joint Earth-observing mission by NASA and ISRO aims to map the entire globe in 12 days using synthetic aperture radar? NISAR.

🧬 Biology: Physiology, Cells & Taxonomy
What is the largest organ of the human body? The Skin.

What is the normal, resting blood pressure for a healthy human adult? 120/80 mmHg.

A normal human somatic (body) cell contains exactly how many individual chromosomes? 46 (or 23 pairs).

Which specific pair of chromosomes determines the biological sex of a male? XY.

Which specific pair of chromosomes determines the biological sex of a female? XX.

Which cell organelle is responsible for packaging and modifying proteins for transport? The Golgi Apparatus (Golgi Body).

What is the primary structural component of a plant cell wall? Cellulose.

plant cell structure diagram, AI generated
Shutterstock
What is the primary structural component of a fungal cell wall? Chitin.

Which protein gives hair, nails, and the outer layer of skin their tough, structural integrity? Keratin.

Which medical condition is caused by a chronic deficiency of insulin or the body's inability to use it effectively? Diabetes Mellitus.

Which specific group of cells in the Pancreas secretes insulin? The Beta cells (within the Islets of Langerhans).

What is the scientific term for the study of birds? Ornithology.

What is the scientific term for the study of insects? Entomology.

Which gas is artificially used to accelerate the ripening of fruits for commercial sale? Ethylene gas.

Which vitamins are classified as completely "Water-Soluble"? Vitamin B-complex and Vitamin C.

Which vitamins are classified as "Fat-Soluble" (meaning they can be stored in the body's liver and fatty tissues)? Vitamins A, D, E, and K.

What is the biological phenomenon where living organisms emit light (like fireflies)? Bioluminescence.

🧪 Advanced Physics & Chemistry
What spectacular natural phenomenon in the high-latitude skies is caused by the interaction of the solar wind with Earth's magnetic field? The Aurora Borealis (Northern Lights) and Aurora Australis (Southern Lights).

What is the SI unit used to measure magnetic flux? Weber.

Of the four fundamental forces of nature (Gravity, Electromagnetism, Strong Nuclear, Weak Nuclear), which is the absolute weakest? The Gravitational Force.

Which gas law states that the volume of a given mass of gas is inversely proportional to its pressure at a constant temperature? Boyle's Law.

Which gas law states that the volume of a gas is directly proportional to its absolute temperature at a constant pressure? Charles's Law.

What is the most abundant element found in the Earth's crust by weight? Oxygen (roughly 46%).

What is the most abundant metal found in the Earth's crust? Aluminum.

Which element is historically known by the ancient name "Quicksilver"? Mercury.

Bauxite is the primary commercial ore used to extract which metal? Aluminum.

Hematite and Magnetite are the primary ores of which essential metal? Iron.

Which strong acid is commonly used as the electrolyte in traditional car batteries? Sulfuric Acid (H 
2
​
 SO 
4
​
 ).

Which compound, made from heating Gypsum, is widely used by doctors to set fractured bones? Plaster of Paris (Calcium Sulfate Hemihydrate).

Which physical process involves a gas turning directly into a solid, skipping the liquid phase entirely (e.g., frost forming on a window)? Deposition.

🌐 Environmental Institutions & Treaties
Which Indian statutory body is entrusted with the execution of the Water Act (1974) and the Air Act (1981)? Central Pollution Control Board (CPCB).

The "Agenda 21" action plan, focusing on sustainable development, was the major outcome of which historic 1992 UN conference? The Earth Summit (held in Rio de Janeiro).

Which global climate summit in 2023 (COP 28) reached a historic agreement to transition away from fossil fuels? The UN Climate Change Conference in Dubai, UAE.

What does the acronym BOD stand for in water pollution measurement? Biochemical Oxygen Demand.

What does COD stand for, a faster and more comprehensive test for water pollution than BOD? Chemical Oxygen Demand.

What is the environmental technique called where plants are specifically used to absorb, accumulate, or break down pollutants in the soil? Phytoremediation.

What is the technique called where fungi are used to decontaminate polluted environments? Mycoremediation.

Which mass global event, organized by the WWF, encourages individuals to turn off non-essential electric lights for one hour to show commitment to the planet? Earth Hour.

Under India's Bio-Medical Waste Management Rules, highly infectious anatomical waste (human tissues, organs) must be disposed of in bins of which color? Yellow.

What is the primary greenhouse gas emitted during the natural decay of organic waste in municipal solid waste landfills? Methane.

🔥 Final Rapid-Fire Review
Who is universally recognized as the "Father of the Indian Space Program"? Dr. Vikram Sarabhai.

What is the absolute zero temperature, the theoretical point where all atomic motion stops, in Celsius? -273.15 °C (or 0 Kelvin).

Which is the only metal that is an exceptionally good conductor of electricity, outperforming even copper? Silver.

What is the SI unit of Luminous Intensity? Candela.

What is the SI unit used to measure the "Amount of Substance" in chemistry? Mole.

Which instrument determines the specific gravity (relative density) of liquids? Hydrometer.

Which instrument is used on ships to measure the depth of the ocean? Fathometer.

What is the overarching study of the origin, evolution, and large-scale structures of the universe? Cosmology.

What is the primary, overarching source of energy that sustains almost all ecosystems on Planet Earth? The Sun.

What is the generic name for the group of chemicals (mostly CFCs and HCFCs) traditionally used in refrigerators and air conditioners that severely damage the ozone layer? Freons.

What is the exact chemical name for "Chalk" or "Marble"? Calcium Carbonate (CaCO 
3
​
 ).

Which extremely hard, synthetic carbon allotrope is structured like a hollow soccer ball and is used in nanotechnology? Fullerene (Buckyball).

In computing, 1 Byte is equal to exactly how many bits? 8 bits.

What does the acronym HTTP stand for in web browsing? HyperText Transfer Protocol.

What is the phenomenon where a virus jumps from an animal species to humans, sparking a new disease outbreak (like COVID-19)? Zoonotic Spillover.

Which human organ is directly affected by the Hepatitis virus? The Liver.

The "Elisa" test is to HIV as the "Widal" test is to what? Typhoid.

Which fundamental property of matter causes it to resist any change in its state of rest or uniform motion? Inertia.

What kind of mirror is specifically used in solar cookers to focus sunlight efficiently? Concave Mirror.

What is the phenomenon where the frequency of a sound wave seems to change based on the movement of the source relative to the observer (like a passing ambulance siren)? The Doppler Effect.

What is the name of the boundary that separates the Troposphere from the Stratosphere? The Tropopause.

Which class of animals are "Amphibians" known for? Animals that can live both on land and in water, and typically undergo metamorphosis (e.g., frogs).

What is the biological process by which a caterpillar completely transforms into a butterfly? Metamorphosis.

Which is the largest and most complex part of the human brain, responsible for memory, speech, and conscious thought? The Cerebrum.

Which vital trace mineral is absolutely essential for the formation of hemoglobin in the blood? Iron.

Which vital gas do humans and animals exhale as a waste product of cellular respiration? Carbon Dioxide.

What does "Bhangar" refer to in the context of Indian soil geography? Older, less fertile alluvial soil found higher up in river valleys.

What does "Khadar" refer to? Newer, highly fertile alluvial soil deposited in the floodplains.

What is the common name for the condition "Hypertension"? High Blood Pressure.

Ultimately, what is the fundamental unit of all known life on Earth? The Cell.
